# Pando — project context

Read this first. It is the single place that says what this project is, what state
it is in, and what has already been decided. Keep it current (see the last section).

## What we're building

Pando is an **SMS-first local parenting trust network**: a parent texts a US number
saved as a contact and gets recommendations about local classes, camps, activities
and caregivers, backed by real parents and honestly labelled by source and
freshness. *"AI knows things. Pando knows someone."*

The long-term asset is not the interface — it's the structured **trust + freshness
graph** and the **two-layer matching** on top of it (social affinity × life
relevance). That is what the paid tier sells.

- **Phase 1 — Seed Tool** (what we are building): a mobile-first web app for ~350
  curated founding contributors. Collects the matching profile, activity/place/tip
  recommendations and caregiver nominations. It is *not* a survey — it is the first
  version of the human-truth ingestion layer.
- **Phase 2 — SMS pilot**: the real product. Client folded the old "Phase 3"
  (Twilio + Stripe) into Phase 2 — the channel *is* the product, so it ships with it.

Client: Janet (non-technical, owns product decisions). Agency: QuitCode.

## Working model (why the code looks like this)

**Claude Code writes the app · the developer specifies pages · the app owns the
backend.** So:

- Everything the browser touches is our Next.js app: pages, route handlers,
  validation, sanitising, secrets.
- Business logic is TypeScript in `lib/server/repo/*`, running in the same
  process. `lib/server/db.ts` is the **only** file that knows a connection
  string.
- Unset `DATABASE_URL` ⇒ the route answers `persisted: false` rather than
  pretending. That honesty rule survived the move off n8n and is what makes the
  flow walkable before there is a database.
- Postgres is Supabase, reached over the **pooler** (IPv4). The direct host is
  IPv6-only without the paid add-on and an IPv4 VPS cannot use it — the same
  constraint that once forced an HTTP transport for n8n.
- The n8n instance still runs on the box and still hosts other work; nothing in
  this app talks to it.

                     consent scope · 0013 the 5/10 allowance; then 0014, which turns

Never edited in
                     place: drizzle hashes them, and an edit desynchronises every

newer than the spec; where they conflict, it wins).

Caregiver follows C1–C11: firsthand-employment hard gate, kind of care, 18+, recency, ages cared for, closed strengths, fit, know-first vs private note, hire-again (yes/hesitant/no → hold + restricted why), needs horizon + type + check-back permission, pay band + separate benchmark consent, reference, and a parent-sent invite. **Stage 1** (Product Strategy, 11 Aug) adds the three captures that only mean something together with the rate — schedule pattern, hours per week, benefits — all skippable and all inside the same `pay_benchmark_consent` decision, because an hourly band alone cannot tell a guaranteed-hours 40-hour role from ten hours of date nights. **No caregiver contact details are collected at all.** Remaining: nothing on the app side — `lib/server/repo/cards.ts` shapes a card into rows, with the nomination and its restricted notes in one transaction |
| 1.7 | Completion screen | ✅ built — **three screens, not one** (client's call, 6 Aug): `/done` tells (thank-you, "Founding contributor · in review" activating on the 2nd approved contribution, what they shared), `/done/ask` asks (D1 demand question with sensitivity routing, versioned follow-up consent, OTP gate), `/done/next` explains (received → being read → added, D2 referral, return link). `components/seed/done/*` |
| 1.8 / 1.9 | Extraction engine · annotation & flags | ✅ built — **and note what 1.8 is not.** The estimate's word "extraction" assumed a parent would type a paragraph; the capture asks closed questions instead, so ages, price band and unit, recency, how much and whether they recommend it all arrive as *taps*. There is nothing left to pull out of prose, so the pass **classifies and flags** the few sentences a parent chose to write, and never rewrites them (invariant 8). `lib/server/extract.ts` scores that text 0–1 with `claude-haiku-4-5` and structured output (one bounded classification per card at 1/5 the Opus price; **Haiku 4.5 rejects `output_config.effort` and has no adaptive thinking**, so neither can be added to that request without changing the model back); `lib/server/repo/flags.ts` applies the rules (`possible_named_person`, `low_confidence` at the same 0.6 the admin filter uses, `stale_at_capture`) with one open flag per reason+subject. **Every score is stored with the sentence explaining it** (`confidence_note`, 13 Aug) and shown next to the number — before that, the reason existed only as a side effect of raising a flag, so a card at 0.85 carried a number nobody could interrogate. Runs after the save response, never blocking the parent, with `POST /api/admin/extract` as the catch-up sweep. Caregiver cards are excluded — their free text *is* the restricted note. An **admin edit clears the score** rather than keeping one that described a sentence which no longer exists; the sweep picks it up again. Unset `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` ⇒ no score rather than a fake one |
| 1.10 | Session / passwordless | 🟡 device-local autosave + **phone OTP at the end of the profile** (13 Aug — it spent a day on the entry screen and was wrong there), on spec §19's numbers: 6 digits, 5 min, 3 sends, 3 wrong guesses, then a **15-minute lock on the number** — keyed to the phone rather than the pending verification, so dropping the cookie is not a way around it. A **confirmed** number then stays confirmed for 12 hours (`VERIFICATION_SESSION_HOURS`) — it has to outlive a whole visit now that it opens one, and running past it costs nothing but a fresh code at the end. Structure done and gated server-side; sends blocked on A2P campaign approval + Messaging Service SID, and while they are, the flow falls back to the old deferred shape |

Rate limiting was the one thing in the row genuinely missing: admin sign-in and the OTP were throttled per *identity*, and nothing stopped one machine working through many. `lib/rate-limits.ts` is the policy (pure), `lib/server/rate-limit.ts` the counters, and nine public routes carry the guard. **The other four items in the row are properties of the whole codebase**, so `npm run test:security` (64) asserts them mechanically rather than in a comment — including a walk of `app/api` that fails on any route which is neither signed, secret-gated, session-gated nor limited. It found three things: a **dormant trap** in `caregivers_answerable` (`drizzle/0030`), a **zero-limit hole** in the limiter, and — via `test:e2e` — that the first set of limits **would have refused a roomful of parents signing up together**, which is Pando's own distribution model. See the decisions below. **15.3** is covered by `test:payments-live`; **15.2** by `test:matching` (44); **15.5** by `test:jobs` (33) plus the lock walked live. **15.1 and 15.6 need the real Twilio number and a deploy**, so they are the client's to schedule — the Slack relay (`test:relay-live`) is what stands in for 15.1 until then |
| M13 | Twilio channel & payments (13.2–13.7) | ✅ **complete** (3 Sep). **13.2 and 13.4's status half** were built on 27 Aug. **13.3** is `lib/sms-segments.ts`: the estimate says "the ~160-character segmentation" and the tilde is load-bearing — one character outside GSM-7 moves a message to UCS-2 and cuts the budget from 160 to 70, so a 150-character template with one em dash in it is **three** segments. It reports and never rewrites, because `sms-templates.ts` is registered A2P copy. **13.4's retry half** is `shouldRetry` + `repo/retry.ts` + the `retry_failed` job, and it is mostly refusals — see the decision below. **13.5–13.7** are `lib/server/stripe.ts` (one caller, `fetch`, no SDK), `lib/stripe-signature.ts` and `repo/payments.ts`, with `drizzle/0029` carrying the money on `blasts`. `npm run test:payments` (87) · `npm run test:payments-live` (18 — every CHECK watched refusing something, and the retry counter walked) |
| M5 | Response building — 5.1, 5.2, 5.5, 5.6 | 🟡 the parts that depend on nothing. **5.6 built** (27 Aug): `lib/trust-labels.ts` holds the approved wording verbatim, the per-category freshness ladder and the guard that public information never wears a parent label — `npm run test:trust` (47 checks), most of them asserting a label does **not** appear. **5.5 built**: `lib/server/repo/retrieval.ts` pulls what could answer a question with invariant 1 in the WHERE clause, and walked against live data. **5.1 / 5.2 were already there** — the OTP, `VERIFICATION_SESSION_HOURS = 12` and the gate authenticate the web forms, and the web layer is `join / profile / share / done`; the estimate says so itself ("the identity model is already built in Phase 1"). **5.3 built** (27 Aug): `lib/intent.ts` is the deterministic reading and `lib/server/intent.ts` the classifier on top — same model and same honesty rule as `extract.ts`. `npm run test:intent` (31 checks). **5.7 built** (27 Aug) — and **without a model**: `lib/answer.ts` composes the answer from records, because the labels come from records and not from an AI's imagination (strategy §3). `npm run test:answer` (34 checks). **5.8 built too** (27 Aug), with **14.2**: `drizzle/0023` is the `answers` queue, `lib/answer-routing.ts` decides what waits for a person, `lib/server/repo/answers.ts` sends what was approved. `npm run test:routing` (30 checks). `/admin/answers` is the screen. **The chain now has an end**: 5.5 retrieves → 5.6 labels → 5.7 composes → 5.8 holds → 14.2 reads and sends. **5.4 and 5.9 built** (27 Aug): `lib/onboarding.ts` is the one-question-at-a-time reading — what to ask next, and how to read the answer. `npm run test:onboarding` (38 checks). The repo half is built too: `repo/onboarding.ts` creates the person, records the opt-in and writes the answer back, and the webhook runs all four steps. ✅ **M5 complete** — 5.1–5.9 |
| M6 | Matching mechanism (6.1–6.7) | 🟡 **built, first cut** (27 Aug) — `lib/matching.ts` is the two-layer scorer as pure functions, `lib/server/repo/matching.ts` the one query that feeds it, `drizzle/0018` the adjacency table 6.3 needed. Walked against the live demo cohort, not only fixtures: a shared school tops every list at 5, adjacency fires on real pairs (Sierra Madre for a Monrovia parent), a near age band shows as `tween+grade`, context adds 0.5 a dimension and never outweighs a school, and every score prints the reasons that add up to it. `npm run test:matching` (44 checks). **6.7 built too** (27 Aug): `/admin/matching` — pick a parent, see who Pando would ask and why, with the weights it scored with on screen. No send button and no write action for the resource, deliberately. **Reworked twice on the client's reports** (1 and 2 Sep) — the picker is now a real combobox and each row shows the points that add up to its score; see the two Decisions rows. ✅ complete |
| 2C | Caregiver's own flow (G1–G10) | 🟡 **built, first cut** (11 Aug) — `/caregiver`, the address in the invite a parent sends. G2 consent *first*, then identity + OTP, then six skippable tap screens (roles, ages, strengths, areas + driving, days + when they can start, rate band), then G8–G10 as three separate permissions. Ends on a screen that says plainly they are **not listed yet**. Writes a `caregiver_claims` row, never a `caregivers` row — see the decision below — and an admin resolves it at `/admin/claims`. **Deletion is real** (12 Aug): `claim.delete` removes the claim, the copied profile, their consent records and the identity they made for it, in one statement, and takes a linked `caregivers` row back down the ladder — the flow promises "text DELETE and the whole profile goes", so a status change would have made that sentence false. It records *how* they asked and never asks why. **The inbound `DELETE` keyword is built** (3 Sep, M11.3) — the channel it was waiting for now exists, so the promise is kept by the thing that makes it; see the decision below. Remaining: everything that happens *after* being listed (introductions, freshness, appearing in a real answer), which is Phase 2 |
| M14 | Phase 2 admin (14.1–14.9) | ✅ **complete — 14.1–14.9** (3 Sep). **14.3 and 14.5 finished the row**: `/admin/blasts` is the blast manager (list, pool preview, fulfilment and refund flags) and `/admin/payments` is the money. The other seven were already built; what follows is the record of each. **14.1 is new** — `/admin/conversations`, and the row's wording promises something the schema deliberately cannot give; see the decision below. **14.2** is `/admin/answers` (27 Aug), **14.7** is `/admin/claims` (11 Aug) and **14.8** is `/admin/responses` (27 Aug) — all three were built under their M-numbers and need nothing. **14.3 is `/admin/blasts`** — and three of the five things the row asks for were already built elsewhere: reading a reply, rating it and promoting it are `/admin/responses` (7.6/7.9, and 14.8 under its own number). What had no home was the blast itself, so this page is the Ask, the pool preview and the two verbs nobody could reach: mark it answered, flag a refund. **The pool preview calls the same `selectPool` a live send calls**, and shows the *held* list as prominently as the chosen one — a short pool is usually four contributors inside their 48-hour gap rather than a thin network, and only that list can say which. **The observation that ties 14.4, 14.6 and 14.9 together, and it is worth acting on as one thing:** M9 and M10 wrote four tables that **nothing in the admin reads** — `impact_events`, `freshness_pings`, `answers.helped`, and the tier `standingsFor` computes. Every one of those is a page in this row (contributor tiers, thanks/impact, freshness outcomes), and every one of them is currently dead data. **14.9 is new** — `/admin/freshness`, with the two write actions `share.retire` and `share.keep`; see the decision below. **14.4 is a third view on `/admin/contributors`** rather than its own nav item, on the 13 Aug precedent that moved the consent file there — the same people asked a third question; it is the **first thing that has ever read `lib/tiers.ts`**. **14.6 is `/admin/impact`**, and it joins 9.1 to 9.2 so the case that is invisible from either half becomes visible: a parent said yes and the contributors behind it were never thanked. Between them these two and 14.9 close the hole where M9 and M10 wrote four things the admin could not see. **14.5 is `/admin/payments`**, unblocked by M13 and its own page rather than a column on 14.3: the rows overlap almost entirely and the reading does not — one asks how an Ask is going, the other what Pando owes and to whom. It leads with the configuration, on `/admin/delivery`'s rule that a page reporting money says whether the thing that moves money is switched on *before* it shows a total; **test mode is called out as loudly as unconfigured**, because a screen of plausible payments that all happened in a sandbox is the most misleading state this surface can be in |
| M11 | Caregiver processes, extended (11.1–11.5) | 🟡 **11.1–11.4 done, 11.5 refused** (3 Sep). **11.1 and 11.2 were already built** under other row numbers and are now pinned by tests rather than assumed: the four caregiver consent scopes (`caregiver_profile` · `_listing` · `_introduction` · `_reference`) with the estimate's own rule that a reference is asked of the *family*, and the 18+ gate at three layers — the chat question, the write route, and the `adults_only` CHECK, with `is_adult` in invariant 1's WHERE clause. **11.3's DELETE keyword is new**, and it is the piece the Slack relay unblocked. **11.4 is `lib/named-person.ts`** — the hole was narrower and sharper than the row's wording suggests, and it is a record whose *own name* is a person; see the two decisions below. `npm run test:caregiver` (68) · 14 live checks on the delete cascade · the detector measured against all **588** curated records in the market. **⚠️ 11.5 is superseded by invariant 13 and must not be built as written**: it asks Pando to text a nominated caregiver, and the client reversed exactly that on 3 Aug |
| new (v3.2) | ✅ demand capture (incl. the asker's neighborhood, §9) · ✅ founding approval queue · ✅ seed-reward eligibility · ✅ referral capture (`referrals`, admin-recorded) · ✅ **golden answers** (`shares.answer_ready`, §17.1) · ✅ **camps as a first-class category** (§8.4/§15.3) · ✅ **market_options import** (`npm run options:import`, QC Answers Q10) · ✅ **consent file, exportable** (`/admin/consents`, A2P §3.3) · ⬜ freshness pings · credit granting · graph write-back · forwardable answers · matching test harness · the rest of the SMS compliance layer (send/opt-out/delivery monitoring — Phase 2 surfaces) | 🟡 |

Public site: `/`, `/about`, `/privacy`, `/terms` ✅ ported from the static HTML.

## Decisions already made — do not silently revert

| Decision | Why |
| **The public half was deleted by the rule meant to protect it, and a tip was sent as a filing label** (9 Sep) | Her report on a real answer about Tom Sawyer Camps: *"я не бачу паблік інфи, як вона продемонстрована і чи присутня взагалі"*. It is built, it is presented under its own heading — and on that message it was **computed and then thrown away**. Reproduced through the real pipeline rather than reasoned about, and the log line is the whole finding: `[web-search] nothing to add { parsed: true, reply_length: 871 }` — the search ran, the model replied with 871 characters, and **every finding was dropped**. **The cause is the 8 Sep exclusion applied one record too widely.** `exclude: retrieved.shares.map((s) => s.name)` handed the search *every* retrieved name, so a question that names a place the graph already holds had its best finding removed by the rule that exists to stop a place being named twice. That rule is right about the **alternatives** — offering one class as "Validated by multiple parents" and again as general information says the two are equivalent, and they are not — and wrong about the record the question is **about**, where the parents' experience and the page's own facts are complementary and the 9 Sep heading already says which is which. So the lead is exempt (`shares.slice(1)`), and it is `shares[0]` because `rankForAnswer` preserves retrieval's order among parent records; a caregiver can outrank it only on a care question, where the search does not run at all. Measured after: the same question returns *"Public/general information: Tom Sawyer Camps - Traditional summer day camp in Altadena. Ages 3-14; 1 and 2-week sessions, five days a week."* ⚠⚠ **And the two probe assertions that should have caught this were both dead, for the same reason — the 9 Sep prose rewrite moved the thing they keyed on.** `publicLines` filtered on `includes(PUBLIC_LABEL)`, which since 9 Sep matches **only the heading**, so invariant 3 was being asserted against Pando's own approved copy: a check that cannot fail. And the duplicate check split each line on `:` or `(` because the composer once wrote `Name (venue): labels`; against prose it extracted whole sentences and could not fire at all. Both now read the block **under** the heading, and the duplicate rule is restated as the narrower thing that is still true: *no alternative is offered under both headings*. The invariant-3 check went from one assertion per answer to one per public line. **Two quality fixes on the same message.** *A finding was cut mid-word*: `clean()` was a bare `slice(0, max)` and sent *"Summer day camp with horseback riding an in Altadena"* and *"door-to-door transportatio."* — a severed "and", and a severed word wearing the full stop the composer adds, on the one block whose job is to look like a page somebody could go and check. It cuts on a word now and takes a dangling connector off the end; with no whole word inside the budget it drops, which is the rule the rest of that module already follows. ⚠ It is a presentational trim and **not** a repair — the words that remain are the model's own, in order. *And a tip was sent as its filing name*: the answer ended *"Also nearby: Camp registration timing, a tip, 1 parent; Kidspace summer camp…"*, where "Camp registration timing" is what the record was called so it could be found again and tells a parent nothing — under a phrase claiming it was **nearby**, which a tip cannot be. A tip is not an alternative, so it takes no `ALSO_LIMIT` slot and gets its own line carrying its sentence, or it is dropped. ⚠ The first attempt put that sentence *into* the list and was worse — *"…not February.; Kidspace summer camp, a class in Playhouse District…"*, a two-sentence paragraph joined to a compact directory line by a semicolon. ⚠ **The cost, measured rather than assumed:** the tip line is ~190 characters, so on that answer it squeezed out the second "Also nearby" option. The tip was the more actionable of the two — *"Most Pasadena day camps open registration in the second half of January…"* — but it is a trade, not a free win, and `TIP_LIMIT` is 1 so it cannot become a newsletter. `npm run probe:web-search` gained a `named-place` case that pins the whole finding; 40 checks across eleven live searches, `test:answer` 89, `test:e2e` 285/0. |
| **Every explanation is off every admin page — the five layers under the `Explainer`** (9 Sep) | Her report on `/admin/blasts`: the page is cluttered with information, piles of unnecessary text and explanations, *"можеш прибрати це все на ВСІХ сторінках адмінки"*. The 7 Sep pass deleted all seven `Explainer` blocks and the component; **what it did not touch is everything one level down**, and that is almost the whole of her screenshot. Counted before cutting: 21 page intros, ~45 ⓘ popovers, 25 `title=` tooltips, ~17 field help sentences, ~17 loose paragraphs, 19 empty-state bodies, 10 `noSample` sentences, 9 `FLAG_REASONS` meanings, and **21 nav hints nobody knew were rendered** (they moved to the nav link's `title` on 19 Aug — still a tooltip, still prose). ⚠ **This supersedes the 4 Sep triage rule** — *"a sentence naming a constraint the reader would otherwise walk into stays, a sentence explaining why goes"* — which is why five of the eight hints cut that day came back. It also closes the 4 Sep exception that kept **nineteen `Button title=`** as an accepted cost. Both reasonings are now history, not policy. **The rule that replaces them, and it is mechanical rather than per-page.** A string goes if it explains a concept, gives a rationale, or restates a label already on screen. It stays if it is (a) **data** — `hint="…"` is prose and `hint={…}` is a row value, which turned out to be an almost perfect discriminator, (b) a **consequence warning** on a control whose act is irreversible or recorded under the reader's own name, or (c) **current state**. Six sentences survive under (b), trimmed: *"Opening it is recorded against your name."* (the restricted note, ×2 plus its dialog), *"Your name goes on this in the audit log."* (releasing a hold), and *"Saved with your name. Never reaches the parent."* (the three admin note fields). Three survive under (a) that read like prose and are not — `"Of everyone who started"`, `"Paid and not refunded"`, `"not counted either way"` are the **denominators** of the numbers they sit under, and a bare percentage with no denominator is worse than a long one. **Status banners are kept and cut to one line**, her second instruction: the quiet-hours note is now `11:48 PM in Pasadena — outside 8am–9pm Pacific, so nothing will send.`, the three Stripe states are one clause each keeping the env var that names the fix, and `NotConfigured`'s `noSample` went from ten bespoke paragraphs to a **boolean** and one shared line. ⚠ Deleting a `<p>` is not enough on its own: `{cond && ( … )}` left behind is a **syntax** error, the fault this file already records on `/admin/freshness`, so the remover collapses the wrapper too. **Measured before and after on the same data** (the 4 Sep figures are not comparable — the queues have grown since): `/admin/blasts` 253 → 180 words and 1148 → 1037px; `/admin/flags` 1785 → 1548 and 10418 → 10093px; `/admin/caregivers` 896 → 750 and 3704 → 3363px; `/admin/activities` 2657 → 2585; `/admin/demand` 910 → 888; the Overview unchanged at 900px, one screen. Elements carrying a `title` attribute inside `<main>`: flags **80 → 0**, activities **87 → 0**, demand **46 → 0**. ⓘ triggers: activities **17 → 0**, caregivers **8 → 3** (the three kept warnings). The height falls less than the words because these pages are mostly rows, which is the honest result rather than a disappointing one. ⚠⚠ **Three facts were stated nowhere else and are now gone — they are hers to want back, not ours to reinstate.** (1) *The two contribution thresholds*: one approved contribution earns the reward, two activate Founding — the only statement of that difference anywhere in the product. (2) *`invites.opens` is not a headcount*: it counts opens, so a parent returning twice counts twice and link previews count; the column now reads as people. (3) *"The first Targeted Ask is free"*, which an admin needs when deciding whether to take payment. ⚠ And `/admin/conversations` now says nothing at all about **no message text being kept** — invariant 7 at the schema level — so a reader goes looking for the messages and the honest conclusion available to them is that the page is broken. That was already half-true after 7 Sep; deleting the intro finished it. **What the compiler did rather than a regex:** `title` was removed from `Button`, `Badge`, `Th` and `Td` outright, so `tsc` named all 25 call sites instead of a pattern guessing which `title=` was a heading and which a tooltip — `Card`, `Empty`, `Dialog` and `PageHead` all take a `title` that **is** the heading. ⚠ `TierSpec.note` in `lib/blast-tiers.ts` now has no reader but is still asserted by `test:blast` and `test:tiers`; left in place deliberately rather than dragging money tests into a copy change. ⚠ A stale `flagMeaning` assertion in `test:caregiver` had to go with the sentences it checked (68 → 66). All 25 pure suites green, `test:e2e` **285 / 0**. ⚠⚠ **And the environment trap this file already documents bit again, exactly as described**: the first `test:e2e` run reported **8 failures** in consent, phone parsing and caregiver claims — none of them anywhere near this change — after a `build` and a `git stash`/`pop` under a live dev server. Stopping the server, clearing `.next` and restarting gave 285/0 with no code change. Treat "the suite fails somewhere new" as an environment signal, not a bug. |
| **A failed read rendered "Nothing waiting" — on twenty-two open flags** (7 Sep) | Found by walking `/admin/flags` while a session had quietly expired, and it is the worst class of bug this surface can have. The page showed all four of these **at once**: the banner *"Could not load that right now"*, two section headers reading **"(0)"**, the empty states **"Nothing urgent"** and **"Nothing waiting"**, and — in the sidebar two inches away — **Flags 22**. The cause is structural rather than local: every admin page is `loading ? … : !configured ? … : empty ? … : rows`, and `error` adds only a banner **above**, so a failed read falls all the way through to the empty branch and the work area states there is nothing to do. **That is `persisted: false` broken on a read.** "We could not save your answer" and "there was nothing to save" are different sentences, and so are "we could not look" and "there is nothing waiting" — and of the things on that screen the one a person acts on is *"Nothing waiting"*, because it is where the work would be. The honest reading of that page was *I have no work today*. `Failed` is the branch now, on **all twenty-one** pages that read rows (`account`, `login` and the `consents` redirect have none), and it is deliberately one line: the banner already says what went wrong, so this only has to stop the space claiming to be empty. **The same lie was in the filter pills and needed a second fix.** Every count there is computed off the rows the page holds, so during the outage all six read **0** — "To review 0 · Needs a read 0 · … · Everything 0", six pills asserting there are zero of everything. `SegmentedFilter` takes `unknown` and suppresses the numbers wholesale: **a missing number says "not known", a zero says "none"**. One suppression at the group rather than a ternary per pill, because the pills are written one per line and the one that gets forgotten is the one nobody was looking at. Walked both ways with a stubbed 503: outage → no numbers and the failure line; healthy → `Needs a person 19 · All open 27 · Everything 33` and nineteen rows. ⚠ **The cause was not a fluke and is documented behaviour**: `admin_users` is read on every admin request behind a 60s cache, and if that store is *configured and unreadable* the mode is `unavailable` and no session is honoured — fail-closed, by the 12 Aug decision, and correct. So this state is reachable in production by a single pooler hiccup, which is exactly when a false "nothing waiting" is most expensive. ⚠ **One smaller instance is left and named:** a `Card` title carrying a count (`Needs a person today (13)`) still renders `(0)` on a failed read. It is the weaker case — a heading rather than the place a reader looks for work — and fixing it means threading the unknown state into `Card`, which is a wider change than this pass. |
| **Grid or line is a property of the values — `/admin/demand` was 347px of card for a sentence and two words** (7 Sep) | Measured at 1440×900 rather than eyeballed. The demand record carries exactly **two** facts, one word each — a category and a neighbourhood — and `FactGrid` is `auto-fit, minmax(14rem, 1fr)`, so with two items it gave each of them **half of a 1,130px row**, as a label stacked above a value. Thirteen questions came to **4,508px**. The grid was doing its job (a fact never squeezed below a readable line); the job was the wrong one. `FactLine` + `FactInline` put short facts on one baseline, and the rule is the table-or-cards rule one level down: **three or more facts, or any longer than about three words → `FactGrid`; two or three short ones → `FactLine`.** `/admin/activities` keeps the grid, because eight facts on one line is not a line. It stays a real `<dl>` — the facts are still facts to anything reading the document, and only their arrangement changed. **Result: 347px → 176px a card, a 49% cut, with nothing hidden.** Three more things went from that page in the same pass, all of them repetition rather than information. *The two banners lost their second paragraphs* — an allegation banner explaining that these are never circulated, a high-stakes one explaining that resources were already shown — keeping the counts, which are the only part the tabs do not carry (the "Needs a person" tab merges the two classes). *A duplicate badge went*: every open row carried **"Waiting for you to read it"** next to a status badge reading **"Not looked at"**, two pills for one fact, and what the first was really distinguishing — *this one needs a person* — is what the sensitivity badge to its left already says. Its history is kept in a comment, because it once read "not usable until read" driven by a column **nothing ever clears**, so a question you had read, followed up and answered claimed to be unread for ever; tying it to `status` fixed that lie and deleting it fixes the repetition. *And the policy hint on the sensitivity badge* rendered once per row, so two sentences became **nineteen** `?` triggers on one page — the same fault as the contributions queue's twenty-three, and the page is now at zero. Words on the page: 946 → 778. |
| **Every explanation is off every admin page, and the component is gone with them** (7 Sep) | The client's instruction, immediately after the pass that unified them: *"Видали пояснення з усіх сторінок."* All **seven** `Explainer` blocks removed — contributions, Network Asks, conversations, withdrawn recommendations, thanks & impact, payments, and the contributor standing view — and the component deleted rather than left as a dead export, so there is nothing for a future session to reach for by habit. `peek` and `muted` went with it: between them they had exactly one consumer and it was that wrapper, and an unused prop is the dead-payload fault this file already records twice. **`Disclosure` stays**, with one caller: the Overview's "Show the numbers", which is *content* rather than explanation — she asked for the explanations, and folding away nine sections of statistics was her own instruction of 4 Sep. **This supersedes three earlier rows and the reasoning in them is now history, not policy** — 2 Sep ("explanations go on the page, said once, where the work is"), 3 Sep ("closed by default, hover peeks, click pins") and the 7 Sep consolidation that moved 23 repeated `Hint` triggers into one explainer on the contributions queue. That consolidation still paid off in the direction that matters: the hints are gone and did not come back, so the queue carries **zero** repeated `?` triggers rather than 23, and what was deleted here is the one paragraph they had been folded into. ⚠ **One deletion has a named cost, and it is the client's to accept or reverse.** `/admin/conversations` said, once, at the top, that **no message text is kept** — invariant 7 holding at the *schema* level, since `message_log` has no body column. That page's remaining intro is *"Every parent Pando has exchanged a message with. Nothing is sent from this page."*, which does not say it. So a reader now goes looking for the messages and the honest conclusion available to them is that the page is broken. Putting it back is one clause in that page's own one-line intro rather than an explainer. ⚠ **Two smaller consequences of the sweep, both fixed rather than left:** removing the block on `/admin/freshness` left `{filtered.length > 0 && ( )}` — an empty conditional, which is a *syntax* error and was caught by `tsc` rather than by review; and four comments across `matching`, `page.tsx` and `kit.tsx` still named `Explainer` as a live thing to reach for, which is a file lying about the code beside it. |
| **Seven ways to hide something, three of them the same `<details>` written three times** (7 Sep) | The client's report was *"some sections hide and some don't, it's all unclear, too much information on the page, the structure is hard for a new user"*. The first half was countable, so it was counted. **Seven distinct "there is more here" affordances**: `Explainer`, the Overview's own numbers fold, the sidebar's group fold, `Hint`, `RevealMore`, `RecordDrawer`, and `NotConfigured`'s sample button — of which the first three were **hand-written variants of one `<details>`**, with three different chevrons (`▶` at 10px, `▶` at 12px, a lucide `ChevronRight` at 11px) at three type sizes (12.5px uppercase, 14px medium, 11px uppercase). Which one a reader met was not a decision anybody took; it was whoever wrote that page. **`Disclosure` is the one now**, and `DisclosureChevron` is exported so the sidebar's fold — which *cannot* be a `<details>`, because on a phone that nav is a horizontal scrolling row and every link has to stay in the document — still turns the same shape the same way. What stays distinct is only what is genuinely a different job: `Hint` for one word inside a sentence, `RevealMore` for more rows of the same list (a **down** arrow, because that is what it means), `RecordDrawer` for a panel belonging to one record, `SegmentedFilter` for changing which rows you are looking at. **Two properties differ, and both are properties of the content rather than of the author.** *`peek`* — hovering previews an explanation, which is the client's 3 Sep instruction kept verbatim — is deliberately **not** the default: peeking a nine-section statistics grid would make the page jump every time the pointer crossed it, so content opens on a click and only help previews on hover, and `Explainer` is the name for the help case so no call site has to decide. *`flush`* puts the box on the trigger row alone and drops the panel below it unpadded, because the Overview's numbers are a grid of `Stat` tiles and cards inside a white bordered card is the card-in-card the design system argues against — while prose has to be *inside* the box, or a bordered trigger leaves a naked paragraph on bare paper, which is the exact fault this component was moved out of a `Card` to fix on 4 Sep. **On "too much information", one page was measured rather than argued about.** `/admin/activities` is 3,637px and carried **23 `Hint` triggers**, every one of them with the accessible name "What this means" — which is the "23 buttons all called Edit" fault from the parent flow's review screen, on the admin side. Only *two* of them were in the source: the rest came from the card map, so two sentences rendered eleven times each. That is the 2 Sep `RecordGroup` rule stated backwards — a fact true of a whole run of records belongs above them, and what stays on the card is what differs between them — so the words moved, unchanged, into one `Explainer`, and the page now has **zero** repeated triggers. Three status-badge hints went with them: three of the four sentences restated the label they hung off ("Waiting for you" → *"Nobody has looked at this yet"*), and the four statuses are already the tabs at the top of the page. `ProvenanceBadge` lost its hint on `parent_submitted` only — *"A real parent submitted this"* against a badge reading **Parent** — and `admin_entered` keeps its sentence, because that one carries a rule the label does not say (invariant 4). **The rule left behind is legible: a badge has a hint exactly when the sentence says something its label does not.** ⚠ **Two things deliberately not done, both hers rather than ours.** *A queue card shows eight facts* (where · who for · would recommend · paid · how recent · follow-up · counts toward Founding · how useful) and folding half of them behind a disclosure would halve the page — but it changes what a reviewer sees *before* approving a record, which is a decision about how she reviews and not a layout choice. *Five of twenty pages have an explainer and fifteen have none*, which is its own "some do, some don't"; closing that means writing fourteen new explanations, and new user-facing copy is hers to approve. ⚠ And a testing note that cost twenty minutes: **`pointerenter` does not bubble**, so a synthetic `PointerEvent` dispatched on the `<summary>` never reaches the handler on `<details>` and the peek looks broken. Drive it with a real hover. The chevron's rotation is likewise invisible to a `transform` check — Tailwind v4 sets the `rotate` property, and `transition-transform` in v4 covers `transform, translate, scale, rotate`, measured at 0.15s. |
| **"Change password" and "Sign out" were two underlined words, because `Button` could only ever be a `<button>`** (7 Sep) | The client pointed at the sidebar footer and asked for real buttons. The interesting part is *why* they were not: `Button` took `onClick` and rendered a `<button>`, full stop — and "Change password" is a **navigation**. So the pair could not both come out of the primitive, and what happened instead is what always happens: both were hand-written, as underlined text, in **four copies** across the desktop and phone footers. That is the `TextAction` lesson from the parent flow arriving one surface along, and it is worth restating because no care at the call site prevents it: a browser **centres a `<button>`'s own label vertically and leaves a blockified `<a>`'s at the top of its box**, so two 44px controls in one `items-center` row still have their labels 11.5px apart — `items-center` aligns the boxes, which was never the problem. `Button` takes an `href` now and renders the `Link` itself, from the same box. **The two shapes are a union, not one loose prop bag**: a link cannot be given `type="submit"` or a `disabled` an anchor has no way to honour — a disabled link is not a thing the platform has, and faking it with `pointer-events: none` leaves it in the tab order, reachable and dead. **The layout is a measurement, not a preference.** Side by side the pair came to **225px inside a 224px column** and wrapped by one pixel, leaving a wide button above a narrow one; stacked and stretched they are 223px each and nothing about them depends on a label staying short. The phone footer keeps its row, where auto width is right. ⚠ **Sign out is `secondary`, not `danger`** — red on this surface means *owed a person today* (the `alert` token's own rule), and signing out is neither urgent nor a mistake. **`TextLink` came out of the same pass**, because the fifteen underlined links across the admin were the same drift one level down: `12px` / `12.5px` / `13.5px` / inherited, some `font-semibold` and some not, one hovering to `alert` rather than green. It deliberately **sets no font size** — several live in 12.5px table cells and forcing one size would make the link the biggest thing in its row — so what it fixes is the treatment, which was never a per-call-site decision. Seven converted; ⚠ **three deliberately not**, and the reason is the one this file keeps relearning: consolidating is where a deliberate one-off gets flattened. The invite code is `font-mono` and *not* semibold and carries its own `aria-label`; the "wrong" control on a referral is 12px, muted and `disabled`-able; and a voided referral's name renders struck through with no underline at all, which is a link that has stopped being one. **And the sign-in form's error was a bare `<p className="text-alert">`** — no `role`, so "that didn't match" was announced to **nobody**, on the one screen whose only next step is reading the message. It is `ErrorNote` now, which carries `role="alert"`; that component gained a `className` that **replaces** its default `mb-4` rather than joining it, because `cn()` is a plain join and two margins in one layer are resolved by Tailwind's output order, not by the string. |
| **The sidebar scrolled with no scrollbar and no fade, and the fold put a door in that blind spot** (7 Sep) | Found by measuring the nav after folding it, not by looking at it: at 1440×900 the nav needs **726px and gets 643**, and the thing sitting in the last 83px was the **"Records & tools" toggle** — the one control that reveals six pages. It was reachable only by scrolling a column that gives no sign it scrolls. **The cause is older than the fold and is a one-word assumption.** `useEdgeFade` measured `scrollLeft` / `clientWidth` / `scrollWidth` only — it was written for the phone's horizontal nav strip and for `TableWrap` — while the admin nav is **horizontal below `md` and vertical from `md`**. So on every desktop it computed `end: false` (nothing overflows sideways), the mask came out `none`, and since the same element carries `no-scrollbar`, a sidebar taller than the window had **no scrollbar and no fade: nothing at all saying there was more of it below**. That had been true since the sticky sidebar was written — with twenty links unfolded, ~244px hung below the edge unannounced — and the fold is what made it expensive, by moving a *door* into that band rather than the tail of a list. **The axis is measured, never passed in.** A prop would make the caller say "x below md, y above", which is a second copy of the breakpoint in a file that cannot see the stylesheet; asking the element which way it actually overflows survives the breakpoint change on its own, and `TableWrap` keeps its horizontal fade with no call-site change. Vertical wins a tie only because nothing in this admin scrolls both ways — a container that did would need two masks composited, and one gradient could not say so. Walked live: at 1440 the nav's mask is `linear-gradient(black, black calc(100% - 16px), transparent)`, scrolling to the bottom flips it to fade the *top* and brings the toggle fully into view, and the toggle then goes 14 links → 20 → 14; at 375 both the nav and the contributors table are back to a right-edge horizontal fade, and a table that fits gets `none`. |
| **The admin was built to be read and not to be worked — six faults, and two of them were comments describing a badge that was never there** (7 Sep) | The developer's verdict was that the admin is not comfortable to use, so it was measured rather than restyled — every one of the recent passes on this surface was about *how it reads* (labels, hints, prose, spacing, row counts) and none about *what it is like to work through a queue*. **(1) A worklist row named a number and then linked to a different list.** `/admin/activities?filter=golden` had been on the Overview since 19 Aug and the contributions page **does not read search params at all**, so the parameter did nothing and the reader landed on "To review" — the dead-payload fault this repository has already paid for twice, on the one screen whose job is to point at work. Worse in the demand queue: "other questions parents asked · 13" linked to a page that opens on **Needs a person**, i.e. precisely the rows that are not in it. `lib/admin/url-state.ts` is the fix and it buys three things at once — the link works, a reload keeps the tab, and a tab is a shareable address. ⚠ **`replaceState`, never `pushState`**: a filter is not a place, and with `pushState` somebody who tried five tabs would press Back five times to leave the page. The tab list is a module-level `as const` on each page, so the pills and the address bar are typed against one array and cannot name different tabs. **(2) One click froze every button on the page.** Twelve of fourteen pages held `busy` as a page-wide boolean, so approving one recommendation disabled all thirty rows for the round trip — on the surface whose whole purpose is working through a list. It is the row's id now on every queue (`claims` and `founding` had been right since 11 Aug), and the *binding* is done at the call site rather than inside the row: `run={(label, fn) => run(r.id, label, fn)}`, so the four module-level row components — the ones extracted on 3 Sep because a component declared inside a render is a new type every keystroke — keep their `busy: boolean` prop untouched. The rule for the two pages that keep a page-wide flag is stated rather than left as drift: **a queue disables the row it is working; a form disables itself** (`/admin/matching` saves weights, `/admin/account` changes a password), and `/admin/blasts` and `/admin/invites` are both, so their create form has its own key. `/admin/freshness` keeps a page-wide flag and is not an exception: its decision is taken in a drawer that only one record can be open in at a time, so the row and the page are the same thing there. **(3) The sidebar went stale the moment you acted.** `useQueueCounts` re-read on navigation only, under a comment claiming *"a count that goes stale the moment you act on it is worse than no count"* — half the sentence was true. `adminAction` now fires one `pando:admin-write` event, fired **in the one function every write goes through** for the same reason `/api/admin/action` is the one path that cannot forget its audit row; the shell debounces it 600ms, because clearing a queue is a burst and the overview is the heaviest read here. **(4) Two nav items had comments explaining a count they did not have.** `/admin/answers` carried *"A queue, so it counts — and during the pilot it counts every answer"* and `/admin/responses` sat in "Waiting on you" with no badge at all, because `Overview` had **no field for either**. So the group named for things waiting on you contained two pages that could not say whether anything was. Both are counted now, and `answers.waiting` deliberately includes **approved-but-unsent** — that is a parent still waiting, which is exactly why 14.2 gives it its own gold card. **(5) Twenty nav items, grouped by a rule that had leaked.** Payments sat under "Records" while carrying the only red badge in the admin, and "Names & places" sat there with a gold one, while two counted-nothing pages sat under "Waiting on you". Regrouped: the Ask lifecycle (14.3 → 14.8 → 14.5) is one group instead of three items in two, and **"Records & tools" folds away** — six links to one line on desktop. ⚠ **Which pages fold was not a judgement**: it is exactly the set carrying no count, a decision this file had already taken page by page with the reason written above each one (a tool you open, 6.7 · a gauge not a queue, 12.5 · a record not a worklist, 14.1 and 14.6). The type enforces it — a folding section takes `QuietItem`, which is `Omit<NavItem, "count" \| "urgent">` — so a page with a badge cannot be filed behind a fold, which would be a badge nobody sees. It opens itself when you are inside it, closes when you leave, and remembers nothing; and it is `md:hidden` rather than `hidden`, because on a phone the nav is one horizontal scrolling row where a fold saves no height and costs a tap. **(6) Nothing answered "where is Sarah".** Every route to a person went through a page that happens to list them, so `components/admin/QuickFind.tsx` is a `/` (or Ctrl+K) dialog over the contributors list. **It reuses `lib/admin/person-search.ts` rather than ranking again** — the module `test:person-search` already pins, so "south pas" finds South Pasadena and every term must match something; a second ranking rule would be a second thing to get wrong, and a search rule fails silently. It is fetched on first open (`useAdminRows`'s `enabled`), it navigates and never writes, and it distinguishes three silences — no database, nobody yet, nothing matching. ⚠ **Deliberately people only, and the description says so**: a box that silently covers one of five kinds of record is worse than one that names its subject. ⚠ **What was not done, and is the client's rather than ours:** merging 14.1 with 12.5, or the three Ask pages into one screen. Each is an estimate row with its own deliverable and the 3 Sep decision gives the reason they are separate pages — *"the rows overlap almost entirely and the reading does not"* — so the nav groups them and nothing is merged. |
| **The database owns a saved profile; the phone is a cache — and every phone is reset once** (8 Sep) | The client's report, and it is two faults with one cause: *"when a profile is saved localStorage should be cleared"*, and *"when a person comes back they are pulled what is in localStorage rather than what is in the database"*. **The cause is that both copies exist and the wrong one outranked the other.** The flow is autosaved to the device (1.10), the profile is written to Postgres on the confirmed code, and after that moment nothing ever reconciled them — so they diverge the first time the same parent fills the form on a second device (the write is an upsert on the number, invariant 10, and every derived set is *replaced*), or the first time an admin corrects a record. A cache that beats the record is `persisted: false` inverted. **The literal instruction is the one thing not done, and the reason is worth stating.** Clearing `localStorage` at the moment of saving takes the *session* with it — `phone_verified`, the invite code, the chat draft, the referral code, the name `/done` greets by — so `/share` and the three completion screens would go blank one tap after "saved". What is stale is the **answers**, and the answers now come back from the database instead: `GET /api/seed/me` returns `people.raw_answers`, `ProfileFlow` re-reads them **on mount** for a session whose profile is already saved, and `/signin` hydrates them on a device that has never seen this parent. Walked live: a device copy edited to `monrovia` / age 17 / no referral code snapped back to `altadena` / age 4 / `referral-346f4f` on the next `/profile`, and a signed-in browser with **empty** storage came back with all 32 answers. ⚠ **This reverses the 7 Sep decision** — *"What signing in restores … **not** the answers. Those live on the device that gave them"* — and that decision's own reason is spent, the same way the single-select one's was. It rested on there being no per-parent token (31 Jul, 12 Aug, 27 Aug), so nothing could prove a browser belonged to a profile. `/signin` **is** that proof: the phone comes from `verifiedPhone(verifyCookie(request))`, never from the request body, and it is the same proof `submitGate` demands before anything may be **written** — handing a caller their own answers is strictly less than what that cookie already lets them overwrite. ⚠ **What still does not travel, and both screens say so**: the session itself — no `screen_index`, no chat draft, no half-finished card. Those belong to the device making them, and a recommendation is not a fact Pando has agreed to keep until it is saved. **On mount only**, so it can never overwrite an edit somebody is in the middle of making; and every silence (no cookie → 401, no database → 503, a profile older than the column) leaves the device copy exactly as it was — a refresh that fails is silence, never an empty review, which is the `persisted: false` rule applied to a read. `fetchMe` is one typed shape rather than the third hand-written copy of it, and it reports each failure as a *state* rather than throwing, because 401, 503 and `found: false` mean three different things to a parent. **And the second half of her ask is one line**: the storage key is `pando.seed.v2`, with `pando.seed.v1` **removed** on load rather than left to sit. That is how every browser is reset at once — `clearSession` was reachable only from a control on `/join` somebody had to find and press, so sessions from every build since July were still being resumed. ⚠ The cost, stated rather than discovered: anybody mid-questionnaire when this deploys starts again. A **saved** profile loses nothing, because the database is the record. |
| **Two pluses on one control, and the copy and the affordance were each right** (8 Sep) | Her report: the "Something else" option has two — one outer, one inner. Both are load-bearing on their own, which is why neither side looked wrong in review. `SOMETHING_ELSE` in `questions.ts` is **her own wording, quoted verbatim** on 1 Sep — *"Keep only '+ Something else,' which opens a short optional field"* — so the string carries a literal plus; and `AddOtherChip` draws its own `+` glyph, as it does on the eight labels that never had one ("Another school", "Another camp"). Together: an icon and a character, twice. **The glyph wins**, because it is the mark every one of these controls shares and the text is not. Stripped in the **primitive** rather than at the five call sites, so a future question that copies her label cannot bring the duplicate back — her wording stays exactly as stored, and `test:feedback`'s assertion that the five questions carry `otherLabel === "+ Something else"` is untouched (112). It also fixes an accessible name that read *"plus Something else"*. ⚠ The "Other" sheet's heading takes the same treatment, because it is the same string with no icon beside it to pair with. Walked at 375px: one glyph, one label, 165×48. |
| **The invite link is on the screen a parent actually sits on** (8 Sep) | Her instruction: put the referral link on the sharing screen too, as a small highlighted box beside the Pando wordmark with a way to copy. Until now it appeared **twice and both were easy to miss** — a popup shown once at the first verification, and a panel on `/done`, which is two taps past the screen a contributor spends their whole session on. **A pill that opens a box, rather than a button that copies.** A header at 375px leaves about 200px to the right of the wordmark, which is a pill and not a link — and a pill that copied straight away would have nowhere to put the sentence that matters when the clipboard is **refused**, which is real (some webviews, and any browser with site data blocked) and which every earlier version of this pattern in the app swallowed: the parent taps, something says "copied", nothing is on the clipboard, and they paste the previous clipboard into a group chat. So the box carries the link *visibly*, and `CopyLink` — the same one the popup and the thank-you panel use — owns both the copying and the honest failure line. Making the link readable is the only fallback there is. **`absolute` inside a `relative` wrapper, not `fixed` and not a `<dialog>`**: the header is sticky and already paints above the transcript, while a `fixed` overlay would be clipped by any animated ancestor (the 5 Aug bug — which is why the *popup* two functions along **is** a real dialog). It is deliberately not modal: nothing behind it needs to be inert, and trapping focus on a link somebody may want to read beside the conversation would be hostile. Escape and a pointer outside both close it, or a panel whose only exit is the control that opened it is the "dead button" complaint one step along. Walked at 375px: pill 104×48 at the right edge, panel 335px wide inside a 375px viewport with `document.scrollWidth` still 375, and all four open/close paths. **And the link is picked out where it was already printed**, on her follow-up the same day: the referral card on `/done/next` put `pando.is/join?i=…` in a run of grey inside a grey paragraph, so the one string on that card that has to be *recognised* — the address about to be pasted into a group chat — looked like the sentence around it. ⚠ The message is built from three pieces now (intro · link · tail) precisely so `message` stays **byte-identical**: it is what `CopyButton` puts on the clipboard, and any markup that reached it would arrive in somebody's chat as literal angle brackets. The token is `rounded-2xl` rather than a new radius — the design system's input step, which on one line reads as the same pill the `/share` header wears, so the two places a parent meets their own link look like one thing — and `inline-block break-all`, so a longer code grows it to two lines instead of pushing the card off a 375px screen. Measured: 221×31, green-deep on green-wash at 7.0:1 against `text-muted` body copy, `document.scrollWidth` still 375, copied text unchanged. ⚠ The pill's wording is new user-facing copy, on the list for the client. |
| **What a parent actually wants: who it suits, the tip, and a fact on every public line** (9 Sep) | Her third pass: the parents' half is *"достатньо непогана"* but improvable on what is genuinely interesting, and the public half is still too thin. **Two fields were captured, approved, and never sent.** `who_for` is the closest thing the graph has to the strategy's own *"all with high-energy kids around that age"* — *"A cautious toddler who warms up slowly"*, *"First-time parents who want to be told what to do"*, *"A child who is not sporty yet"* — and it is what a parent choosing between two classes is actually choosing on. `tip_text` is her example's *"one tip: it fills fast after Labor Day"*, and it is a different sentence from the caveat: a caveat is a reason to hesitate, a tip is how to do it well. Both render now, under the same two guards as the quote (firsthand only, null on an open `possible_named_person` flag). ⚠ `lowerFirst` is the **only** change ever made to a parent's words besides collapsing whitespace, and it applies only where the field is a fragment Pando introduced ("Best for:", "One tip:") — a **quoted** sentence is never touched, because an altered sentence in quotation marks attributed to somebody is precisely what invariant 8 is about. **On the public half, two changes.** Each finding may now carry **one short factual thing the page actually states** — the ages it takes, when it runs, drop-in or a term, a published price — where the prompt previously banned hours and prices outright. That ban protected the wrong thing: what makes a price honest is not its absence but the heading above it, and "Public/general information:" says where it came from. The prompt forbids inferring, rounding and guessing, and `readFindings` drops rather than repairs, so a missing detail is a page that did not say it. And the floor went from **one line to two**, up to four when the parents are thin — one was set when the budget was three segments and every public line cost a parent record, and neither is true any more. Live: *"Swimphi Altadena - private swim lessons. Ages 3+, Sundays, $60 per 30-min class"*, *"LoveBug & Me Music - baby and toddler music classes. Ages 0-5 years"*. ⚠ `test:answer` had a second fixture hard-coded against a constant (four findings against a cap of three); it is sized from `MAX_PUBLIC_FINDINGS` now — the same fix `test:blast` needed an hour earlier, which is the pattern: a fixture written against the number rather than the rule. ⚠⚠ **Still open, and a ranking question rather than a bug:** *"any good toddler classes near South Pasadena?"* leads with a **library storytime** over a music class with three parents in the right town, because `focusInQuestion` reduces "classes" to the catch-all `activities` bucket, the library is tagged that way and Little Maestros is `arts_music`. A catch-all topic should probably not outrank the area and the evidence, or a question should carry a *set* of topics rather than one. Put to the client rather than guessed at. |
| **Both halves in every answer, and the budget is no longer what decides** (9 Sep) | Her follow-up on the rewritten answer: *"а де в цьому всьому паблік інфа… мені не важливий наразі розмір повідомлення, але має бути як паблік інфа, так і від батьків"* — strategy §3's rule that an answer carries up to four kinds of material and **always says which is which**. Three changes. **(1) `SMS_BUDGET` 459 → 765** (153 × 5, kept a whole number of segments). At three segments the two halves competed for one space: a lead block in the parents' own words is 250-350 characters, a second option ~90, and general information got the remainder, which was frequently nothing. ⚠ **Roughly 67% more per answer**, one constant, and *наразі* is her own word for how long it stands. **(2) The public material is its own block with its own heading** rather than a third list item distinguished by a trailing label — which is what made it invisible: same shape as a parent's record, and the one sentence saying otherwise was at the end of the line. The heading is `TRUST_LABEL.PUBLIC` **verbatim**, taken from the labels 5.6 computed rather than written out here; each line under it then drops the label it would have repeated. Suppressed when the whole answer is public, where `head` already says it. **(3) Every public line is reserved for, not just the first** — reserving one meant a second was dropped silently whenever the parents' half ran long. ⚠ **And `publicSlots` could never do what its own doc claimed.** *"More when the parents are thin, up to three"* was `3 - parentBacked` measured against what retrieval **returned** (limit 10, and every band holds fewer records than that), so the expression was routinely `max(1, negative)`: **one line, every time, whatever the search found.** It measures against what a parent actually reads — a lead plus one "also nearby", so at most two — which makes the sentence true. ⚠ `test:blast` failed on the budget change because a fixture hard-coded 300-character replies against 459; it is sized *from* `SMS_BUDGET` now, so it tests the dropping rule rather than the constant. |
| **An answer speaks in the parents' own words now, and the trust chain is prose** (9 Sep) | The client sent the strategy and said the message Pando replies with to an ordinary question is not what she wants. The diff against her own example is structural, not cosmetic, and the decisive fact is in the database: **eight approved contributions already carry a parent's sentence** — *"Saturdays are packed, take the 9am"*, *"Sign up the week registration opens or you are on a waitlist"*, *"The water is genuinely warm, which is the whole thing at this age"* — which is verbatim the material her example is made of (*"Two said the 9am class is calmer than the 10:30 … it fills fast after Labor Day"*), and **not one had ever reached a parent.** What went instead was a catalogue entry: `Little Maestros - class in South Pasadena. $30 a session. Validated by multiple parents. Human-reviewed. Last confirmed Aug 2026.` ⚠ **This reverses the 11.4 boundary** — `AnswerCandidate` had *no free-text field at all*, on the reading that invariant 8 forbade one. It does not: the invariant is that free text **about a named person** is never published **without human review**, and both halves hold here, because these sentences are about a class and `share_contributions.status = 'approved'` **is** the contributions queue. Two guards make that real rather than asserted — firsthand contributions only, and **null for any record carrying an open or escalated `possible_named_person` flag**, which is the one place a reviewer's doubt outranks their approval. ⚠ **And the trust chain is now prose, which is a re-approval under invariant 3 and hers to give** — she gave it, in the strategy's own wording: *"Three parents near you have used Toddler Tunes"* and the compact `3 parents · confirmed 4 weeks ago`. It **says more, not less**: a number where "multiple" was a word, from the same `firsthand_count` 5.6 labels off. `Human-reviewed` is dropped as a process fact her own format omits; `Public/general information` is **untouched and verbatim**, because invariant 4 is the one this prose could actually break. `SAID_IN_PROSE` is a second copy of approved copy in the product file, and `test:answer` checks it against `trust-labels.ts` character for character. **Shape: one record deep, one line for the second** — her choice when offered depth-only against depth-plus-comparison, which keeps the reason `SMS_BUDGET` went to three segments on 4 Sep. The 38-character preamble is gone: the first sentence now *is* the evidence. ⚠⚠ **Two defects in my own build, both found only by composing from live rows and neither visible in review.** *A parent's em dash took a 304-character answer from 2 segments to **5*** — so the note goes through `toGsm7`, which changes typography and never words, and is **dropped whole** when it cannot be made GSM-7 (never truncated, never reworded); ⚠ `toGsm7` returns null both when the text is *already clean* and when it is *hopeless*, so the two are separated by asking what the original encodes as — bare, it drops every clean note; with `?? text`, it sends the one thing it exists to catch. And *the quote picked was the least useful one* — ordering by recency chose "Lovely for the little ones" over "Small groups and the teacher is unbelievably patient". It orders by `sc.confidence` now, which is precisely the question the 12 Aug extraction pass answers: how much could another parent act on this text. ⚠ A backtick in a SQL comment closed the template for the **fifth** time. Measured on real rows: three answers, 3 segments each, all GSM-7. |
| **An answer was ranked by evidence and never by relevance, so the whole of 5.5 reached no parent** (9 Sep) | The client's report was that the messages carry too little information and *"публічна інфа замала"*. Two audits — one over the outbound path, one over matching — and the first finding is the whole of the complaint's first half. **`retrieveFor` orders by the question's topic, then the asker's area; `rankForAnswer` re-sorted by evidence and reversed it.** `AnswerCandidate` had no field for relevance at all, so the ordering could not survive the hand-off — and measured on the live graph every age band holds fewer records than retrieval's own `limit`, so the SQL `ORDER BY` could not even change *which* rows came back. It ordered them and the composer threw the order away. **`focusInQuestion`, `drizzle/0032`, `npm run focus:backfill` and the area rank were all computed, carried, and read by nothing** — the written-and-never-called fault, on the one path that talks to parents. From `answers` on 8 Sep, sent to a real phone: *"Please give me some great school in Passadena"* came back with a **watershed park** and a **summer camp**, the only school on the page being the single public line. The fix is `AnswerCandidate.rank`, set from retrieval's index and sorted immediately after `public_only`. ⚠ **It ranks and never filters** (the 4 Sep rule: the tag is model-written, so a wrong one re-orders and can never drop the right record and leave nothing saying so), and it is the *index* rather than the topic, so the rule stays in the one place that knows the question. **And `answer_ready` was the first key in the SQL too, which was the wrong reading of §17.1**: that flag means *complete enough to answer a question with* — about the record, not about **this** question — and six of fifteen live records carry it, so any golden record beat the only on-topic one. Measured: for three of the seven populated topics the first on-topic record sat at **rank 7**, and the budget fits about three. It is a tiebreak among equally relevant records now. ⚠⚠ **Two things the client must still decide, with the numbers**: the public half is **one line by arithmetic** (`publicSlots` reserves `3 − retrieved`, and retrieval has no subject so it is never thin), and **53% of every message is scaffolding** — 1,173 of 2,229 characters across six real answers are the opening sentence plus the repeated trust chain, which at 71 characters a record is the 4 Sep open item ("shortening it is a re-approval under invariant 3, not a copy edit"). A second public line and a bigger `SMS_BUDGET` each cost roughly one parent record. |
| **Everything that was not a question got silence, and a web result could cost seven segments** (9 Sep) | Three more from the outbound audit, all confirmed by driving the built app. **(1) `chitchat` and free-form `contribute` fell off the end of `handleInboundMessage`** — no reply, no queued answer, no pending question. The 8 Sep `pending_questions` work closed `unclear` and a *mid-exchange* `chitchat`; a **cold** one and **any** offer were still dropped, and the offer is the worse of the two because the parent is trying to *give* Pando something (7 Sep's gap 1, whose own example is *"we loved Little Gym"*). `lib/replies.ts` holds the two answers — `SHARE_INVITE` sends an offer to the flow that can take it properly, on `capture.ts`'s reasoning that five closed questions collect a record and a stray sentence does not; `SMALL_TALK` says what the number is for and **asks nothing**, because 5.4 owns onboarding and a second question would make the next reply ambiguous. Its own module rather than `sms-templates.ts` (registered copy), `onboarding.ts` (clarifying questions) or `capture.ts` (the script) — and pure, so `test:routing` can pin both at one GSM-7 segment. ⚠ Both are new user-facing copy, on the list for the client. **(2) A public finding's name never went through `toGsm7`.** Business names carry curly apostrophes and en dashes routinely, and one character outside GSM-7 moves the **whole message** to UCS-2: measured, 426 chars / 3 segments clean against 439 chars / **7 segments** with one `’` in a finding's name — so `SMS_BUDGET`'s documented "exactly three segments" broke silently and the bill rose 133%. Safe to rewrite, unlike everything registered: a name read off a web page is neither approved copy nor a parent's own words, which is why `blast-answer.ts` keeps the opposite rule for a parent's own em dash. **(3) Five things on that path are computed and read by nobody** — `ComposeInput.has_question`, `Retrieved.parent_backed`, `PublicSearchResult.configured`, `ShareCandidate.secondhand_count`, and `claimsAParent`, whose own doc claims 5.8's review queue runs it (only the test does). ⚠ Not fixed here, and `configured` is the one that matters: *"the search did not run"* and *"the search found nothing"* are currently indistinguishable downstream, which is the `persisted: false` honesty rule inverted. |
| **A Pasadena district was an island: 14 of 39 parents matched no school, no neighbour and no record** (9 Sep) | The matching audit's two critical findings, one cause. `market_options.neighborhoods` holds **79** values — the seventeen curated towns **plus** fourteen Pasadena districts (Old Pasadena, Bungalow Heaven, Linda Vista, Hastings Ranch, San Rafael…) and Altadena Foothills — and every one of them already carries an `area_slug` naming its city. **The roll-up was in the data and nothing read it.** Everything else is keyed on the town, so a district matched nothing anywhere: **14 of 39** contributors were offered eight schools from Highland Park, Alhambra and Monterey Park while seven curated Pasadena schools went unoffered; the same 14 had a neighborhood appearing nowhere in `neighborhood_adjacency`, so **8 of its 17 areas had no residents at all** (`pasadena` among them, and it alone appears in 6 of the 29 seeded pairs) and two parents in *adjoining districts* scored zero; and **13 of 39** matched no approved record's area. ⚠ Four of those parents are `source = direct`/`link` — real walk-throughs, not demo rows. This is the **third** time one area comparison has silently matched nothing (27 Aug: a display name against a slug; 1 Sep: capped before the area was known), and all three share a shape: typecheck clean, suites green, feature doing the opposite of its purpose, visible only against real rows. Fixed in three places from one idea — the options endpoint now serves an `areas` roll-up (it must be **served**, because only the seventeen starters reach the browser and a district arrives only if somebody searched for it), `lib/server/repo/areas.ts` gives the server the same map behind the option cache, and `starters.ts` takes `areaCity` as an argument so it keeps its whole point of loading in plain node. ⚠ **The group always contains the area itself**, so a town, an unknown value and an unconfigured database behave exactly as the bare comparison did — nothing here can make a match narrower. ⚠ **Two districts of one city take the *adjacent* path at weight 1, not same-area at 3**: they are genuinely closer than two towns and 1 understates it, but claiming 3 would say two families are in the same place when they are not, and a fourth weight is a change to the model rather than a fix to a lookup. Measured after: parents with **zero** own-city schools 14 → 1; with **no** neighbourhood partner at all 8 → 1; whose area matches an approved record 26 → **32**; 34 of 39 gained near-area partners. ⚠ The last one is a **data** fix rather than code — `la-canada` is a second, non-starter neighborhood option for a town whose starters are all tagged `la-canada-flintridge`, so one row's `area_slug` was corrected in `market_options`; the durable place for that is the taxonomy sheet. |
| **Nine of twenty-four real questions read as no topic, and a refusal chip was becoming the heaviest edge in the graph** (9 Sep) | Four more from the matching audit, each cheap and each confirmed. **(1) `focusInQuestion` missed plurals.** `\bpreschool\b` does not match "preschool**s**" — there is no word boundary between `l` and `s` — and the same held for `school`, `park`, `nursery`, `playground`, `trail`, `library`, `museum`, `outing`, `doctor`, `dentist`, `therapist` and `club`. A list of singular nouns matched against real sentences fires about half the time. **And the order contradicted the file's own rule** that specific topics are tested before general ones: the bare `school` sat above `after ?school`, so *"after school care options"* read as `preschools_schools`, and above `sport`, so "high school sports" did too. All twelve of the audit's failing questions now resolve. **(2) The two halves of the age ladder disagreed about kindergarten** — `BAND_WORDS` said `preschool`, `bandsForAge(5)` said `grade`, so the same child described two ways filtered retrieval two different ways. It is both bands now, which is what it genuinely straddles. **(3) A refusal chip became a graph edge.** `deriveAffinities` filtered only `prefer_not_to_say`, so `none`, `homeschool`, `not_in_school_yet` and `not_doing_any_yet` were written into `social_affinities` — and `school:homeschool` is **5 points, the heaviest edge in the graph**, awarded to two families for each *not* being at a school. `faith_community:none` is live on one person, so the mechanism was real and only the volume was not. `test:feedback` now triages **every** exclusive option on a graph-writing question, which is the drift guard the flat list could not be. ⚠ `exclusive` is not the same as "a refusal" and the first version of that check conflated them: *"no reliable backup childcare"* is a real and rather important fact about a family, exclusive because it contradicts the others rather than because it declines. **(4) A shared "no preference" was scoring.** `deriveLifeRelevance` writes `across_price_points` and `no_fixed_preference` when the parent **skips** — right for the parent (1 Sep: skipping opts them into nothing), wrong for the matcher: **26 of 109** live relevance rows are those two defaults, 14 people share one of them, and two parents who skipped both collected **1.0 of the 3.0 relevance maximum for saying nothing**. They are still written, because downstream reads them; they simply score zero. ⚠⚠ **And the distinction the audit was most useful for: the rest is sparsity, not logic.** 15 answerable records, two of them junk; **6 of 13 curated topics have zero records** (nannies, babysitters, newborn_care, pediatric_health, special_needs_resources, new_to_area_help); 15 of 39 contributors can be matched at all; one school holds 10 of the 26 school edges. No ordering fix reaches those six topics — which is the same finding as 8 Sep's total silence on a health question, arriving from the other side. |
| **The blast chain runs end to end now — and 7.8 had no button at all** (8 Sep) | The client asked for the whole blast process to work and to be checked. Three links were missing, and only one of them was on the 7 Sep list. **(1) `blast.send` was unreachable from every screen.** Implemented on 27 Aug, allowlisted on 2 Sep, given six named refusals and a 409 for each on 7 Sep — and **no button anywhere called it**, so an Ask could be created and its pool previewed and then never sent. That is the written-and-never-called fault this file records four times, on the *middle* of the chain, and no test would have caught it: `test:blast` is pure and `test:e2e` asserts the action is allowlisted rather than reachable. Found by walking the page. **(2) Nothing sent the approved replies back to the asker**, which 7 Sep recorded as "the exit is not built": a parent could pay $15, five parents could answer, an admin could approve every reply, and the person who asked would hear nothing. `blast.deliver` is that exit — `lib/blast-answer.ts` composes from the replies' **own words**, and what makes forwarding free text safe is not the shape of the data but that an admin approved each one (invariant 8 forbids publishing verbatim *without human review*; `blast_response.approve` **is** that review, and only approved replies are read).
