Ranked by an admin's rating with unrated last but never dropped, whole replies dropped rather than truncated, the count taken from what fits rather than what was approved (the 4 Sep fault, avoided by composing the body before the header), no names in either direction, and `null` rather than an empty message. `drizzle/0037` is `answers_sent_at`, and it earns itself twice over: a second press is refused instead of texting the parent the same thing twice, and the card can say **"Still waiting — the answers have not gone out"**, which is 14.2's gold card one surface along. ⚠ **Deliberately not folded into `blast.fulfil`**: marking an Ask answered is a judgement with a note, delivering is a carrier round trip that can fail and be retried — one column would make a failed send look like an un-made decision. ⚠ `transactional`, so quiet hours do not apply and the asker's monthly allowance is not spent: that ceiling is about how often Pando *asks* them things, and this is them being answered. **(3) `test:relay-live` had not been re-run since the morning's auto-send change**, and was asserting a build that no longer exists — *"held for a person, because the pilot holds everything"* against an answer that is now sent automatically. Six assertions failed in a row behind a **timing** fault of the same vintage: the 8 Sep web search added 4–9s to the pipeline, the suite's 12-second settle expired, and the first thing to fail was *"the question reached the answer queue"*, which reads exactly like the wiring having come undone. It had not. Both fixed, and `settle`'s return value is now **checked**, so a timeout says so instead of producing a cascade. **The live walk is the proof, and it is honest about one thing it does not do**: it never calls `blast.send` for real, because that runs `selectPool` against the live cohort and would text real demo contributors — spending their 48-hour gap and their monthly allowance for a test. The send is asserted through its refusals, which is where its logic is; pool selection is `test:matching` (53) and the pool preview. ⚠⚠ **And the walk found a gap that is the client's to weigh, not ours to fix in a test: a sensitive health question gets total silence.** *"My 4 year old keeps having nosebleeds, is that normal?"* is classified `high_stakes` correctly and would be held correctly — but retrieval finds no record about paediatric health, `composeAnswer` returns null, and `answerQuestion` returns before anything is queued, so there is no answer, no holding line and no flag. That is 7 Sep's second gap one step worse than recorded (it said "held, and answered with nothing"; it is not even held), and the 3 Aug rule is that health/legal/safety gets professional resources **immediately** — which the web D1 flow does and the SMS path does not. |
| **The number is checked at the door now, and the client bought that with a disclosure** (8 Sep) | Her instruction, after the cost was put to her in words: *"коли заповнив базову інфу на сторінці join і натиснув на перехід — відбувається перевірка, чи є цей номер, і якщо є, то не пропускає"*. `POST /api/seed/registered` is that check and `/join` refuses on it. ⚠ **This reverses the 8 Sep decision three rows down, and the reasoning that decision rested on has *not* gone away** — it is the one place in Pando that answers a question about somebody who has proved nothing, i.e. an **enumeration oracle**: anybody can type a neighbour's number and learn whether that neighbour is in the network. This file's own words are that who is in the network *"is the asset, and it is exactly what this product does not publish"*. **What she weighed it against is real and is the harm she actually hit.** The write is `onConflictDoUpdate` on `people.phone` (invariant 10) and every derived set is **replaced rather than merged**, deliberately, so filling the form again from a second device overwrites the richer profile — and the only warning came *after* the code, i.e. after eighteen screens of work. Offered three shapes: the code moved to the front door (no disclosure, but it reverses her 13 Aug call and puts an SMS round trip before question one), this, or leaving it at the end and making *"I've joined before"* louder. She chose this. **Four rules are the whole of the mitigation, and none is decoration.** (1) *A boolean and nothing else* — no name, no standing, no neighborhood, no date; the answer says **a profile exists**, never *whose*, and a field added here to make a screen friendlier is how this becomes a directory. (2) *`profile_captured_at`, not the row* — `people` gains a row for a cold inbound text (5.9) and for anybody who verified and abandoned, so keying on the row would both turn away a stranger who once texted the number and leak a wider fact than the one asked. (3) *It fails open* — no database, a dead pooler, an unparseable number all answer `registered: false` and the parent goes on, because turning a warning into a wall on a read that did not come back takes the tool offline for people who did nothing wrong, and `afterVerified` still catches the case at the end. (4) *Its own bucket*, `phone_lookup` at 30 per ten minutes — sized like `verify_start` for a roomful of parents at a meetup rather than for an attacker, ⚠ **and its comment says plainly that this makes enumeration slow rather than impossible**: ~1,400 numbers a day from one address, and a hundred addresses do any list in an afternoon. Nothing keyed to the caller can close that; only asking for the code first could, which is exactly what this steps in front of. **It logs nothing** — not the number, not the answer (invariant 7) — and `seed_number_already_registered` carries `source` only, which is how often it fires and therefore whether the disclosure is buying anything. **The refusal is recoverable and says why.** Gold rather than red (`warning` is *pending or needs care*; `alert` never appears in the parent flow), it names the consequence rather than the state — "filling the questions in again would replace what is there" — because *this number is registered* on its own reads as being turned away, and the overwrite is the whole reason the screen exists. The way on is `/signin`, which since this morning restores the answers too, so updating a profile is still possible and is now the honest route to it. Editing the number clears the refusal, since it belongs to the number that produced it. Walked at 375px: a registered number is refused and stays on `/join`, the panel is gold with a 44px link to `/signin`, one edit clears it, an unknown number goes straight to "Where do you live?", the endpoint answers false for junk and malformed JSON, and the 30th call in ten minutes returns the limiter's sentence with `Retry-After: 545`. ⚠ The panel's wording is new user-facing copy, on the list for the client. |
| **The context step gets a bar, and the objection to it was about the reader rather than the bar** (8 Sep) | The client, pointing at the weights card: give *"Any kind of similar context"* a bar too. ⚠ **This reverses the note written the same day** — *"No bar, deliberately: the bar's denominator is the heaviest weight, and this is points per dimension with up to five stacking; 0.5 drawn against 5 would read as the weakest thing on the card, when the whole of life relevance is worth about half a shared school."* Re-read against her request, that objection is about what somebody might **infer**, not about the bar being wrong — and the card settles it in its own title. It is headed **"What one shared connection is worth"**, which is the unit every bar already draws: one of these, against the strongest one. One matching context dimension really is worth 0.5 against a school's 5, so the bar is `step / heaviest` like the other seven, it comes out a tenth, and it is the shortest on the card because that is genuinely the smallest single contribution in the model (`RELEVANCE_STEP`: *"a boost, not a second scoring system"*). Drawing the *total* instead would have made this the one bar meaning something else. **What the old note was right about goes in the footer**, where the stacking belongs: the bar answers "how much is one worth", the sentence answers "how many can there be". ⚠ **And writing that sentence is what found a stale number.** `RELEVANCE_STEP`'s own doc had said **five** dimensions since M6; there are **six** — budget · tenure · logistics · family_setup · childcare · trust_circle. The 24 Aug split promised `life_relevance` would "gain rows and never a dimension" and that held, but nobody re-counted. So the count is `RELEVANCE_DIMENSIONS` in `matching.ts` and `test:feedback` (114) asserts it equals the distinct `relevance` values the question set declares, plus that all of them together still lose to a shared school. **It has to be stated rather than computed**, and the reason is structural: the scorer reads dimensions off the rows a parent produced, so at runtime there is nothing to count until somebody has answered — and `matching.ts` deliberately has **no runtime imports** (that is what lets `test:matching` load it in plain node) while `questions.ts` imports *it* for the age ladder, so reaching back would be a cycle. A number plus a test is the only shape that keeps it in one place; a hand-maintained count on its own is what had already drifted. Walked at 1440: school 5 → 80px of an 80px track, context 0.5 → 8px, 3 → 48px, 9 clamped full and **alert-red** with Save disabled, 0.07 red at the 4% floor, and back to 0.5 green with "Nothing to save". ⚠ The read-only branch (a deployment behind `drizzle/0036`) draws `RELEVANCE_STEP`, not the empty draft — otherwise the bar would sit at the floor beside a field reading 0.5. |
| **A question Pando cannot read is asked about, not answered with silence** (8 Sep) | The client: if a parent writes something and the request cannot be classified, ask for the detail needed to classify it — and hold the exchange as one thing. `drizzle/0035` is `pending_questions`, `lib/pending-question.ts` the rules and the copy, and the branch sits where `unclear` used to fall off the end. **Silence was the old behaviour and was the worst of the three ways a message could vanish here** (recorded as a gap on 7 Sep): `unclear` is by definition the parent whose sentence did not land, and they got nothing back and no way to tell the number was alive. **A table, because the conversation is nowhere else.** `classifyIntent` has taken a `recent` array since the day it was written and **nothing ever passed one** — `message_log` deliberately holds no message bodies (invariant 7 at the schema level), so the turns cannot be reconstructed from it, and that is a property worth keeping rather than working around. Deliberately not `sms_captures` (that is a *known script with a known next step*; this is Pando not knowing what it is collecting, which is the whole reason it is asking) and not `answers` (`answer_text` is NOT NULL because that row is a promise about what will be sent). **Two rules keep it from becoming a machine that argues with somebody.** The window is **three turns** — what the model is actually shown, so a fourth would be stored and never read — and Pando asks **twice**, then hands over: a third attempt from the same classifier will not read what two could not, which is 5.4's "one refusal is a parent who did not want to answer" one step along. When it resolves, the **whole exchange** is the question: "camps" then "for a 6 year old in Altadena" is retrieved on both and stored that way, so the admin queue shows what was asked rather than the fragment that tipped the classifier over. **Three things the live walks changed, and none was visible in the code.** (1) *The CHECK meant to refuse an empty open row accepted it.* `array_length(turns, 1)` is **NULL** for an empty array, so `>= 1` evaluates to NULL and a CHECK treats NULL as satisfied — three-valued logic, and the constraint walk caught it accepting exactly the row it exists to refuse. `cardinality` instead. ⚠ 0035 was **corrected in place rather than patched by an 0036**, which is allowed here and nowhere else: it had never left this machine, so the one environment holding it was reset deterministically — the never-edit rule is about environments disagreeing silently, and there was only one. (2) *`chitchat` mid-exchange was still silence.* "hmm" was read as `unclear` and answered; the follow-up *"stuff for the kids"* came back `chitchat`, fell straight through, and the parent got nothing in the middle of a conversation Pando had started. The fix is the pipeline's own principle — **the records beat the words**, the same rule as `awaiting_blast_reply`: Pando asked one text ago, so whatever comes back is an attempt at answering. With nothing open, `chitchat` stays what it is; `contribute` is excluded even mid-exchange, because "we loved Little Gym" is a real offer. (3) *The handover promised a person and summoned nobody.* `handingOver()` says "somebody at Pando will read it and come back to you" while the row closed as `given_up` in a table no admin surface reads — a promise kept by nothing, made by the copy rather than by the code. It raises an **escalation** flag now (`unreadable_question`), which is the one severity meaning *owed a person today*, and it carries the words so the queue can be worked. Walked live twice: "hmm" → asked, "stuff for the kids" → asked differently, "any good swim classes for a 4 year old in Altadena?" → resolved, answered from the whole exchange and **sent**; and three unreadable messages → two asks, a handover, `given_up`, and an open escalation carrying "hmm yeah idk really". `test:intent` is 69. ⚠ All four strings are new user-facing copy and are on the list for the client — deliberately **not** in `sms-templates.ts`, which is registered A2P samples where a reword is a compliance event. Each is one GSM-7 segment, pinned by the suite. |
| **A single-select chip can be cleared again, and the reason it could not is spent** (8 Sep) | The client's report: on the tenure screen you tap **10+ years** or **I grew up in this area** and there is no way to take it back. ⚠ **This reverses the 3 Aug decision**, and the reversal is safe because that decision's own reason no longer exists. It read: *"tapping the chosen chip keeps it chosen. Deselecting cleared the pre-set monthly allowance on the first tap, which is the opposite of what tapping it means."* True then — the allowance was preselected at 3. On **1 Sep** the client made participation a required choice with nothing preselected, and today **every** single-select question in `EMPTY_ANSWERS` starts `null`. So the tap that rule protected cannot happen any more, while the cost it imposed is exactly what she reported. It bit hardest on `grew_up_here`, whose own comment in `questions.ts` says *"the one option toggles — which is what a checkbox is"*: it did not, so one tap recorded a parent as having grown up here permanently, on an optional question — a comment describing behaviour nothing implemented, the fault this file keeps naming. **The radio semantics are untouched, and that is why no role changed.** A radiogroup with nothing checked is legal — it is the initial state of all seven of these questions — so clearing returns it to a state it is already allowed to be in; what is non-standard is the interaction, not the state. The alternative (toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`) would have cost a screen-reader user the "2 of 5" position they get now, for no gain. **Fixed in `ChipGroup` itself, so it is every single-select question at once** rather than the two she named — and in `OptionPicker` too, where the same copied rule was a latent trap: removing a single-select chip calls `toggle`, and re-selecting was the only thing that could have done. No dropdown question is single-select today, so it had never fired. Walked at 375px: **10+ years** on → off, **I grew up in this area** on → off, `aria-checked` following both, storage cleared both times, and switching between two neighborhoods still works. |
| **Only sensitive answers wait for a person now — and the detection had to be rebuilt first** (8 Sep) | The client's instruction, and `PILOT_HOLD_EVERYTHING` is `false`. What that alone would have shipped is the finding. **Turning the flag off does nothing on its own**: `answerQuestion` had no send path at all — by design, since a branch nothing exercises is this repository's most expensive repeated fault — so the flag would have changed a recorded reason and nothing else. The send is wired, the row is marked **only when `sendSms` says the message went**, and `markAnswerSent` is deliberately not `approveAndSend`: nobody approved this one, so `reviewed_by` stays null and the queue reads as what happened. **And what decides is much weaker than it looked.** `classifyDemand` is the rule-based classifier, and over SMS it runs **without the category tap it was designed as a net underneath**. Measured against real phrasings: *"my 4 year old keeps having nosebleeds, is that normal?"* → `ordinary`, because the list carried **no medical vocabulary whatsoever**; *"is it legal to leave a 9 year old home alone?"* → `peer_support`, because "alone" is a peer-support word and nothing read "legal". While every answer waited for a person that cost nothing. With the flag off it is the thing between a health question and an unread reply. **Three layers now, and each caught something the others missed.** (1) *The word list carries symptoms and legal words* — fever, rash, vomit, seizure, allerg, medication, diagnos, legal, lawyer, police. ⚠ **Not "doctor" or "pediatrician"**: *"any good pediatricians near me?"* is a **recommendation**, the commonest question this product answers, and filing it as high-stakes would answer a request for a name with a resource list. (2) *`mentions` matches a word's **start**, not the whole word* — "nosebleeds" did not match "nosebleed", and that hole was already in the original list where nobody had looked: `abuse` missed "abused", `danger` missed "dangerous", `seizure` missed "seizures". A list of nouns matched against the singular fires on about half of real sentences. (3) *The model returns a `sensitive` boolean* on the intent call it was already making, and `escalateSensitivity` takes whichever reading is higher — so it can add caution and never remove it. ⚠ It lifts to `high_stakes` and **never** to `named_allegation`, which carries its own storage rule. ⚠⚠ **And the subtlest bug of the day was in that third layer, live for about an hour.** `applyThreshold` drops a model reading below `INTENT_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR` — correctly, the rules read intent better than a hesitant guess — and it was dropping the `sensitive` flag with it. The nosebleed sentence comes back **sensitive: true at confidence 0.35**: correctly unsure what the parent wants, correctly certain it is medical. The caution was being discarded at exactly the moment it mattered. The flag now survives the fallback. **Walked end to end three times** against the live database, watching the answers table: a nanny question holds on `caregiver`, the nosebleed question holds on `sensitive`, and a toddler-classes question is **sent automatically** with `hold_reason = not_held`. ⚠ `not_held` is a new reason value, because the no-hold branch used to return `pilot_review_all` — every automatically sent answer would have been recorded as *"held because the pilot reads everything"*. ⚠⚠ **Say this to the client plainly: no word list is complete and a model flag is probabilistic, so with automatic sending on, something sensitive will eventually reach a parent unread.** `PILOT_HOLD_EVERYTHING = true` is the one-line answer when it does, and every row already carries the reason it was or was not held, so the queue can be re-read afterwards to see what went out. |
| **`answers.share_ids` is written now, and 9.2 could not fire without it** (8 Sep) | `drizzle/0026` added the column so 9.1 could join to 9.2, and **nothing had ever written it**. Two readers: `/admin/impact`, where every row showed "Answer / no records named", and `repo/thanks.ts`, which **inner**-joins contributions on the array — so with it empty no contributor is ever found and the thank-you loop was dead for every answer the pipeline composed. Fixed by carrying the id: `AnswerCandidate` gains an `id` that is **never rendered** and is set only on records that came from `shares` — not on a caregiver, not on general information, because the array joins `shares` and an id from anywhere else would join nothing or the wrong row. `composeAnswer` returns `used_ids` for the records **actually rendered**, never the ones retrieved: an answer that mentioned two of five was built from two, and thanking the other three thanks people whose recommendation nobody saw. ⚠ That required a second change nobody would have predicted: `used` was a **count**, read back as `ranked.slice(0, used)`, which held only while the budget loop could not skip — and it now can, because a parent record too long for the reserved public line is stepped over rather than ending the run. A prefix would have named the wrong records in `labels` and counted the wrong ones in `parent_used`. The loop collects what it chose. Walked live: `share_ids` lands as 2 and 3 on two real answers. |
| **An answer carries both halves now: what is generally known, and what parents backed** (8 Sep) | The client's instruction — *"все що питає користувач, публічна інфа + підкріплення інфою від батьків"*. `lib/server/web-search.ts` looks the question up on the open web and the findings join the composer as ordinary candidates, labelled **Public/general information** and nothing else. **The labels are built in that module, not passed in**, which is invariant 3 made structural rather than remembered: a web result has no `share_contributions` behind it, so nothing could compute a parent claim for it, and `firsthand_count: 0` keeps it out of every count the composer makes about people. It never runs for a question about care (a page calling somebody a wonderful nanny has cleared none of invariants 1, 2, 12 or 13), and it drops any finding whose *name* looks like a person on 11.4's strong signals. ⚠ **On by default wherever there is an API key, and that direction was reversed the same day** (8 Sep). It shipped opt-in on `WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED=1` because the search sends the parent's question to a third party — sound about the *disclosure*, wrong about who it protected: the variable was set **nowhere**, so the feature the client asked for was built, tested, deployed and inert in every environment they could look at, and they asked twice why they could not see it. A switch that must be found before a feature exists is the written-and-never-called fault dressed as caution. `WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED=0` turns it off, the same asymmetry as `SEED_REQUIRE_VERIFICATION`, so deleting the line leaves the working setting. The disclosure stands and is on the list for the client. **Four things the live walk changed, and none was visible in review.** (1) *"Fill the gap the parents left" is a rule that can never fire.* The first cut gave public results only when fewer than two parent-backed records came back — the estimate's own framing — and it never once ran: `retrieveFor` reads no *subject*, so it always returns its best-ranked records, and a question about indoor climbing in Duarte came back with a park in Altadena. There is no gap to fill. One line is **reserved** instead. (2) *And a slot is not a reservation.* Public candidates rank last on purpose, but against a fixed budget "last" and "dropped" are the same thing: three parent records filled all 459 characters and the finding never reached a parent, so the feature existed and produced nothing. The budget is now short by the cost of the first public line while the loop is on parents, and that line is then allowed the full budget. (3) *A candidate that never renders still costs a record.* `sharedLabels` hoists a claim true of **all** candidates into one footer, and a web result carries only the public label — so its mere presence empties the shared set, every line has to repeat its own labels, and three parent records became two. Measured, then paid for deliberately: one reserved line costs roughly one parent record. (4) *`used` stopped being a prefix.* A parent record too long for the reserved budget is now skipped rather than ending the loop, so `ranked.slice(0, used)` would have named the wrong records in `labels`; the loop collects the records it chose. **`parent_used` is new and load-bearing**: every judgement downstream is about the parents' half, and without it one parent plus three pages reads as four records — Pando would stop offering to ask the network on the strength of pages it did not write, and `low_evidence` would stop summoning the reviewer that thinness exists to summon. ⚠ **The tool version and the model are one choice, not two.** `web_search_20260318` and `_20260209` carry `allowed_callers`, which Haiku 4.5 refuses outright — every call a 400, turned by the catch into *no public results*, so the answer still composed and simply never carried anything. That is the third time this repository has paid for a request the API rejects behind a fallback that hides it (`output_config` in `intent.ts` is the same shape, in the same folder), so the failure now logs the API's error **type** as well as the status — an enum, which unlike `message` cannot carry the parent's question. `web_search_20250305` works with Haiku, measured with and without `user_location`. Walked live: *"is there anywhere in Glendora that does toddler pottery?"* came back with two parent-backed classes and **"Color Me Mine in Glendora. Public/general information."** `test:answer` is 65 — the parser is `lib/public-info.ts`, split out so a plain node test can load it, with the person check a **required argument** rather than an import, because that is what keeps the module free of runtime imports and what stops a call site forgetting it. ⚠ **What this cannot fix, and the client should hear it:** with 23 records in the market the parents' half of an answer is frequently not about what was asked. General information makes the answer more relevant and leaves that untouched. |
| **Why every answer is in the admin queue, and why the reason on all ten was wrong** (8 Sep) | Two different questions, and the screen answers neither. **Everything is held because `PILOT_HOLD_EVERYTHING` is `true`** — the blanket rule of §19, a named constant meant to come off, and until it does `routeAnswer` ends by holding whatever the specific rules let through. Nothing is auto-sent today by design. **But every stored answer said `caregiver`**, which is a *permanent* reason, including on *"any good toddler classes near South Pasadena?"* — so the queue read as though none of it would ever send automatically. Measured rather than assumed: all ten rows are from **4 September**, before that day's fixes, and re-running the pipeline today gives `pilot_review_all` for the classes question and `caregiver` only for the nanny one. The model half of 5.3 is doing its job — "toddler classes" comes back `ask_recommendation` at 0.95, "any good nannies near Altadena?" `ask_caregiver` at 0.95 — so the classification is not broken and the visible evidence is stale test data. ⚠ **Turning the blanket rule off is a product decision, not a tuning change**, and the specific rules are already written for that day: sensitive, caregiver-related and "the generator asked" are permanent, public-only and thin are not. ⚠ **And one real bug found while looking: `answers.share_ids` is written by nothing.** It is read in two places — `/admin/impact` and `repo/thanks.ts` — and `drizzle/0026` added it specifically so 9.1 could join to 9.2. The thank-you query is an **inner** join on it, so with the column empty **no contributor is ever found and 9.2 cannot fire at all** for an answer the pipeline composed; the impact page shows every row headed "Answer / no records named". Not fixed here, because it was not what was asked. |
| **The circles questions are a dropdown with search now, and what stayed a button is the answer** (7 Sep) | ⚠ **Schools came back out on 8 Sep**, on the client pointing at that screen: *"на цій сторінці не потрібні були dropdown"*. Her original instruction named "local groups, schools, faith communities", which reads as three questions and is two **screens** — only one of them is the circles page. The schools screen keeps its chips, and the reason is its own: the starters are already trimmed to the parent's area (eight per area, her curation), so what is on offer is a short familiar list rather than the wall of hundreds a dropdown is for — and on a per-child screen each block would otherwise be a closed box a parent must open before they can tell whether Pando knows their school at all. So the dropdown is the four questions on the circles page and nothing else. The developer's instruction: on the local-groups, schools and faith-communities screens, the option buttons become a dropdown you can search. `components/ui/OptionPicker.tsx` is that control and `ChipGroup` keeps everything else, because the split is a property of the options rather than a preference: a short closed set a parent is meant to read whole stays chips, a directory of hundreds becomes a box. **The chips that remain are the ones that carry an answer.** A dropdown hides what is *not* chosen, which is the point; hiding what *is* chosen would leave a parent unable to see or undo their own answer until the review screen seventeen questions later — so selections sit above the box as removable green chips, and `CustomChip` gained a `tone` rather than being copied (gold still means *pending*, i.e. a typed answer awaiting promotion under invariant 9). ⚠ **`wholeList` deliberately keeps its chips**, and undoing that reintroduces a bug she has already reported: the seventeen approved towns are hers to be read whole, and on 1 Sep five of them were invisible because the list was trimmed. Static questions are untouched — the tenure bands on the same screen as *previous places* still render as chips beside a dropdown, which is the rule being visible rather than an inconsistency. **Camps is in, and not through `SearchableChipGroup`.** It has no directory behind it, so it takes `OptionPicker` directly with local filtering only: routing it through the searchable wrapper would also have applied the area trim and the twelve-item cap, which camps has never had — this changes how options are presented, never which are offered. Leaving it as the one wall of buttons among three dropdowns on one page is the "some do, some don't" the client already reported once on the admin. **Two faults found by measuring rather than reading, both fixed.** *The list opened underneath the dock*: a flow screen's dock is `sticky z-30`, so a list dropped below a box near the foot of the page was painted over — three of thirteen options on screen and unreachable. The dock is `data-screen-dock` now, the picker measures it as a ceiling, flips above when what is left below is not worth reading, and clamps its height to the space. *And the first clamp was on the wrong element*: `max-height` on the `<ul>` left the "Can't find it?" footer hanging past the ceiling, so the list ended 5px clear and the panel still covered Continue — it belongs on the panel, with the list flexing inside. ⚠ A dock **above** the box is not a ceiling: above `md` it stops being sticky and sits in the flow, and treating that as the ceiling would flip a list that had a whole screen beneath it. Walked at 375×812: four boxes, two placed below and two flipped, none overlapping the dock; a remote-only record ("The Little Gym", found by search and in no starter list) selected, stored as a slug, and still named on the chip *and* in its per-selection heading after a reload — which is `registerFoundOptions` and the resolve-by-id effect surviving the refactor. Per-child attribution still lands per child (`child_of.schools` → the 2017 child), area-first ranking still holds (Pasadena schools, then the top-up), and the schools screen is 812px where it used to scroll. ⚠ **Two things to put to the client.** The question's own furniture — "Homeschool", "Not in school or daycare yet" — now sits at the **bottom of a scrolling list** rather than in a wrapped grid, because `visibleStarters` returns it last and this is the same order; it is a refusal-adjacent answer and may want pinning to the top. And the box's placeholder and its "Nothing matching …" / "Start typing to search." lines are new user-facing copy. |
| **Quiet hours do not apply to the relay, and that is a narrowing rather than a hole** (7 Sep) | The developer could not test sending: §14's window is 8am–9pm **Pacific**, which from Kyiv is 18:00–07:00, so their whole working day is inside it. The wrong fixes were both available — a switch that turns the rule off, or an env var that moves its timezone — and each is dangerous in the same way: left set in production, one texts parents at 3am and the other does it while looking correct. **What the rule is actually for decides it.** Quiet hours exist so a phone does not buzz on somebody's nightstand at midnight. A post in the Slack test channel wakes nobody, and its "recipients" are demo rows. So the exemption is the **transport**, not a flag: `quietHoursBlocks` returns false when the transport is `slack`, and it cannot leak into production because production has no relay — and a deployment that had one would be posting every parent's message into a Slack channel, which is a far louder failure than the hour. ⚠ **This narrows CLAUDE.md's own "the relay is a swap of the provider step only", deliberately and only here.** Opt-out, the 48-hour gap, the monthly ceiling and the response governor all still run on the relay untouched, because each answers *may this person be contacted at all* and each must be exercised by the testing the relay exists for. Quiet hours is the one rule that is purely about the clock on the wall, and the relay has no wall. **The rule is pure and the clock is not**, which is what makes it testable: `isQuietHours()` needs a clock, `quietHoursBlocks({category, inReplyTo, quiet, transport})` does not, so all four inputs are checked exhaustively rather than at whatever hour the suite runs (`test:outreach` 57). Every exemption is **logged** — a rule that stops applying silently is how it stops applying somewhere it should not. **And the admin says so now, in two places, because it said nothing anywhere.** `/admin/blasts` carries the Pacific clock and whether sending is open — gold only when a send would really be refused, neutral when the relay means it will go through, so the banner cannot contradict the send layer. `/admin/delivery` says outright that messages are reaching a Slack channel rather than phones, which is the one thing a page reporting a delivery *rate* has to say before any number on it means anything — the same rule that page already applies to Stripe. Both read one new field, `DeliveryHealthRow.relay`. Walked live at **00:20 Pacific**: the send logged "quiet hours ignored — relay" for each of five recipients and carried on to the provider step, where the one-off harness's stub was unreachable — `test:relay-live` (45) posts through that same path successfully. |
| **M5–M8 walked end to end: four gaps, and quiet hours is on screen now** (7 Sep) | A call-graph pass over every exported function in M5–M8 plus the ten suites (intent 31 · answer 48 · routing 36 · onboarding 38 · trust 47 · matching 53 · blast 50 · outreach 50 · inbound 49 · compliance 26). **Everything is reachable** — the "written and never called" fault that has cost this repo four times does not appear in M5, M6 or M8. Four things do. **(1) An intent that is not a question gets silence.** `inbound.ts` returns unless the reading is `ask_recommendation` or `ask_caregiver`, so `unclear`, `chitchat` and a free-form `contribute` ("we loved Little Gym", which is not a capture start) produce no reply at all — while 5.3's own design says `unclear` and `chitchat` are different answers *because* the first is routed to a person, and 5.9's whole subject is a stranger never meeting silence. Nothing routes it. **(2) A sensitive question over SMS is held, and answered with nothing.** `classifyDemand` runs and `routeAnswer` holds it permanently, which is right; but the 3 Aug rule is that health/legal/safety gets **professional resources immediately**, and the web D1 flow does that while the SMS path sends the generic "somebody will look". The higher-stakes channel is the one giving less. **(3) An SMS question never becomes a demand signal.** `demand_signals` is written by the Phase 1 completion route and the demo seed, and by nothing else — so §9's market-expansion number and `/admin/demand` do not grow from the channel that *is* the product, and every question asked by text is invisible there. **(4) 6.5's hard requirements are a parameter nothing passes.** `selectPool`'s `mustHave` is read and honoured; neither caller sets it, so a stacked requirement cannot be expressed from any surface — benign today (they are "rare, and never inferred") and the same shape as the four faults above it. **And the practical one, found by misdiagnosing my own test:** a blast send that reached nobody reported "no provider configured", when the real cause was that it was **23:53 in Pasadena**. §14's window is 8am–9pm *Pacific* — the parent's clock — which for anybody testing from Europe covers their whole working day, and the failure it produces looks exactly like a broken pool: every recipient skipped, the Ask marked for review. Nothing in the admin said so. `/admin/blasts` now carries the Pacific clock and whether sending is open, gold when it is not, and the refusal sentence names quiet hours first. Rendered after mount only — a clock computed on the server and again in the browser is a hydration mismatch by construction. |
| **M7 has an entry now, and every refusal says which one it is** (7 Sep) | The developer asked why the row had no way in and how to test it. **Why: the estimate is row-shaped, and the connectors between rows are nobody's row.** 7.1–7.11 were each built and each reachable from the admin, except the one that begins the chain — and `7.4` and `7.10` appear **nowhere in this repository**, in no document and no comment, so whatever the workbook calls them was never reconciled. **`blast.create` is the entry, and it is deliberately the manual one**, because that is how the rest of the pilot already works: an answer waits for a person (5.8), a claim is matched by hand, a question arrives as a demand signal. It records the Ask and redeems a credit; it does not send, does not charge and does not pick the pool. ⚠ **The automatic path is still a decision** — a thin answer already ends with *"Want me to ask a few nearby parents for more?"* and nothing reads the yes; wiring it means saying what that reply costs. **The insert is `createBlastIn`, not a copy**: extracted so the action writes the blast and its audit row in one transaction, and because two writers of the credit redemption is the half that would quietly stop matching its tier. **Three faults the entry exposed, none of which existed while nothing could create an Ask.** (1) *A paid Ask was born saying "no payment required"* — the column default, harmless until now, and `/admin/payments` exists to answer what is owed; `paymentFor` sets it at creation, and `price_cents` still waits for checkout because it records what was actually charged. (2) *Every send refusal read as "not implemented".* `sendBlast` distinguishes six outcomes and `blast.send` collapsed all of them to `not_found`, writing the real one to a container log; an admin pressing Send on an unpaid Ask was told the row had changed. Each has its own sentence and a 409 now — unpaid, waiting for a person, already sent, contacts nobody, nothing went out. (3) *A **passive** entry marked itself for review*: it contacts nobody by design (7.11), so the send found an empty pool, counted nought and flagged the Ask — a question doing exactly what its tier promises, reported as needing a human. Refused before anything is written. **And `not_found` itself stopped meaning "not implemented"** for every action: nearly every write here is a conditional UPDATE (11 Aug), so a row that moved under the screen is the commonest refusal in the admin, and telling somebody a button they use daily is unbuilt sends them to report a bug. Walked live against the real database: created draft/5/48h with the neighborhood read from the profile, a credit redeemed and marked spent in the same transaction, `last_minute` self-flagging for review, the pool preview choosing five with reasons, and all five refusals in words. |
| **A child on the way was still asked about, and the test's own name said otherwise** (7 Sep) | The 3 Sep round fixed the **mixed** family — `answerableChildren` keeps an unborn child out of the attribution chips, the repeated blocks and the "same for all children" shortcut — and left the family whose **first** is on the way being asked all of it. Measured rather than read: a profile with `child_ages: [-1]` reached fifteen screens carrying three per-child questions, and the schools one read *"Where does your child go to school, preschool or daycare?"* over two options ("Homeschool", "Not in school or daycare yet") — a screen about a child who has not been born, immediately after the parent said so. **Why it survived, and it is the recurring shape:** `childBlocks` returns nothing for a one-child family *by design* (there is nothing to attribute, so the caller renders the ordinary household list), and the test asserted exactly that under the name **"a parent expecting their first is asked no per-child question either"**. The assertion was true and the sentence was false. ⚠ **`showForBands` cannot express this, and the comment on the schools question claimed it could:** `bandsForAge(EXPECTING)` returns **`["expecting", "baby"]`** deliberately — a family about to be in the baby band is who they most want to hear from — so every band list admitting a baby admits an expecting parent. Narrowing those lists would break matching to fix a screen. The gate is one clause in `isQuestionVisible`: a `perChild` question is not asked when `child_ages` is answered and holds no born child, so `visibleScreens` drops the whole screen rather than rendering an empty one. **Only when they have answered** — an empty `child_ages` is somebody who has not reached the ages screen, and hiding half the flow from them is the same mistake inverted. Expecting-only now walks **thirteen** screens with no per-child question on any of them; one born child brings all four back. `test:feedback` is 104 and asserts the screens rather than the blocks. |
| **The guarantee was paying twice, and minting credits for Asks nobody paid for** (7 Sep) | Found by reviewing M7 end to end, and confirmed by running `expire_blasts` against the live database: of four expired Asks it credited **three**. The rule was "any priced tier", and it was wrong in two directions at once. **(1) A card-paid Ask got a credit *and* a refund.** `refundOwed` reports money owed on the payments page while the job quietly granted a credit for the same failure — two currencies spent on one promise. **(2) An Ask whose checkout never completed minted a free credit.** `createBlast` starts a paid tier at `payment_status = 'pending'`, and nothing in the job looked at it, so a Board Ask created, never paid, never sent and left to expire produced a `targeted_network_ask` credit — repeatable by anybody willing to create an Ask and not pay. Nothing was taken, so nothing is owed. `automaticCredit` in `blast-tiers.ts` is the rule now, beside `owedRefund` **on purpose**: the two halves of one guarantee have to be readable next to each other or they overlap again, and `test:blast` asserts across every tier and payment state that they never both fire. Re-run live afterwards: six expired Asks, **one** credit, the credit-funded one. **And the credit half was invisible, which is the second half of the same story.** `credits` carries a person, a kind and a reason and **no reference back to the Ask**, so `refundOwed` could only ever answer "a credit is owed" — the blast manager painted that in alert red for ever on a promise the job had already kept, and the obvious response is to grant a second by hand. That is the 3 Sep clock bug one step later: the guarantee is a state with a beginning and an end, and the page could see only the beginning. `drizzle/0033` adds `blasts.credit_granted_at`, stamped in the same transaction as the credit; ⚠ **null keeps its plain meaning — not granted** — which matters in the direction nobody looks: a deployment whose cron is not wired grants nothing, so a null has to keep reading as owed rather than as done. The row now says "granted automatically when the window closed" in neutral type, because *the guarantee was owed and has been met* is a different answer from *nothing was ever owed*. |
| **M7 has no way in and no way out, and both are the client's call** (7 Sep) | The review's finding, recorded rather than fixed because each end is a product decision. **Nothing calls `createBlast`.** Grepped: no route, no admin action, no job — only `test:blast` and `seed-blasts.mjs`, which inserts rows directly. So a Network Ask cannot be created by anybody, and `/admin/blasts` manages Asks only a seed script can produce. **And Pando already offers one.** A thin answer ends with *"Want me to ask a few nearby parents for more?"* (5.7, stored as `answers.next_step = 'offer_blast'`) — the parent replies **yes** and nothing reads it: not the pipeline, not the queue, not a person. The one promise in the product that the code cannot keep at all. **Nothing sends the replies back to the asker either.** `blast_response.approve` writes the reply into the graph and records the impact; `blast.fulfil` sets a status and a note; neither addresses the asker, and the only outbound path to a parent is `answer.send` from `/admin/answers`, whose rows are created by the *inbound* pipeline. So a parent could pay $15, five parents could answer, an admin could approve every one — and the asker would hear nothing. **Three smaller ones, measured.** *PASS frees the seat and nobody takes it*: `passed_at` is set and the copy says the question "moves to someone else immediately", but nothing tops the pool up and `sendBlast` refuses a blast that has already sent. *The blast request is four segments where two would do* — `planSegments` on the real template: 241 slots, UCS-2, **4 segments**, offender `—`; the identical text with a hyphen is **2**. That is five recipients paying double, and it is not ours to change: registered A2P copy, and the 27 Aug note already says the wording must be checked against the registered sample. *The Stripe checkout URL is returned to the admin* with nothing sending it to the parent, which is consistent with a hand-run pilot and worth saying out loud before the first paid Ask. What **is** sound: `selectPool` (matcher → opt-out in the WHERE → M8 rules, asker excluded from his own pool), the expiry clock, idempotent payments, and the send path's four refusals. |
| **The numbers are behind a button, and every queue reveals in thirty** (4 Sep) | The developer asked for the Overview's statistics folded away and for **every** page reviewed rather than that one. Both, and the second is where the finding was. **The fold.** `<details>` rather than state, for the reasons `Explainer` uses it — it works before hydration, it is reachable by keyboard and by find-in-page, and it needs nothing remembered. **Closed on every visit deliberately:** a fold that remembers being open is a page that is long again tomorrow. Deleting the nine sections was the other option and was not ours to take — only the client knows whether she reads the funnel. Measured at 1440×900: `/admin` is **900 pixels**, exactly one screen, worklist and nothing else, which is what the 19 Aug decision asked for and had stopped delivering. **The review of the rest found something text could never have fixed: the queues render every row they hold.** `/admin/audit` renders 500 at once. `useReveal` shows the first thirty and puts the rest behind a button that says how many — *"Show the other 470"* — because the number is what a reader decides on: seventeen more is a scroll, four hundred and seventy is a different plan for the afternoon. ⚠ **This is not the paging the 4 Sep audit decision declined**, and the distinction is what makes it allowed: that was about *server* paging, an offset and a stable sort key, a read-layer change. Every row here is already fetched and in memory; only how many reach the document changes. Two rules in the hook: **it never hides the last few** (a list within `slack` of the limit renders whole, or a queue of twenty-eight shows twenty-five and a button), and **it is one-way** — somebody who opened a long queue is working it, and taking the rows back is the page moving under them. **Applied to all thirteen queues that can grow** — audit, demand, activities, caregivers, contributors, answers, impact, freshness, conversations, claims, options, payments and blasts — rather than to the four that are long today, because a queue is short until the pilot fills it and the one that gets forgotten is the one nobody was watching. ⚠ Twelve of the thirteen are therefore **inert right now**, and that is the change being correct rather than wasted: only audit is over the threshold, where the button reads *"Show the other 470"* and opening it goes 2,534px → 31,726px, all 500 rows. Walked in a browser at 1440×900: every one of the thirteen renders, and the one live button reveals and then removes itself. **⚠⚠ And the correction that matters most for the next session: the first round of measurements in this turn was taken in a Browser pane with `innerWidth: 0`.** Every height was inflated several-fold (audit "42,055px", flags "17,701px", the Overview's "4477px" recorded above), the ordering between pages was wrong, and *nothing about the numbers looked suspicious* — they were internally consistent and told a plausible story. It surfaced only because a card measured **390px tall and 0px wide**. Real figures at 1440×900: flags 4952 · demand 4508 · activities 3578 · caregivers 3236 · audit 2534 · contributors 2290 · founding 1945 · overview 900. **Check `window.innerWidth` before believing a layout measurement from that pane.** |
| **The admin's chrome was already lean; the Overview is ten screens tall** (4 Sep) | The developer asked for the hints and the excess text stripped from every admin page. Measured first, and **two of the three premises did not hold** — which is worth recording, because the obvious next session will reach for the same three. **(a) The hints are not what makes a page dense.** A `Hint` is a collapsed 24px trigger; until it is pressed it occupies almost nothing. Removing all 59 would take information out of a tool without changing how any page looks — and several are the only place a fact is stated anywhere: the difference between the reward threshold and the Founding threshold, that `invites.opens` is not a headcount, that opening a restricted note is logged against the reader's name. **Eight were removed and five came back on the read-through**, which is the part worth recording: the sweep took out the very hint this row names as untouchable — *"Opening it is recorded against your name"*, on the menu item that opens a restricted note — so the paragraph and the code disagreed, and the paragraph was right. Back with it: whose willingness a caregiver's *Reference* row reports (the code comment above it says in so many words that it is the one fact there not about the caregiver), what the contributors table's *Shared* column counts, and that *Joined* on the invites table is people rather than opens — which had lost its hint while the columns either side of it kept theirs. **Three removals stand**, all of them restating the label they hang off: "Already returned" under a column headed *Refunded*, "This family used it themselves" under *Firsthand*, and a field help under a field labelled *Group name*. That is the 4 Sep triage applied to rows that survived it, not a reversal of it — and the general lesson is that a sweep is where a documented exception gets swept. **(b) There are not eighty loose paragraphs.** That count came from grepping `text-muted`, and most of those are **data** — masked numbers, dates, counts. The real always-visible prose is one intro sentence per page. Eight were trimmed on one rule: a sentence naming a constraint the reader would otherwise walk into stays, a sentence explaining *why* goes. **(c) What the screenshot was actually showing is the worklist**, and one thing in it was a genuine duplication: every row named its destination in words — "13 contributors to confirm → **Founding queue**" — two inches from a sidebar reading "Founding queue 13". Nine repetitions on the one screen meant to be read at a glance. The code carried a reason for it (a new admin could not tell "caregivers held for you" from "caregiver sign-ups to match") and those are two distinct sentences, each a link; it is an arrow now. **And the measurement that matters, which nobody had taken: `/admin` was ten sections tall, nine of them statistics.** ⚠ The first figure recorded here — "4477 pixels" — was **wrong, and wrong in a way worth knowing about**: it came from the Browser pane with `innerWidth: 0`, where every line wraps to one character and `main` is 32px across, so every height was inflated several-fold. Re-measured at 1440×900, the numbers are five times smaller and the *ordering* changed too. **Check `window.innerWidth` before trusting a layout measurement from that pane** — it reports 0 when the pane is collapsed, and nothing about the result looks suspicious. That directly contradicts its own 19 Aug decision — *"an admin arrives asking 'is there anything for me', so the overview answers that and nothing else"* — which removed exactly such a grid. It grew back by accretion, one section at a time, and no single addition looked wrong. ⚠ **Not fixed here, deliberately:** cutting nine blocks of statistics is a decision about *content* rather than chrome, and only the client knows whether she reads "WHERE PEOPLE STOP". The three shapes it could take — worklist plus four headline numbers, the rest behind one toggle, or a separate `/admin/numbers` — are on the list for her. |
| **A caregiver answered a question about classes, and the trust chain was half the message** (4 Sep) | Two findings from one screenshot the client sent, and the first is the one that mattered. **(1) A named person was surfaced to somebody who did not ask for one.** The retrieval gate narrowed the *shares* half and left `caregivers: true` unconditional — half a fix, and the wrong half — so a question about toddler classes came back naming **Elena V.** beside two music classes. It passed invariant 1 the whole time (she consented and is discoverable), which is exactly why it looked fine, and it spent the slot a second class should have had. Gated on **two signals** now, because each catches what the other misses: the word list is blunt and generous and does not read "who can look after my toddler on Tuesdays", while `focusInQuestion` maps that onto `nannies`/`babysitters`/`newborn_care`. **(2) The trust chain repeated verbatim on every line** — 70 characters a record, roughly half the message saying one thing three times. The labels are approved copy and shortening the *wording* is a re-approval, so what was shortened is the **repetition**: a label every record carries is printed once about the set. Nothing is reworded and no record loses a claim. **Three rules keep it honest.** *Only labels every record has* — which is what makes the hoisted line true of each, and the mixed case is the interesting one rather than an edge: "Validated by multiple parents" on one record beside "Shared by a local parent" on another stays **on the lines**, because hoisting what differs would tell a reader both were equally backed. *Computed over every ranked candidate, not only the ones that fit* — dropping a record can only shrink the shared set, never grow it, so a hoisted label is still true of whatever survives the budget; getting that backwards would put a claim in a footer about a record nobody was shown. *A single record keeps its own labels and gets no footer*, because a shared claim about one thing has nothing to share it with. **What it buys: three records where two fit**, same budget, same segments — and it makes the pending question to the client *smaller*, since the space they would have freed by cutting "Human-reviewed" is already free. **And a caregiver's line finally says something.** It read "Elena V. - caregiver", which is nothing the record's presence in an answer about care had not already said, while `CaregiverCandidate` held the kind of care and the areas served and the mapping dropped both — the same fault as the shares' neighborhood, one type along. The kind of care *replaces* the bare word rather than joining it, and **one** area is rendered, not three: a list reads as a service radius and costs the space a second candidate needs. A caregiver with no profile of their own falls back to the plain word. `test:answer` is 48, `test:relay-live` 45 and asserts against the caregivers table rather than a name, so it does not break the day the cohort changes. |
| **The subject taxonomy existed and nothing read it — the model tags records now, and the tag ranks rather than filters** (4 Sep) | Retrieval could not tell "toddler swim classes" from "toddler music classes", and the fix was not the embeddings everyone assumes. **`market_options.focus` already holds thirteen curated topics** for this market (activities · arts_music · sports · camps · outings · nannies · babysitters · newborn_care · pediatric_health · preschools_schools · special_needs_resources · working_parent_logistics · new_to_area_help) — and `grep '"focus"'` over the whole repository returned **nothing**. It is what a parent is *asked* about in P11/P12 and it was never attached to a record. So the work was three pieces and only one was code worth writing first: tag the records, read the topic out of a question, rank on it. **Who assigns the tag is the developer's call, made 4 Sep with the cost read back to them** — *"дешево, і помиляється так, що ніхто не помітить"* — so it is the extraction model, in the **same call** that already scores the card: no extra request, no extra money. Three things make that bargain survivable. **(1) The tag ranks and never filters.** This is the whole mitigation. A wrong topic re-orders an answer; a wrong topic that *excluded* would drop the right record and leave nothing on screen saying so, which is precisely the unnoticed failure being accepted. Same rule the chip list already applies to the asker's area. **(2) "none" is a real answer and the schema says so** — the largest source of unnoticed error is forcing a pick, and a model given thirteen options and no way out returns the nearest one for a record belonging to none. **(3) The value is written only into an empty column.** A share collects many contributions, so this runs again for each one, and a second opinion overwriting the first would make the tag depend on who wrote last. **Measured, not estimated:** run against all ten seeded records the model got **nine right**, the miss being a library tagged `activities` rather than `outings`. The full backfill of 23 records tagged 22, declined 1 ("Test"), and had **zero** answers outside the vocabulary — validated against `market_options` on the way in, so an invented value is dropped rather than stored. Debatable calls that stand: a museum's classes and a gym as `activities` rather than `outings`/`sports`, and a record literally called "Untitled" getting `working_parent_logistics`, which is the forced-pick failure showing through the instruction not to. **Why the backfill is a script and not the sweep:** `sweepExtraction` selects on `confidence is null`, so every already-scored record would have been skipped and the feature would have applied only to future cards — the "written and never fires" fault, avoided by checking. Widening that condition was the obvious fix and is wrong, because `focus` is legitimately null for a declined record and those rows would be re-swept and re-scored for ever. `npm run focus:backfill` follows `options:import`'s shape: a diff first, `--commit` before anything is written. ⚠ **There is no admin control for this column, and three comments claimed there was** until that was caught — `--retag` is the correction path today, and a control on the contributions queue, where a reviewer already has the record open, is the gap to close. **The question half** is `focusInQuestion`, thirteen word lists, specific before general so "swim class" is sport rather than the generic bucket, and every answer checked against what the market actually offers — a market with no `sports` still reads "swim classes" as a class rather than returning nothing. **And there is no CHECK on the column, deliberately:** the vocabulary lives in `market_options`, which an admin edits (12 Aug), so a constraint here would be a second copy of a list this codebase does not own. **Two documented traps, both hit:** a backtick in a SQL comment closes the `sql` template (fourth time), and a drizzle journal entry whose `when` predates the previous migration is silently treated as already applied — `0032` reported "up to date" twice before that turned up. |
| **An answer says what a thing is, where, and what it costs — and the budget went to three segments** (4 Sep) | The client read the first real answer and said *"якесь дуже скромне… не дуже пояснює і заохочує"*, which was right, and the fix needed measuring rather than rewording. **What was available and unused:** `price_band` (17/19 approved contributions), `price_unit` (14/19), `worth_it` (17/19), `last_there` (19/19), `how_much` (15/19) — all **taps**, all structured, none of them reaching the answer. So the line now carries the price and the worth-it judgement beside the kind and the neighborhood. ⚠ **The free-text boundary is untouched and is why this can never read like a friend texting:** `AnswerCandidate` has no free-text field at all, by construction (11.4), so a parent's own sentence structurally cannot reach another parent. Everything added here is a tap. **The aggregation rule is agreement, not the average**, and it is the same for both fields: two parents saying `50_100` and one saying `100_200` is not a range Pando may state, and a modal value would silently discard a real disagreement about money. ⚠ **Measured before committing to it, because the coverage is the finding:** parents agree on the price for **11 of 13** records and on worth for **8 of 13** — and of the five records with more than one contribution, only two agree on worth. So worth shows mostly on single-parent records, which are exactly the ones whose *trust* labels are weakest. The disagreements are shades ("fair" against "great value"), which is an argument for a modal value and a better argument against one: enthusiasm is what a reader takes from the word. **A negative consensus is kept** — an answer that reports only the good ones is an advertisement. **The price is rendered by the caller, not the composer**, because `answer.ts` imports nothing at runtime so a plain-node test can load it, while the words for `50_100` live in the option lists `seed-chat/scripts.ts` already exports; passing ids would put a second copy of those labels in the composer. It goes through `toGsm7` on the way — the label is "$50–100" with an **en dash**, one character outside GSM-7, which would drop the whole message to UCS-2 and undo the encoding fix for a dash nobody would miss. That is `toGsm7` used exactly as its own header intends: on copy that is *not* registered. **And `SMS_BUDGET` went 306 → 459**, on the client's instruction after being told what the number was. Read this before changing it back: 306 was **never a client decision**. It was set in code with 5.7 (`968447b`) on the arithmetic plus *"the strategy's own example answer is about that long"* — an inference from an example, with **no Decisions row**, so it looked settled only because it was a named constant. ⚠ **The strategy document is not in the repo and nobody here can check that example**; if it is two segments, this is a deviation from her intent and is on the list for her. What forced it: with the record now saying what it is and what it costs, two no longer fit, and a question about classes came back naming **one** — an answer that offers no comparison is one the parent follows with a Network Ask, so the second message is spent either way. Cost is roughly +50% per answer, and `test:answer` pins that the budget stays a whole number of segments (153 × 3), because a budget between two wastes one. **Still open and still the client's:** the trust chain repeats verbatim on every line — **140 of 323 characters** in a two-record answer, 43% of the message saying the same thing twice — and shortening it is a re-approval under invariant 3, not a copy edit. |
| **The first real answer named a trail in reply to "toddler classes", and read like a database dump** (4 Sep) | The client's own reaction — *"якесь дуже скромне"* — and both halves of that instinct were right. The question was **"any good toddler classes near South Pasadena?"**; the answer named **Little Maestros** (a class, in South Pasadena, for toddlers — right on all three axes) and **Hahamongna Watershed Park** (a trail, in Altadena, for preschool and up — wrong on all three), each wearing the identical trust chain, so the wrong one read as endorsed as the right one. **Three separate faults.** **(1) The bands came only from the asker's children**, and a cold number has none — so no band filter applied at all and the composer padded to its 306-character budget with whatever ranked next. `bandsInQuestion` reads the age out of the **question** now and wins over the profile when both exist, because a parent who names an age is asking about *that* child: a mother of a toddler and a teenager asking about toddler classes does not want the teenager's options mixed in. Band **names** are safe to read where a *kind* is not — "toddler" means exactly one thing in this taxonomy, while a word list mapping "birthday party venues" to `place` would exclude the record of that name, which is a `tip`. A bare number goes through `bandsForAge`, anchored on the noun so a price or a house number cannot be read as a child. **(2) The line never said what the thing *was*.** `name (venue): labels` tells a parent who does not already know what Little Maestros is precisely nothing — and the identical chain on the next line is what made it look machine-generated. It carries the kind in a word a parent would use (`activity` → "class", because nobody says activity) and the neighborhood, both structured fields the record already held and the mapping was dropping. ⚠ **The labels are untouched and must stay so** — approved copy, verbatim, invariant 3; what changed is everything around them. The cost is named rather than hidden: ~20 characters a record, so a long answer fits one record fewer, which is the right trade against naming three things nobody can identify. ⚠ **And the free-text boundary is unchanged**: `AnswerCandidate` still has **no** free-text field, by construction (11.4), so a parent's own words can never reach another parent — which is *why* an answer reads more like a list than like a friend texting, and that is an invariant rather than a style choice. **(3) It was five segments.** The label join was ` · ` and one character outside GSM-7 drops the whole message to UCS-2 and the budget from 160 to 70. Measured on one rendered record: **130 chars / UCS-2 / two segments** with the interpunct, **128 / GSM-7 / one** without — and *both* the interpunct and the em dash had to go, because either alone keeps it in UCS-2. The full two-record answer went **5 segments → 2**. The design system's "em dashes are fine" is a rule about screens; `sms-segments.ts` exists for exactly this argument on the other side, and `test:answer` now pins the encoding so a future edit cannot quietly triple the bill. **What is still not fixed, and is the client's call:** retrieval reads no *subject*, so "toddler swim classes" and "toddler music classes" return the same activities — narrowed now by band, area and care-versus-not, and no further. And every line repeats the full trust chain, which is correct and verbose; shortening it is a re-approval, not a copy edit. `test:relay-live` is 44 and asserts against the table rather than against two record names, so it does not break the day one is retired. |
| **A 200 that arrives late is a non-2xx, and Slack was retrying every question** (4 Sep) | Reported as *"іноді повідомлення подвоюються"*, and `message_log` shows it exactly: one message from `+16265559999` logged **twice, 3.3 seconds apart**, then two *different* replies — `clarify_child_age` from the first pass and `answer_queued` from the retry, because by then the first pass had written the clarify row that `pendingClarification` reads. **Slack allows three seconds.** The route `await`ed the whole pipeline before answering, which was fine while that pipeline was a keyword lookup and a post and stopped being fine the day it grew a model call, a retrieval query, a queue write and a send: measured in `message_log`, **4.8 to 6.3 seconds** from inbound to reply. So Slack retried, and everything ran twice. **Two defences, because either alone leaves a hole.** `after()` runs the pipeline once the response is out, so the ack is milliseconds and no retry is provoked — Next's own mechanism rather than a floating promise racing the request's teardown; and `x-slack-retry-num` is acked and **dropped**, because a cold start can still cross three seconds and Slack saying "I already sent you this" cannot be improved on by processing it again while the first attempt is still running. The header is read **after** the signature: an unsigned header is a claim, not a fact. **`/api/sms/inbound` got the same shape**, which is the whole point of there being one `handleInboundMessage` — Twilio is more forgiving (fifteen seconds, and a timeout is an error rather than a duplicate) so it was not where this surfaced, but a pipeline that makes a model call should not be holding open the response that tells the carrier the text arrived. ⚠ **What it changes, worth knowing rather than discovering:** two messages arriving within a second are now processed **concurrently** rather than serialised by the response. That race always existed — Slack delivers events in parallel — but it is wider now. **And it broke the live walk in a way worth keeping:** every fixed `setTimeout` in `test:relay-live` became a race the suite loses on a slow query and wins by accident on a fast one, which is worse than failing because it fails *sometimes*. They are `settle(condition)` waits now, each naming what it waits for. Two exceptions prove the rule: the queued answer waits on its row rather than on a post, and **STOP waits on nothing at all** — an absence cannot be waited *for*, only given long enough to fail to happen, and too short a wait there is an assertion silently proving nothing. Walked twice to check it is not timing-lucky: 42, 42. |
| **Four faults the client's own A4 walk found, and one had never run at all** (4 Sep) | The first walk of the newly wired answer path, on the caregiver test: *"I want to add our nanny Marisol, she is wonderful"* came back as a **queued answer** offering Little Maestros and Hahamongna Watershed Park — a music class and a park — under the sentence *"10 local parents have shared something on this"*, above **two** listed records. Four separate faults in one screenshot. **(1) A caregiver *offer* must be refused, not answered.** The 1 Sep redirect to `/share` was gated on `capture || isCaptureStart`, because it was written for 10.1's script — so an unprompted offer sailed past it. `offersSomething` in `lib/capture.ts` is the discriminator, and the asymmetry is the point: "any good nannies near Altadena?" and "we need a nanny three days a week" carry no offer verb and are **answered** (held permanently, because `routeAnswer` treats anything caregiver-related that way). ⚠ The obvious inverse test — *is it question-shaped* — was tried and rejected: it fires on "we need a nanny", which is exactly the parent the redirect is not for. **(2) A question about care retrieved music classes.** `retrieveFor` reads **no subject at all** — a question is a market, an area, age bands and kinds, ranked by answer-ready, area and firsthand count — so it always returned both halves of the graph. `shares`/`caregivers` flags now let a care question skip the shares query entirely. ⚠ **Narrowing *within* a kind is deliberately not attempted:** a word list mapping "birthday party venues" to `place` would exclude the record of that exact name, which is a `tip`. And **name matching specifically does not work** — "toddler swim classes" shares no word with "Rose Bowl Aquatics parent & me", the right answer — so the next session should not reach for trigrams and conclude they helped. Recorded in `retrieveFor`'s header as the gap it is. **(3) The opening sentence counted records and called them parents** — and counted them *before* the budget loop dropped the ones that did not fit, while the comment above it claimed the opposite of both. How many **people** stand behind an answer is the strongest claim Pando makes (invariants 3 and 4) and it is **not derivable**: `firsthand_count` is per record and one parent can back several. The count is gone rather than corrected; the per-record labels already carry the claim where 5.6 computes it truthfully. `test:answer`'s three assertions had enshrined the same misreading and now assert the absence. **(4) The model half of 5.3 had never run once.** `SCHEMA` declared `confidence: { minimum: 0, maximum: 1 }` and the API refuses it — *"For 'number' type, properties maximum, minimum are not supported"* — so **every** call was a 400, and the `catch` turned each into the rule-based fallback, which is a legitimate outcome, so nothing looked wrong. `test:intent` exercises the pure fallback and never the request. The range is not lost: `applyThreshold` falls back on anything under `INTENT_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR`, and a schema the API rejects enforces nothing. **The difference it was hiding is real** — the fallback maps *any* caregiver word to `ask_caregiver`, while the model reads that same sentence as `contribute`. ⚠ **And the reason nobody could see it: `err.constructor.name` is minified in the standalone bundle**, so weeks of 400s were logged as `{ error: 'a' }`. It logs `status` now — a number survives minification, and unlike the SDK's `message` it cannot carry the request body, which is the `withDb` leak in a different library. `test:relay-live` is 42 and now reports `source: 'model'`. |
| **A question is answered now — the chain was built and joined to nothing** (4 Sep) | The developer's instruction was "Slack must behave exactly like Twilio, so add whatever is missing", and the first thing to say is that **there was no Slack-versus-Twilio difference**: both transports call one `handleInboundMessage`, so Twilio was exactly as silent. The gap was in the pipeline. 5.5 (retrieve) · 5.6 (label) · 5.7 (compose) · 5.8 (route and queue) were each built, each tested, and each **reachable from nothing** — `inbound.ts` classified the intent, logged an enum and returned. A parent who texted Pando a question was read and answered with silence. Same for 5.4: the parser for a clarifying reply existed, `saveClarification` existed, `pendingClarification` existed, and **nothing ever sent the question**, so that branch could never fire. **What the wiring is.** The tail of `handleInboundMessage` now runs `classifyIntent` (the comment it replaces promised exactly this: *"when 5.7 lands, this becomes `classifyIntent` and the fallback stays underneath it unchanged"*), and on `ask_recommendation` or `ask_caregiver` composes an answer from records and queues it. **Six rules, and the first is the one most likely to be read as a stub.** (1) *It never sends the answer itself.* `PILOT_HOLD_EVERYTHING` is true, so `routeAnswer` holds everything today and an auto-send path would be a code path nothing exercises — the `bands` / `area_slug` / starter-list lesson, three times paid. The answer goes to `/admin/answers`, where 14.2's approve-then-send already works; the day the flag comes off is a decision with somebody watching. (2) *Only a message nothing else claimed reaches it* — a blast reply, a freshness "yes", a settings choice and a clarifying answer were each a reply to something Pando asked, and `clarified` had to become a returned fact rather than a local, because an unreadable clarifying reply is very often a question and must fall through. (3) *The area and the age bands come from the person, never from the message* — the 11 Aug derived-graph rule, and `bandsForBirthYears` recomputes at query time because a stored band goes stale. A cold number has neither and still gets an answer: `retrieveFor` ranks by area and never filters by it. (4) *`caregiver_related` is read three ways* — the classifier, the words, and whether a caregiver actually reached the answer — because the cost of missing it is the highest-stakes sentence Pando sends going out unread. (5) *No database is not an empty answer*: `retrieved.configured` false returns without queueing, rather than telling a parent there is nothing from local parents when the truth is Pando could not look. (6) *The question text is stored in `answers.question_text`* and that is not an invariant 7 breach — the invariant is about **logs**, and the admin has to read what was asked; the log line carries counts and enums only. **And the parent hears something.** A held answer used to mean silence until an admin opened the queue, which on a stranger's first message is indistinguishable from a dead number — the one thing 5.9 exists to prevent. `heldReply` names a person rather than a system (that is the actual promise, §19) and **promises no time**, because nobody can keep one in a pilot worked by hand. ⚠ New user-facing copy, on the list for the client; deliberately **not** in `sms-templates.ts`, which is registered A2P samples where a reword is a compliance event — the same call `CLARIFYING_COPY` already makes. **5.4's question rides along with it**, asked only when `pendingClarification` is empty (asking twice is the "one refusal is a parent who did not want to answer" rule broken, and it would make the next reply ambiguous) and logged under the `clarify_*` template, which is the thing that makes the reply findable at all. **One measurement changed a character.** The age question carried an em dash — house voice, and not in GSM-7 — so the acknowledgement plus that question was 194 characters and **three** segments; with a comma it is 193 and **two**. A third of the cost of the most frequently sent message in the onboarding path, for one character. Safe because `CLARIFYING_COPY` is explicitly not registered copy; `test:routing` pins both the wording and the segment count.
