Walked end to end by `npm run test:relay-live` (38, was 25) against a real server and the live database: a cold "any good camps near Altadena?" now creates the person, queues a held answer with composed text, and posts the acknowledgement carrying `clarify_child_age`. |
| **`SKIP` was two words at once, and the one that lost was the one on screen** (4 Sep) | Found by the developer walking the capture in Slack, at the step that says *"anything another parent should know? Reply with a sentence, or **SKIP**"* — and getting **nothing back**. `PASS_KEYWORDS` is `["PASS", "SKIP"]` and the keyword block runs before the capture branch, so the word the screen asked for was read as a decline to somebody else's question. Three consequences, in rising order of cost: PASS is deliberately silent (strategy §6), so the reply was silence; the card five answers had gone into was **never written**; and the capture stayed `open`, so every later message from that number was swallowed as an answer to a question already finished — recoverable only with `CANCEL`, which nothing had told them about. Resolved by this pipeline's own tie-break rather than by reordering the block: **the records decide, and the more recently asked question wins** (the 1 Sep rule for a bare "yes"). A capture is a conversation Pando is holding one text ago; a blast is a question put to five people at once, so an open capture takes the word. `hasOpenCapture(phone)` is keyed by **phone, not person**, because it is consulted above `ensureInboundPerson` and running that first would mean creating a person in order to decide whether a stranger's PASS is a PASS; an unreachable database answers "no", so the effortless exit keeps working when the capture branch below could not run anyway. ⚠ **The first version of this guard was too broad, and review caught the fault it introduced.** It diverted *any* PASS-keyword message to *any* open capture — so a parent mid-capture who replied PASS to a Network Ask had that word read as a capture answer, and on a free-text step it would have been **stored**: "PASS" as a record's name. The guard now asks two things instead of one, and the second is the load-bearing half — the word must be a skip word (only `SKIP` is in both lists, so an ordinary PASS never even queries the database) **and** the capture must be at a step that accepts one. `readsAsSkip` lives in `lib/capture.ts` beside `CAPTURE_QUESTIONS`, because "which words does this question take" is a property of the script; the repo only reports which step is open. At `name`, which is free text and not skippable, "SKIP" is still an ordinary answer — and that is correct, because the alternative is a record called SKIP. **Removing `SKIP` from `PASS_KEYWORDS` was the third candidate and is worse than either: every message that offers the exit advertises *PASS*, but a parent who types SKIP at a blast would then fall through to `attachResponse` and be recorded as having **answered** it with the word "SKIP", which is a wrong record rather than a missing one. |
| **Slack's event text is not what the person typed, and the test was checking a copy of the parser** (4 Sep) | The relay's cold-inbound form is `+16265550001: hello`, and it had **never worked**: Slack linkifies a phone number, so the event carries `<tel:+16265550001|+16265550001>: hello`, the address parser requires the message to *start* with digits, and the route took its `ignored` branch — 200, `addressed:false`, no reply, nothing in `message_log`. Reported as *"з плюсом не спрацьовує, без плюса отримав повідомлення"*, which is exactly right: Slack does not linkify a bare `16265550001`. **The reason it survived review is in the test suite.** `test-relay.mts` restated the regex inline with a comment saying the route is a server module and cannot be imported — so it was checking a duplicate, and would have passed with the route's own parser deleted. Hence `lib/slack-text.ts`, following the house rule for a testable module (`phone.ts`, `capture.ts`, `matching.ts`): **no runtime imports at all**, which is why `addressedNumber` returns the number *as written* and the route composes `toE164` around it. **The whole text is normalised, not just the address**, because the escaping is not phone-specific: Slack escapes `&`, `<` and `>` in every body and wraps every URL, email and mention in its own syntax, so a parent's free-text capture answer would be stored containing `&amp;`. Entities are decoded **last**, or a typed `&lt;` becomes a link to unwrap. **And the prefix is stripped inside a thread too** — that half reached the database. A thread needs no address, so the whole message was the body, and a tester replying in-thread out of habit had `+16265550005: Sierra Madre Tumbling` stored as the record's **name**: a phone number one step from `shares.name`, the field published to other parents, stopped only by that capture never completing. ⚠ **And it is stripped only when the prefix really is a phone number** — narrowed on review, because the first version keyed on the regex alone: `addressedNumber` accepts seven to twenty digit-ish characters before a colon, so a free-text answer like "1234567: that is the gate code" would have lost its first seven characters silently, which is the same quiet loss the fix exists for, reintroduced by the fix. `npm run test:relay` is 31, was 21. |
| **Nothing in the seed flow sends anything, so the relay looked broken when it was idle** (4 Sep) | Recorded because it cost a testing session and will cost the next one. With the relay correctly configured — `MESSAGING_RELAY=slack` plus token and channel, `/api/slack/events` answering **403** to an unsigned request rather than 404 — a walk of `/join` and the profile produces **no Slack traffic at all**, and that is correct: Phase 1 writes to the database, and its one outbound is the verification code, which `transportFor` pins to the real provider always (a code in a test channel is a code the parent never receives). So the channel is silent until somebody writes into it. The three doors that produce a reply are an **inbound message**, an **admin action** (`/admin/answers`, `/admin/blasts`), and a **job** (`/api/jobs/run`). ✅ **Asking Pando a question is one of them since 4 Sep** — it was not when this row was written, and the row above records the wiring. A question now produces a **queued** answer in `/admin/answers` plus an acknowledgement to the parent, not an instant reply: the pilot holds every answer for a person, so the channel goes quiet again between the acknowledgement and an admin pressing Send. That is the design rather than a fault, and it is the thing to check before concluding the relay is broken. |
| --- | --- |
| **Closing `/join` alone moved the open door one URL to the right** (4 Sep) | The second half of the row below, and the more important half. `/join` was gated and `/profile` was not — and `ProfileFlow` says so in a comment written on purpose: *"a parent can deep-link straight here from a forwarded URL; don't block them"*, then `newSession({ invite_code: null, … source: "direct" })`. So typing `/profile` or `/share` by hand walked **straight into the questionnaire**, with no code, no attribution and nothing on screen looking wrong. `/done` was the only one that noticed, and only to show a "no session" panel. **The gate is now in `proxy.ts`**, which is the one layer that can both read a cookie and write one: `/join` with a valid `?i=` issues `pando_invited`, and `/profile`, `/share` and `/done*` redirect to the public site without it. **Six things worth not undoing.** (1) *The seed branch returns **before** `adminCredentials()`* — that call is a database read, and putting the admin credential store in front of a parent's first request would make the front door of the parent flow wait on it and go down with it. (2) *The cookie carries nothing and is not signed.* Presence is the whole signal; attribution still comes from the session's own `invite_code`, re-resolved server-side on every write (12 Aug), so nothing here can be forged into a group. Signing it would buy nothing a forwarded link does not already give away, and every write behind it is still gated by phone verification (invariant 11). **What it closes is the realistic way in** — a typed address, a stale bookmark, a crawler; ⚠ **a hard gate means a token per parent, which the client has declined three times** (31 Jul, 12 Aug, 27 Aug). (3) *Ninety days*, because the flow is autosaved to the device and a parent legitimately returns days later — a cookie that expired mid-pilot would bounce them to the marketing page with their own answers stranded in a `localStorage` key they cannot reach, which is a worse failure than the one this prevents. (4) *The code is resolved in the proxy **and** in the page*, deliberately: both call `validateInviteCode`, whose table is cached 60s, so they read one answer and cannot disagree — and a door must not depend on a matcher staying right. (5) *An invalid code neither issues a marker nor clears one* — somebody reopening a stale link with a since-retired code is a parent, not an intruder, and their own screens keep working. ⚠ **And `/join` is one of their screens, which the first version got wrong.** It bounced *every* codeless arrival, and `ProfileFlow` sends Back from the first question to `/join` **with no `?i=` on it** — so tapping Back two taps into the questionnaire threw a parent onto the marketing site with no way back but the original link. Nothing was lost (the session is autosaved) and it looked exactly like being ejected. Both the proxy and the page now consult the marker on `/join` too, which is the gate's own question answered rather than a hole in it: a browser carrying `pando_invited` already arrived through a valid link once. Walked on the built server — no cookie bounces `/join`, `/join?i=nope`, `/profile`, `/share` and `/done`; with the marker all five render, and the codeless `/join` is the real landing card rather than an error. (6) *`/caregiver` is deliberately **not** gated*: 2C is reached at `pando.is/caregiver` with **no token**, because Pando holds no contact detail for a nominee (invariant 13) and there is nothing to key one against — gating it would break the only path that flow has. **Two links had to go with it**, both now dead ends rather than cosmetic: the marketing page's gold **"Join the founding network"** button pointed at `/join`, which from that page redirected straight back to it — a control that visibly does nothing, on the screen a prospective contributor decides from; and `/done`'s no-session panel told a parent to *"start from your invite link"* over a link to the same place. Both are sentences now, because there is no honest destination left: the way in is an invite somebody sends you, and a button cannot hand you one. ⚠ Both wordings are new user-facing copy and are on the list for the client, along with the two header/footer buttons that still read "Join the founding network" and now scroll to a section explaining it is by invitation. |
| **The popup opens *on* a page, and the copy control is an icon** (7 Sep) | Her third instruction, and it took a correction: the first version returned the dialog over an **empty** screen, and her report was exactly right — a modal with nothing behind it does not read as a popup, it reads as a broken page. So the profile write now saves, goes to `/share` (the screen the parent is going to next anyway) and the dialog opens over it; closing it leaves them where they were headed. That also makes "once" honest with no second mechanism: the trigger is a code in the session with no `referral_shown_at`, so it cannot reappear on a re-save and a parent who reloads mid-popup still gets it. Measured at 448×282 with the chat thread visible behind it. **The copy control is a 44×44 icon button** — the icon carries the meaning and the `aria-label` carries the words, because a bare icon with no name is an unnamed control and an icon *plus* a visible label makes the row wider than the link it is copying on a 375px phone. **It says when it failed**, which is the `CopyButton` (3 Sep) and `/admin/invites` (7 Sep) lesson for the third time: `navigator.clipboard` is refused outright in some webviews and with site data blocked, and the earlier versions of this pattern swallowed it — the parent taps, the button says copied, nothing is on the clipboard, and they paste the *previous* clipboard into a group chat. The link stays selectable and wrapping so a person can take it by hand. **Two things a future session will otherwise re-break.** `m-auto` on the `<dialog>` is load-bearing: a modal dialog centres itself through the UA's `margin: auto` and Tailwind's preflight zeroes every margin, so without it the popup sits in the top-left corner — the admin's own `kit.tsx` `Dialog` had the same bug and was fixed in the same pass. And the link is built from `window.location.origin` rather than a constant, so a walk on a staging host produces a link that works where the walker is. **`ReferralPanel` is the same content in the other frame**, on `/done` — her instruction's second half, and `/done` is the screen headed "Thank you." while `/done/next` is "what happens next", two taps further on past a question a returning parent has already answered. It renders for anybody holding a code rather than only for a returner, because the popup is shown once and somebody who dismissed it has nowhere else to look; the anonymous path has no person row and therefore no code, so it needs no condition of its own. ⚠ All of this wording is new user-facing copy and is on the list for the client — including the one thing the panel must not overclaim: inviting somebody is not a promise of anything, because a credit is denominated in Network Asks and those do not exist yet (10 Aug). |
| **A number already in the database could register again, silently, and the write replaces everything** (8 Sep) | The client's report was that nothing tells a parent their number is already registered. What sits behind it is worse than a missing message: the person write is `onConflictDoUpdate` on `people.phone` (invariant 10, and correct), and every derived set — children, affinities, relevance, schools — is **replaced rather than merged**, deliberately, because a parent who removes a school must stop matching on it. So somebody filling the form again from a second device with fewer answers overwrote the richer profile they gave the first time, and the only evidence was a completeness number nobody was watching. ⚠ **The obvious place to say it is the number field, and that place is wrong.** `/join` takes a phone with nothing proving it belongs to whoever typed it, so an answer there is an **enumeration oracle**: anybody could work through a list of numbers and learn which of their neighbours is in Pando. Who is in the network is the asset, and it is exactly what this product does not publish. So the question is asked once the code is confirmed — the same proof `submitGate` already requires before anything is stored — and `GET /api/seed/me` reads the phone from that record rather than from the request, so it cannot be asked about somebody else's number. **It asks rather than refuses**, because updating your own profile is legitimate and is what the upsert is *for*; what was missing is the parent knowing that is what will happen. Two buttons, and neither is destructive by accident: *Replace it with these answers* is the behaviour that already existed, now chosen, and *Keep what I had* writes **nothing at all** — walked live against a real row, which came back untouched (same name, same neighborhood, same child, same `profile_captured_at`). Keeping also takes the **stored** name into the session, or the next screen thanks them under the name they just discarded. ⚠ A failed check falls through to saving: the parent has answered eighteen screens and holds a confirmed code, and turning a warning into an outage when a read does not come back is the worse failure. ⚠ New user-facing copy, on the list for the client. |
| **The context step is a setting now, and that needed a table before it needed a field** (8 Sep) | The client's instruction on the weights card: two columns, and let the *similar context* value be set here too. ⚠ **This reverses the 2 Sep decision** — *"the context step is shown and cannot be edited … rendering it as an editable field would be worse: a control must not claim a state nothing maintains"* — and that reasoning is exactly why this is a migration rather than a one-line change. `RELEVANCE_STEP` is a constant, and `affinity_weights` **cannot hold it**: that column is `integer` and the step is 0.5, which this file already records reaching the admin as a bare 502 (`invalid input syntax for type integer`). So `drizzle/0036` is `matching_settings`, key and value, `numeric(4,2)` — key/value rather than a column per setting, so the next knob is a row, and with a **CHECK on the key**, because a typo would otherwise create a setting nothing reads: the admin saves 0.8, the page shows 0.8, and the scorer uses the default for ever. `repo/matching.ts` reads it into `config.relevanceStep`, which the scorer has accepted since M6 and nothing ever passed. **0.05 is the grain, and it is enforced in the route rather than left to the column**: `numeric(4,2)` *rounds* rather than refuses — walked live, 0.555 stored as 0.56 — so the page would come back showing a number nobody typed, which is the exact failure the weight check was written after. Checked in **hundredths**, because `0.15 % 0.05` is 0.049999999999999996. **The context step is the eighth row, not a block of its own.** It started below a rule, on the reasoning that it is a different *kind* of number — per dimension, 0 to 5 in steps of 0.05, against whole numbers 1 to 20. The client's second look was right about what that produced: seven weights split 4 and 3 leave a gap at the bottom of the right column, and an orphan row under a rule sat in the card's whitespace instead of filling it. It goes into the **split** rather than being appended, so the columns stay balanced at four and four, and because the split reads down then across the eighth entry lands last — where a number that is not one of the seven belongs. ⚠ Its scale sentence moved to the footer, and that is not tidying: in a full-width row the third grid cell was wide enough for it, and in a column that cell is `minmax(2.5rem,5rem)`, where it wrapped to three lines against a one-line field. The footer already carries the weights' scale for the same reason, and `aria-describedby` still points at it. ⚠ **No bar on that row**, deliberately: the bar's denominator is the heaviest *weight*, and this is points **per dimension** with up to five stacking — 0.5 drawn against 5 would read as the weakest thing on the card, when the whole of life relevance is worth about half a shared school. **Two columns, and the two faults the measurement found.** Auto rows in a stretched grid share the free space, so the 3-row column spaced its rows 69px apart against the 4-row column's 51px — `content-start` fixes it; and the context row sat at a **third x position**, because its grid spanned the card while the columns are half of it, so it now renders inside the same two-column frame. Measured after, at 1440: two x positions (617, 1173), rows aligned at matching y, no label wrapping, card 525px → 396px. Walked live end to end: 0.5 → 1.5 re-ranked the demo cohort (7.5 → 8.5, 4 → 6, 3 → 5), a weight and the step saved together as **two audited rows**, 0.07 and 9 both disabled Save with the field ringed, and Undo restored both. ⚠ Still no send button and still no action on this resource that reaches a phone — what the page writes is configuration. |
| **The web search gets the context the answer already computed, and one place can no longer be named twice** (8 Sep) | Improving the public half, and the measurement is the finding. **It was given the raw question and nothing else.** `answerQuestion` works out the age bands (question first, profile second), the focus topic and the area *before* it calls retrieval, and passed the search none of them — so the two halves of one answer were looking for different things: the parents' half filtered to a toddler in Altadena, the web half handed *"any good classes?"* with no age and no subject at all. That is the fault that put a trail in an answer about toddler classes, one module along. `brief()` is those four facts plus **today's date**, as a labelled block under the question rather than merged into it — the parent's own words are what a search engine reads best, and rewriting them is how a question about swim *classes* quietly becomes one about swimming pools. Proof it works, from the matrix: the identical sentence *"anything for my kid to do after school"* came back with **Boys & Girls Club of the Foothills** and **Mary Wilcox Youth Center** for a 2011 child, and **The Little Gym of Monrovia** for a 2025 one. **The correctness fix is the exclusion.** Nothing stopped the web returning a place the parents' half already names, and the result is not a repetition — it is one class on two lines reading *"Validated by multiple parents"* and *"Public/general information"*, the answer contradicting itself on the one axis this product sells. `readFindings` takes the names retrieval found; `sameName` compares **token prefixes**, never string prefixes, because `"little gymnastics".startsWith("little gym")` is true and those are two businesses, and a one-word key must match exactly or "Waldorf" swallows "Waldorf Early Childhood". The prompt is told as well, and that half only saves a wasted slot. ⚠ **This makes retrieval and the search sequential**, which the old comment called independent; the cost is one warm round trip (~200ms) in front of a 4–9s search. **And a timeout, because nothing above it had one**: the pipeline moved into `after()` on 4 Sep, which removed the pressure and not the problem — an unbounded hang leaves the answer unqueued for as long as the socket lives, which nobody sees as an error. **Two defects the live matrix exposed and the source did not.** *`what` never reached a parent*: `KIND_WORD` translates a `share_kind` and a miss is `undefined`, so the three words describing the place were asked for, length-capped, stripped of praise and then dropped one function later — **eight of eight** public lines read "Tinkergarten in South Pasadena". *And four of eight named the town twice* — "Encore Music South Pasadena in South Pasadena", "Wonderland 4 Kids - Pasadena in Pasadena". Both fixed by deletion rather than repair, and a disagreement ("Swimphi Altadena in Pasadena") is deliberately **left visible**, because this module has no list of the market's areas and suppressing it on a hunch would delete a fact the page actually stated. `npm run probe:web-search` is the matrix — ten questions across cold and profiled contexts, driven through the real pipeline rather than a copy, asserting what has a right answer (no public line on a care question, no parent label on a public one, no place named twice) and printing the rest for a person to read. ⚠ It reports rather than gates: a model plus a live web is not deterministic, and the probe's own first version failed a case for expecting an answer where **asking** was correct — "asdfgh qwerty" opens a pending question, and "camps" was answered on one run and asked about on the next. |
| **A parent can sign back in, and the number is the whole account** (7 Sep) | Her second instruction. **The machinery already existed and nothing pointed at it**: `/verify/start`, `/verify/check` and the twelve-hour `pando_verify` cookie have been in place since 13 Aug, and the OTP only ever appeared at the *end* of the profile as a gate on storing anything. So a parent who filled everything in on their phone and opened Pando on a laptop had no way back — not refused, just never asked who they were. What was missing is a screen that *starts* with the number, and `GET /api/seed/me` to say who a confirmed number belongs to. **Four rules on that endpoint, and the first is the whole of its security.** (1) *The phone is never read from the request* — it comes from `verifiedPhone(verifyCookie(request))`, the same server-side record `submitGate` uses, because a number in the body would hand out a stranger's name and link to anybody who can type ten digits. That is invariant 11's purpose stated from the read side. (2) *It answers `found: false`, not an error*, for a number with no profile: the caller has proved they hold it, and the way forward is `/join`, which is where everybody starts — walked, and it lands there. (3) *It returns their own name, their own link and whether the profile is in, and nothing else* — no phone, no surname, nothing about anybody else (invariant 7). (4) *An unreachable database says so* (503) rather than reporting "no profile", the `persisted: false` rule applied to a read: telling a parent who has a profile that they have none is the one wrong answer available here. **The one write on a GET, and why it is allowed.** A profile completed before `drizzle/0034` has no personal link, and the only other place one is minted is the profile write — which a returning parent is not going to run again, so her third instruction's thank-you link would be empty for exactly the people who have been here longest. `ensureReferralLink` runs on the read, and it is safe because it is **bounded rather than merely idempotent**: `invites_referrer_person_uniq` is a partial unique index, so this can create at most one row per person for all time and a replayed request creates nothing. A failure is swallowed — the panel is simply absent — so it can never turn "who am I" into an error. **What signing in restores, and what it deliberately does not.** Identity: their name, their standing, their link. **Not the answers.** Those live on the device that gave them (31 Jul, reaffirmed 12 Aug and 27 Aug: no per-parent token, so cross-device resume is impossible by design), `raw_answers` is a record rather than a source, and rebuilding a session from it would be a second way to produce one. ⚠ So a parent signing in on a new device sees the thank-you screen and their link, not their filled-in profile, and **the screen says so before they find out** — that sentence is the honest consequence of a decision the client has taken three times. **Three defects the walk found, none of them visible in the source.** *The parent tapped "Text me a code" twice*: `/signin`'s dock said it, then `VerifyPhone`'s own button said it again and the first tap had sent nothing. Hence `autoStart`, off everywhere else because at the end of the profile and in the caregiver flow the dock says something else and firing on mount would text a parent who had only scrolled onto the screen — guarded by a ref, or development's double effect spends one of their three sends before they have read the screen. *The panel's reason line was about a different screen*: "Founding Status is tied to a real, reachable parent — until you confirm it, nothing you write reaches us at all", to somebody writing nothing. `audience: "returning"` says what is actually true — there is no password, the number is the account. *And the screen said one sentence twice*: my `<h1>` was "Confirm it's your number." two lines above the panel's own `<h2>` of the same words. It is `sr-only` now, on the `/share` precedent — the document needs a name and this screen's content *is* the panel. **Also swept:** `SkipLink` read "Skip to the questions" on all five flow surfaces and was true on one of them. |
| **Access is by link, and the code screen is gone** (4 Sep) | The client's call, and it is a change to *who can reach the tool* rather than to how a screen looks — so read both halves before touching either. **The screen went.** `/join` used to branch on an invalid code and *ask* for one: its own screen, a field, a "Checking…" button, a manual-entry analytics path. **The gate did not go with it — it moved up a layer and got stricter.** `app/(seed)/join/page.tsx` now `redirect("/")`s an arrival with no code, a retired one or an unknown one, server-side, so an uninvited visitor never renders the seed flow and there is no flash of a form they may not use. Why the screen was the wrong answer rather than one step too many: it was the one place this address was *useful* to somebody who had not been invited — it confirmed they had found the right door and only lacked the key — while 1.1's own rule is that the founding tool is "shared privately inside parent groups, not published". **Three things deliberately unchanged.** (1) *The gate is still soft below this point*: nothing authenticates anybody, a session's `invite_code` may still be null and every write route still accepts one, because a link forwarded last week must not become a dead end **mid-flow** (12 Aug) — what changed is only what happens at the front door. (2) *Attribution is untouched* — a valid `?i=` still lands on `people.invite_id`, still registers as a PostHog super-property, still counts an open. ⚠ **Corrected on review:** an earlier version of this row said `recordInviteOpen` runs before the redirect so bounced arrivals still count. It does not any more — the gate moved into `proxy.ts`, which turns an invalid code away **before the page renders at all**. For a missing or unknown code that changes nothing (`recordInviteOpen` only ever updated an existing row, so both were already no-ops), but a **retired** code used to increment its own `opens` and no longer does. The loss is small and worth naming rather than leaving as a stale sentence: "this old flyer is still circulating" is no longer answerable from that counter. Moving the increment into the proxy was considered and rejected — it is a database write on the parent's very first request, and keeping database work off that path is the reason the seed branch returns before `adminCredentials()`. (3) *It cannot loop*: `next.config.ts` rewrites `/` → `/join` only when the request actually carries `?i=`, and this redirect carries none — walked, along with `?invite=`, `src=qr`, and an unknown code. **Two costs, both stated rather than left to be discovered.** ⚠ *Uninvited arrivals are now invisible in PostHog* — no client code runs for them, so `seed_invite_invalid` can no longer fire and the ternary that chose it was removed as a branch nothing could reach; `invites.opens` is the remaining signal. ⚠ *`POST /api/seed/invite` is now the only door that answers "is this code real"*, and since an unknown code no longer admits anybody, a real one is worth more than it was. It has **no caller left in the app** and is kept only because `test:e2e` asserts the retired-code path, the `SEED_INVITE_CODES` fallback and the built-in-code refusal through it — deleting it means reworking four checks, which is a decision. It is rate-limited on the tightest seed bucket; if the client wants the gate hard rather than soft, that route is where the remaining exposure is. |
| **One page frame: banners above the controls, and one spacing rhythm** (4 Sep) | The order after `PageHead` differed four ways across twenty pages. **The one that was a defect rather than a preference:** `activities`, `demand` and `blasts` put the filter row *first*, so a green "Approved" confirmation rendered underneath it — and at 375px a six-pill `SegmentedFilter` wraps to three rows, which pushes that confirmation off the bottom of the screen. The reader acts, sees nothing, and has no way to tell whether it worked. The canonical order is now **PageHead → banners → Toolbar → filter → Explainer → the work**, with controls grouped and the explanation between them and the queue. **Two apparent violations are correct and were left alone:** `/admin/delivery` keeps its window picker in `PageHead`'s `right`, which is a header control and therefore above the banners by construction; and `/admin/contributors` keeps its banners *inside* the tabpanel, because they report on the panel — above the tabs they would show a "people" error to somebody reading the consent file. **Spacing:** `PageHead` owns `mb-5` and every block under it owns `mb-4`, which meant `Toolbar` had to own its own margin rather than borrowing one from whatever came next (an `mt-5` here, an `mt-4` there, so the distance between the controls and the work depended on the page). Measured in the DOM afterwards rather than read off the classes: **20px from the head, 16px between every block**, on every page. |
| **The `Explainer` was the first row of the reader's queue** (4 Sep) | On `/admin/blasts` and `/admin/payments` it sat **inside** the first `Card` — so our paragraph about how the page works was literally row one of the work. That was not sloppiness: its classes (`border-b … last:border-b-0`) were shaped for exactly that placement, which is why the three pages that put it above the content rendered it as a naked summary line on bare paper. The two placements could not both be right. It has its own `rounded-xl border bg-card` box now and always sits above the work, which is also the 3 Sep decision one level up — *"a collapsed explainer is a tooltip with a bigger target"* applies twice over when the explainer is also the first thing in the queue. **Still closed by default**, still hover-peeks and click-pins: that is the client's own newer instruction and this pass does not touch it. |
| **`SegmentedTabs` — and adding it is the 2 Sep decision, not a reversal of it** (4 Sep) | `/admin/contributors` used `SegmentedFilter` to swap three whole panels (the table, the consent file, the standing view), each with its own columns and its own `useAdminRows` fetch. That component's own doc says what it is not: *"A tablist implies panels you move between; these buttons filter one list that stays where it is."* So a reader heard six unrelated buttons and nothing announced that the region below had been replaced. The new sibling shares the pills exactly — it reads as the same control, because it is the same control doing a job one step bigger — and differs in the one way that matters: **manual activation**. `SegmentedFilter` chooses as the focus moves, which is right for a free filter and wrong here, where arrowing across to the third tab would fire two round trips nobody asked for. Arrows move focus, Enter commits. Walked live: ArrowRight moved focus to "What they agreed to" while selection stayed on "What they shared", and the click then swapped both the panel and its own accessible name. `VIEWS` is a shared constant so the tab labels and the panel's name cannot become two copies of the same three strings — which is how a tab ends up announcing something other than the tab that opened it. **This is the only page that gets it**; the other eight keep the filter. |
| **`title=` is a tooltip for a mouse and nothing else — 30 of them moved** (4 Sep) | Both `ui.tsx` and `kit.tsx` already document at length that a `title` is unreachable by touch and by keyboard, and that this caused two client reports of an "unexplained screen" while the explanation was already written. The raw count looked like 185, and **most of that was the census being wrong**: `PageHead`, `Card`, `Empty`, `Explainer`, `Dialog` and `RecordDrawer` all take a `title` **prop** that is a heading, not an attribute. The real number reaching the DOM was about fifty. Triaged by one rule — if the sentence is the *only place a fact is stated* it becomes a reachable `Hint`; if it restates the visible label it goes; if it is the documented dense-table affordance it stays. Eleven `Badge`s, seven `Th`s and nine `<span>`s became hints; the `Td` on the Founding column was **deleted**, because the column heading now carries that explanation reachably and saying it twice — once unreachably — is worse than once. The invite-code button got an `aria-label` instead: its visible text is the code, and "Copy the full link" is what it *does*, which is what an accessible name is for. **The nineteen `Button title=` stay, as an accepted exception with a stated cost:** `Hint` renders a `<button>`, and a button inside a button is invalid, so there is nowhere reachable to put it — the page-level `Explainer` is the answer where the sentence matters enough. |
| **The admin's tables were unreachable from a keyboard, and nobody could have seen it** (4 Sep) | `TableWrap` is an `overflow-x-auto` div with **no `tabindex`, no role and no name**, and `useEdgeFade` replaces the scrollbar with a mask — so the nine-column contributors table could not be scrolled sideways from the keyboard *at all*, and there was not even a scrollbar to suggest there was more. It now takes a required `label` and renders `tabIndex={0} role="group" aria-label` on the scroller plus an `sr-only <caption>`. **`role="group"`, deliberately, not `region`:** eight tables becoming eight landmarks is noise in the landmark list. The prop is **required rather than optional**, which is what made this a five-minute change — the compiler named all eight call sites, so no table could be missed. `Th` gains `scope` (defaulting to `col`) and a `hint`, and the five `title=` attributes on `/admin/contributors` moved onto it: one of them held the **only statement anywhere** of the difference between the reward threshold and the Founding threshold, in an attribute unreachable by touch and by keyboard. Measured after: five 24px hint triggers, `scope="col"` on every heading, zero `title=` left on any `Th`. |
| **The audit page sent a limit the server never read** (4 Sep) | `useAdminRows("audit", { limit: 200 })` against `select * from audit_log … limit 500`, which ignores the param. So the number on the client was fiction, the list silently ended at 500, and the reader was never told either thing — the dead-payload fault CLAUDE.md already records costing us once on estimate 2.2. `AUDIT_PAGE_SIZE` now lives in `lib/admin/types.ts`, which both sides already import from (a client page must not import `lib/server/*`), and the SQL and the page read the same constant so they cannot drift. **A line under the table appears only when the list is actually full** — a footnote waits for something to footnote. **No "load more":** paging means an offset and a stable sort key, which is a read-layer change and not presentation, so the honest move is to say where the list stops. Also `toLocaleString(undefined, …)` → `whenExact()`: this was the one place in the admin following the reader's locale, on the page whose whole job is being readable months later and compared against somebody else's screenshot. |
| **The same component had two appearances depending on the page** (4 Sep) | Three instances of one fault. **(a)** `RecordCard` is `px-4 py-4` with no border and no background — built for `Card > RecordList`, which is where five record queues put it. `/admin/freshness` and `/admin/impact` mapped it straight into a `space-y-4` div, so their records floated on bare paper while identical records elsewhere sat in a bordered card. **(b)** `/admin/flags` carried a local `Usefulness` that was a verbatim copy of `ConfidenceBadge`'s 0.4/0.6/0.85 bands. Swapping it in required changing the surrounding `<p>` to a `<div>`, because `ConfidenceBadge` returns a `<div>` and a `<div>` inside a `<p>` is invalid markup React hydrates as a mismatch — the kind of thing that only shows up when you actually make the swap. **(c)** `/admin/delivery` hand-drew its two headline numbers at `text-[26px] font-semibold` in the sans face, against `Stat`'s `font-display text-[1.7rem] font-bold` — so the two biggest figures on that page were set differently from every other big figure in the admin. Measured after: 27.2px/700 in Bricolage Grotesque, matching Payments and Overview. `Stat` gained `tone="alert"` for it (gold means *pending*, and a delivery rate under the 95% floor is not pending) and `explain`, which replaced the `title=` on "Still in flight". |
| **One page, one name — in the nav, in the title, and in the worklist** (4 Sep) | Five pages called themselves one thing in the sidebar and another in their own `<h1>`: *Asked for* / *What parents asked for*, *Withdrawn records* / *Withdrawn recommendations*, *Thanks & impact* / *Thanks and impact*, *Who Pando would ask* / *Who would Pando ask?*, *Network answers* / *What the network answered*. **The wording is the client's call, so the choice is on the list for her; the mismatch is a defect either way** and is fixed by picking one. Two rules decided which survived: "Withdrawn **records**" collides with the *Records* nav group, and a nav item is not a question, so the harness drops its question mark. Then measured, because `Shell.tsx` puts `truncate` on the label in a 16rem column: **zero of twenty labels truncate**, including the new 25-character one. **And the change created a third mismatch it had to chase:** the Overview worklist names its destination in a `where:` field, eight of which already matched their nav label exactly — so renaming the nav left "Asked for" behind in two rows. The rule that follows: this surface names a page in three places, and they are one decision. |
| **`motion` is installed, and it lives on `/profile` alone — the number is 44 KB, not 15** (3 Sep) | The developer asked for an animation library, and the reason it earns a place is narrow and real: **every keyframe in `globals.css` is an entrance**, so the app had no exit animation anywhere — `ProfileFlow` swapped a keyed `<div>` and the old step was gone on the next frame, and `animate-step-in-back` exists precisely because CSS can only animate something arriving. **What was estimated and what was measured are different, so measure.** The estimate was ~15 KB gz behind `LazyMotion`. Measured on the built bundle, the eager payload of `/profile` went **291 → 335 KB gz**: `LazyMotion` does not defer anything here, because `m` and `AnimatePresence` are imported statically and the bundler pulls the feature set in with the route. Giving `domAnimation` its own module specifier recovered 6 KB and no more. 44 KB is **more than the 34 KB the admin's Radix removal saved**, which is the measured decision the no-library rule rests on. So the provider is **not** in `Screen` — where it started, because every flow surface renders one — but inside `ProfileFlow`. Verified per route: `/profile` 334 KB with motion, `/join` 282, `/share` 297, `/caregiver` 280, all three with no motion in the payload. `/join` is a first-time contributor's very first request on a phone and pays nothing. **Two traps, both found by measuring.** `mode="popLayout"` is the obvious choice and silently does not work: it needs layout projection, which is in `domMax` and not `domAnimation`, so the outgoing step never unmounted and both questions stayed on screen. A one-cell CSS grid (`grid` + `[grid-area:1/1]`) gets the same result for nothing — the container takes the taller height, so the dock cannot jump. And **motion is rAF-driven**, so ⚠ *the step transition could not be verified in the Browser pane at all*: that pane runs `visibilityState: "hidden"` with rAF at **0 fps**, which freezes both steps mid-state. That is the same throttling trap `OtherSheet` and `ChatSeeding` already document, met from a new direction, and it is why everything else in this pass uses CSS. |
| **`usePresence` — CSS can animate something leaving; it just cannot keep it mounted** (3 Sep) | The one missing piece that made this app's exits impossible, in ~30 lines and no dependency: hold the element mounted for the length of its exit keyframe, then unmount. `globals.css` gains `fade-out`, `sheet-down` and `sink` beside the entrances they mirror. **This is what the sheet uses, not `motion`** — it is reached from the chat as well as the profile, and the library is confined to `/profile`. The exit mirrors whichever entrance actually ran: `sheet-down` against `animate-sheet-up` on a phone, `sink` against `md:animate-rise` on a laptop, where a centred dialog is nowhere near an edge to slide off. **Reduced motion is zero, not smaller:** the global CSS rule collapses the animation to 0.01ms, so a timer still holding the element would leave it on screen, motionless, after it had visually gone — and the query is re-subscribed, because a parent can change the setting mid-session. **It also degrades better than the library:** `setTimeout` still fires in a hidden tab and CSS animations settle at their fill state, so a backgrounded transition ends correctly rather than frozen. Walked live: opens `animate-sheet-up`, still mounted 60ms after Cancel carrying `animate-sheet-down`, gone by 560ms. |
| **`experimental.viewTransition` puts the whole app on React canary, so it is not enabled** (3 Sep) | Next 16.2 ships React's `<ViewTransition>` integration and the guide is right there in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`, which made native route transitions look free — zero bytes, directional slides via `transitionTypes` on `<Link>`. It built cleanly on the first try. **Then the bundle was checked**: with the flag on, the client ships `react@19.3.0-canary-3f0b9e61`, not the pinned `19.2.4`. The whole app — the flow that writes consent records and verifies phone numbers, and the admin — moves onto a canary React build in exchange for a slide between `/done` screens. Reverted. This repo refuses `tailwind-merge` for a smaller reason (it silently changes class resolution everywhere), and the same reasoning applies with more force to the React runtime. Worth revisiting when the flag is stable; the four-line diff is recorded here. |
| **Step change moves focus and says which step it is** (3 Sep) | Both flows swapped the whole body and left focus where it was — usually on a Continue button that is now a *different* Continue button, with a screenful of silently changed content around it. `lib/use-step-change.ts` scrolls to the top and focuses the new heading, and the flows render a stable `sr-only role="status"` carrying "Step N of M — title". **The region is outside the keyed element on purpose**: a live region has to be in the document before its content changes, which is the mistake `TypingDots` already paid for. **The first run is skipped**, and that guard matters as much as the feature — a resumed session lands mid-flow by design, and stealing focus on load moves the page under somebody who has not touched anything and can raise a keyboard on iOS. ⚠ **`CaregiverFlow` had no `useEffect` and no `window.scrollTo` at all**, so advancing opened the next screen at the previous one's scroll offset; that was the most visible defect in the flow and was invisible in review because the file looks complete on its own. The heading's focus ring is deliberately **not** suppressed: after a click `:focus-visible` does not match, after Enter it does, which is exactly the right split. |
| **`OtherSheet`'s modal leaked focus two ways** (3 Sep) | Its trap queried `"input, button:not([disabled])"` — nothing in the sheet is a link or a select *today*, which is what made it a trap rather than a bug: the next person to add one gets a modal that quietly lets Tab into the page behind, with nothing on screen saying so. Replaced with the full focusable list. And the scrim was a `<button aria-label="Close">` sitting **before** the dialog in DOM order and **outside** `formRef`, so the trap could not see it at all: Tab from the scrim escaped into the page, and Shift+Tab from the input landed on an invisible full-screen control. It is an inert `<div aria-hidden>` now — Escape already closes and there is a real Cancel button, so ⚠ one tab stop is removed and the keyboard path is still complete. |
| **Nine quiet actions, nine heights — and one of them was 36px** (3 Sep) | `TextAction` and `InlineAction` already settled every axis this needed (one size at 14px, `green` for an action and `quiet` for an aside, one disabled treatment, one box for `<button>` and `<a>` alike). Nine call sites predated them and each had re-decided the height: `h-11`, `min-h-[48px]`, `min-h-[44px]`, a bare `<Link>` at ~22px, and **`h-9` on `CardRecap`'s "Try again"** — a 36px target, the smallest in the chat, on the one control a parent reaches for *after something has already failed*. Adoption only; no new API. **Two visible changes to expect rather than discover:** the three under-dock links go 48→44px and 15→14px, and the two 13px chat actions go up to 14px — both are the design system's "Help / secondary" step, and the point is that one thing had five sizes. **`VerifyPhone`'s resend loses `disabled:opacity-50`**, which `TextAction`'s own header argues against and rightly: fading already-muted text drops it under AA *and* leaves it looking tappable. **The bug I introduced and the measurement caught:** converting `ShareMenu`'s label put `&apos;` inside a **string literal** in a JSX expression rather than in JSX text, where entities are not decoded — so the button read "I&apos;ll do this later" on screen. Typecheck clean, build clean, visible only by reading the rendered DOM. Swept the repo for the same shape afterwards; it was the only one. |
| **`Consent` makes the label boundary structural, because four comments could not** (3 Sep) | The rule is that the `<label>` wraps **only** the sentence being agreed to and the carrier disclosure sits outside it, tied by `aria-describedby` — with the registered text inside the label, a tap anywhere in ~230px of legal copy toggles consent, and `qa-checklist.md` calls that check "the one that matters". It was written out as a paragraph-long comment in `InviteLanding` and again in `ProfileFlow`, and remembered from memory in the two caregiver copies. A comment enforces it once per copy until somebody writes a fifth control. **What the four had already lost:** `bg-paper p-3.5` / `bg-card p-3.5` / `bg-card p-4`, `mt-0.5` against `mt-1` on the box (so the tick sat on the first line of text in three places and below it in the fourth), and the caregiver flow put its disclosure **outside** the bordered box, where it reads as an unrelated footnote rather than as part of what was agreed. **And one real defect that had already been found and fixed once:** the profile separated its two links with an inline `" · "` while `/join` used a wrapping flex row — with a comment on `/join` explaining that at 375px the row always wraps and an inline separator is left dangling at the end of a line. The fix existed and had not travelled two files. **The checkbox stays 20px** and that is written into the file header so nobody "fixes" it: the `<label>` is the target and it is several lines tall, so growing the box would be enlarging the wrong element and would break the tick's optical alignment with the first line. **`CaregiverFlow`'s local `Permission` is gone** — it was a `<label>` wrapping a checkbox, which is a control and not a block, so it belonged here rather than in `Panel`. One behaviour change: its `note` ("Available once you've said yes…") was inside the label and is now outside, so tapping it no longer toggles — which is the same rule the disclosure follows. Walked live on `/join`: tapping the disclosure does **not** toggle, tapping the sentence does. And on `/caregiver`: ticking "Appear in answers" washes it green and enables "Be introduced"; unticking clears **and** disables it again. |
| **`Panel` grew two dimensions, and without them the sweep was impossible** (3 Sep) | Twelve hand-written boxes that `Panel` was supposed to absorb, and it could not: it is fixed at `rounded-3xl p-5` while every one of them is `p-4` (three at `p-3`), and **five sit inside another `Panel`** — `VerifyPhone`'s `Blocked()` lives in a `tone="positive"` one. A `p-5` card inside a `p-5` card is a heavier screen, not a calmer one. `size="inset"` is `rounded-2xl p-4` with a plain heading; `rounded-2xl` is the design system's own input radius, so a box at input scale takes it and no new step is invented. **`tone="quiet"` is not an invention either** — `ProfileFlow`'s footnote already carried the argument in a comment: *"Neutral styling, deliberately. In green it reads as reassurance and in gold as a warning; it is neither, it is the honest limit."* The register had existed since the day it was written and had no name. **Radius and padding each come from one conditional expression**, never two appended utilities, because `cn()` is a plain join — the trap `Chip.tsx` documents for `active:scale-*`. **Three boxes deliberately did not convert:** `ProfileFlow`'s per-child blocks are a dense repeated list, up to six on the tallest screens in the flow, and eight more pixels each is how the dock starts eating the content — they are a shared `SUBBLOCK` constant in that file instead, which is one string rather than three and does not put a third size on a shared primitive for one caller. `ShareMenu`'s tiles stayed too: they are buttons with hover and press states, not panels. |
| **`CopyButton`, and `aria-live` on the button is the wrong tool** (3 Sep) | Two near-identical implementations — the caregiver invite in the chat and the referral message on `/done/next` — same class string, same 2400ms timeout, same two faults. **Both put `aria-live="polite"` on the `<button>` whose own label then changes**, which makes the *control* the live region: what gets announced is the whole button re-read, and announcing by mutating the accessible name of the focused element is unreliable in the first place. That is the rule `Note`/`ResultNote` already settled — the region is never the element, and it must exist before its content changes — arriving one surface later. The label swap stays for the visual feedback; a permanently-mounted `sr-only role="status"` carries the announcement. **Both also swallowed a blocked clipboard** (`.catch(() => setCopied(false))`), with a comment in one of them saying that was deliberate — so on an older iOS webview the parent taps and *nothing happens at all*, which reads as a dead button and is worse than a sentence. ⚠ The failure sentence is new user-facing copy, on the list for the client. **The box stays inside the component rather than becoming a fifth `Button` variant:** two call sites is thin, and appending `border-green` to `buttonClass("secondary")` would put `border-bark` and `border-green` in one layer where output order decides. `InviteMessage` stopped being a client component entirely as a result. |
| **An eyebrow on `green-wash` needs the deep green, and that is contrast not taste** (3 Sep) | Caught while moving `InviteMessage`'s label onto the shared `Eyebrow`, which would have quietly regressed it from `text-green-deep` to `text-green`. Measured rather than assumed: `green` (#587a4a) on paper is **4.50:1** — clearing AA for normal text by nothing at all — and on `green-wash` (#eef2e8) it falls to **4.30:1**, which fails. At 11.5px this is normal text, not large text, so 4.5 is the bar. `green-deep` on the same wash is 7.0:1. So `Eyebrow` has a `deep` tone with the numbers in its doc comment, rather than the call site remembering. The general lesson is the one this pass keeps re-learning: **consolidating onto a shared component is where an accessible one-off gets flattened into the inaccessible default**, and the way to catch it is to compute the ratio before changing the colour. |
| **`Field` — and the focus ring existed in two languages that cancelled out** (3 Sep) | Seventeen hand-written inputs and textareas across the seed, caregiver and chat surfaces. The census is what made this a defect rather than untidiness: **fourteen of them set `outline-none`**, which suppresses the global `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid gold }` in `globals.css:201` and substitutes a green border — while the **three in `CaregiverFlow` did the exact opposite**, keeping the gold ring and having no green border. So a keyboard user got one focus indicator on `/join` and a different one on `/caregiver`, and neither screen looked wrong on its own. The first reading of the audit had this backwards (it recorded the caregiver fields as having "no focus styling"); they were the only three that still had the app's own. `Field` does **both** — green border for the pointer, gold ring never suppressed — verified in the shipped CSS, where `.focus\:border-green:focus` lands at byte 30946 against `.border-bark` at 30057, so the later rule wins on focus without `cn()` needing to merge anything. **Why a component and not a `fieldClass` string:** an `<input>` centres its own text in a 52px box through line-height and a `<textarea>` does not, so `px-4 min-h-[52px]` is right for one and puts the caret against the top edge of the other — the three multiline fields had three different answers (`p-4`, `px-4 py-3`, `px-4 py-3 min-h-[84px]`). Same shape as the `TextAction` lesson: two elements that look interchangeable at the call site and are not. **`on` names the rule rather than freezing an answer** — a field is always the opposite surface to the thing it sits on, so `/join`'s names are paper inside a white `Panel` and `/caregiver`'s are white on a paper page; both were locally right and neither knew about the other, so flattening to one colour would have made half the call sites wrong. **`error` sets `aria-invalid` and links, and is deliberately not a live region** — it fires per keystroke on a half-typed number, so a region would read it out on every digit. `aria-invalid` appeared **nowhere** in this app before, which is why `PhoneField`'s "that doesn't look like a complete number yet" was a sentence no screen reader could reach; it has an `id` and a link now. **Two traps caught while building it.** `aria-describedby` is written after the `{...rest}` spread, so computing it from hint/error alone silently deletes one a caller passed — the OTP box passes one, pointing at its "valid for 5 minutes" line, and would have lost it with nothing failing; it merges now. And `OtherSheet` is the one field that stayed hand-written, through `fieldShell()`: the sheet's heading is already a real `<label htmlFor>`, so a second label would have made the input's accessible name the two of them concatenated. |
| **The logo existed in four sizes, and nobody had chosen any of them** (3 Sep) | `Wordmark` is the mark and the word locked up, and it was **hand-drawn three more times**: `BrandPanel` at `1.1rem`, the site header at `1.4rem` with an `h-[1.8rem]` mark, and `CaregiverFlow`'s header at `1rem` with `h-5`. Four sizes of one logo, none of them a decision — and a logo is the one element in a product that must not be approximate. All three call the component now. **The site header keeps its larger size as a named `size="lead"`**, because a masthead legitimately outranks an in-flow header; **the caregiver flow does not keep its smaller one**, because nothing ever made that flow's logo smaller than the parent flow's — every other `ScreenHeader` passes a plain `Wordmark`, and so does that one now. **The old `markClassName` prop is deleted rather than fixed**: it did `cn("h-6", markClassName)`, which puts two heights in one Tailwind layer where output order decides and the string does not, so it would have failed in a way that reads as a caching bug. No caller ever used it — a loaded gun with nothing pointing at it. `size` **selects** both halves instead of letting a caller append. |
| **Every `<main>` has an id, and the app finally has a skip link** (3 Sep) | There was no skip link anywhere and no `id` on any `<main>`, so a keyboard started every screen in the header — and on `lg` behind the whole `BrandPanel` first, on a flow whose profile alone is seventeen screens. One `SkipLink`, exported from `ui/Screen.tsx` and **re-exported** by the site shell rather than written twice, because a second skip link that looked different would be this pass's own fault committed on the day it was written. Two mechanism details: it is the **first child of the outer div and never inside anything animated** (a filled keyframe ending at `transform: none` still computes to the identity matrix and makes its element the containing block — the 5 Aug bug, and this is the one element that must be able to escape its own flow when focused); and `<main>` takes `tabIndex={-1}`, without which the browser scrolls to the anchor but leaves focus on the link, so the next Tab goes straight back into the header the parent just skipped. **The focus ring on `<main>` is deliberately not suppressed** — the last interaction was a keypress, so `:focus-visible` matches, and the ring is the only thing telling the parent where they landed. Walked live: first tab stop, visible when focused, focus lands on `MAIN#main`. ⚠ The wording is new user-facing copy and is on the list for the client. |
| **Three heading faults, and one of them inverted the whole document** (3 Sep) | **(a) `BrandPanel` emitted an `<h2>` before the page's `<h1>`.** It is an `<aside>` that precedes `<main>` in the DOM, so on every desktop screen the outline started at level 2 and only then reached the page title — the page reading as a child of the marketing line beside it. It is a `<p>` now and the `<aside>` is named instead, which is what a landmark actually wants; it is ambient context, not a section anybody navigates to. **(b) `/share` had no `<h1>` at all** — the only route in the app without one. It does not gain a visible heading, because the screen is a message thread and that is the point; an `sr-only` `<h1>` gives the document a name and moves nothing. **(c) `/privacy` went `<h1>` → `<h3>` nine times**, because `.legal` only ever styled `h3`. The sections are `<h2>` now and `.legal h3` is **kept as a second selector with the same rule**, so a future client edit that types an `h3` is still styled rather than silently rendering as body text. The `id`s are untouched, so the section index and every deep link still work. Also `WhatsNext` jumped `h1` → `h3` twice. |
| **The caregiver flow's progress bar filled up and then handed you two more screens** (3 Sep) | It had no bar at all, and a header counter reading "3 of 5" — which counted the **tap steps only**, so a caregiver who reached "5 of 5" still had the three permissions and the code screen ahead of them. A progress indicator that lies is worse than none, which is the same reason `/share` is deliberately getting no bar (an unbounded add-another loop has no honest denominator). `stepIndex`/`totalSteps` now count every screen a caregiver actually walks — intro, identity, the taps, permissions, the code — and the bar and the counter read from the same expression, so they cannot disagree. `done` and the declined ending get neither: they are where the flow stops, not steps in it. Walked live: ten segments, "Step 1 of 10", `aria-valuemax=10`. |
| **The review list was 23 buttons all called "Edit"** (3 Sep) | `ProfileFlow`'s review screen renders one Edit/Add per question, and a screen reader heard the same word up to twenty-three times with nothing saying which row. `Bubble`'s per-row Edit had solved this on the day it was written (`aria-label={\`Edit ${row.label…}\`}`) and the review list had not — the drift argument in miniature, on the screen where a parent decides whether their answers are right. |
| **The type scale is tokens now, and it was 52 sizes** (3 Sep) | The scale had existed since the design system was written and was enforced by nothing: `@theme` carried colours, breakpoints, motion and shadows, and **not one font size**. Measured before touching anything — **52 distinct `text-[…]` values across 588 usages**, against the eight steps the skill names, and **82 of them below the skill's own 12.5px floor** ("if something needs to be smaller than 12.5px, it needs to be cut instead"). That is not drift anybody could have seen in review: every individual value looks deliberate, and only the census shows there are fifty-two of them. **The names are roles, not sizes** (`--text-help`, not `--text-sm`), because a size name invites a judgement call about how small a thing is while a role name has exactly one right answer — and adding a step becomes a decision taken in `globals.css` rather than a bracket value typed into a component. **`--text-field: 16px` is deliberately a separate step from `--text-control: 15px`**, even though they read as one: 15px in an input makes iOS Safari zoom the whole page, so the field size can never be the control size. **Adopted only inside `components/ui/*` in this pass**, and each later stage adopts the tokens in the file it is already editing — a single 16-file sweep would put every visual regression in one unbisectable diff. Two things a future session should not undo: `text-body`, `text-step-title` and `text-landing` will read as 17px if you probe them with a runtime-injected class, because **Tailwind v4 only emits a utility it has seen in the source** — that is the scanner, not a broken token; and the four remaining bracket sizes (`BrandPanel`'s hero, `InfoDot`'s 11px glyph inside an 18px circle) are one-offs that are not on the scale and should not be forced onto it. |
| **One `Eyebrow`, one `Wordmark` — both were quietly two** (3 Sep) | Found by census, not by looking. **`Eyebrow` existed twice**: `ui/Screen.tsx` at 11.5px and `site/Shell.tsx` at `0.78rem` (≈12.48px). Same name, same job, imported from different places, a pixel apart, and which one you got depended on which file you happened to import from. The site shell **re-exports** it now rather than redeclaring — the public site keeps its own shell, which is deliberate, but there is no argument for a second eyebrow size. `tone` (`green`/`gold`/`muted`) exists so the two hand-written gold ones stop being hand-written. The component had **nine distinct `tracking` values** across the codebase; it is one token now. **`Wordmark` existed twice too**, and this one matters more: `BrandPanel` hand-drew `PandoMark` + "Pando" at `1.1rem` beside `Wordmark`'s own `1.15rem` — the same logo lockup twice, half a step apart, which is the one thing a logo must never be. `BrandPanel` imports `Wordmark` now. **`BrandPanel`'s badge is deliberately *not* an `Eyebrow`**: the rounded border is not spacing, and `Eyebrow`'s `className` is spacing only — so it takes the eyebrow *tokens* on its own box instead, which is what keeps it from drifting again. |
| **`ChipGroup`'s question label was an `<h2>` saying what the group already said** (3 Sep) | The sub-label for one question on a multi-question screen emitted an `<h2>` whose text **is** the `role="group"`'s own `aria-label`, two lines below it — so a screen reader met the same words twice, and the document outline gained a heading under an `<h1>` that was itself the question. It is a `<p>` now; the group is still named and nothing visible moved. Its two labels were also **13px and 12px** — the question sub-label and the section heading, two sizes for one kind of thing inside a single file, which is the fifty-two-sizes fault at its smallest scale. Both are `--text-eyebrow` now. Walked live on the four-question "Your circles" screen: four `<p>`s at 11.5px/0.15em, and the screen's heading outline is one `<h1>`. |
| **One invite per group — never per parent** (12 Aug) | The middle ground that turned out to be the actual answer. `invites` holds a row per parent group (code, label, optional `market_options.parent_groups` link), an admin manages it at `/admin/invites`, and `people.invite_id` records which one a contributor arrived on. **What that buys:** "which group actually delivered contributors" — arrivals *and* how many of them gave an approved contribution — which one shared link could never answer, and which is half of what QC Answers Q3 wanted. **What it deliberately is not:** a row per person. The day one is, cross-device resume, automatic referral attribution and `/seed/[token]` come with it, and that is the decision below, made twice. **With a database, the table is the only source** (12 Aug): `SEED_INVITE_CODES` is not consulted at all — not even when the table is empty, because a built-in code that works next to a real table is a door no admin opened and no admin can close. That is what took `sgv-founding` out of service. **The consequence to accept:** a fresh deployment with a database and no invites admits nobody by code, exactly as the admin is dark until somebody is added; one invite fixes it. Only an *unreadable* store falls back to the env, and that asymmetry with admin credentials is deliberate: an outage that let a revoked admin in is a security failure, while an outage that made every invite link a dead end takes the tool offline for people who did nothing wrong. Three more rules: the code is a **soft gate**, not authentication; a retired or unknown code **still lets the parent in**, without attribution, because a link forwarded last week must not become a dead end; and `SEED_INVITE_CODES` stays as the fallback so an unconfigured database still admits people. Resolution is cached 60s (`lib/server/invite-cache.ts`) and cleared after any `invite.*` write, because the first thing an admin does after creating a link is open it. |
| **The "Parent groups" question is gone, and nothing writes a parent-group edge any more** (14 Aug) | The second half of the removal below, and the developer's call: an invite *is* about a group, so the screen stops asking about groups. What made it right is that a parent could not tell the two questions apart — "where this link reached you" went on 12 Aug, but "Parent groups" drew from the **same `market_options.parent_groups` chips**, so the screen still read as the same question asked twice. **The consequence, and it is a real hole rather than a tidy-up:** the invite deliberately writes *no* affinity edge (a link forwarded out of a group is evidence somebody shared it, never that whoever opened it belongs there), so with the question gone there is **no source at all** for a `social_group` membership edge from a parent group — that affinity now comes from "Clubs & leagues" alone. `invited_via_group` stays attribution, and attribution only. **Closing the hole means reversing that sentence** — deciding an invite asserts membership — which is a decision, not a fix, and it is not made. `market_options.parent_groups` stays populated because `/admin/invites` links each invite to one of its values; the table is now read by the admin and never by the questionnaire. |
| **The invite records the group; the profile no longer asks for it** (12 Aug) | "Where this link reached you" (P6) was a question in the tap-first profile. With one invite per group the code already knows the answer, so asking a parent to find their own group in a list was asking them to re-enter a fact we held — and it produced a second, weaker copy of `people.invite_id`. The question is gone, and the two halves it used to mix were split: **attribution** is `invited_via_group`, set server-side from the code the server validated; **membership** was left to the "Parent groups" question — which has since gone as well (14 Aug, the row above), so membership is now asked nowhere. **The invite still writes no affinity edge on its own** — a link forwarded out of a group is evidence somebody shared it, never that whoever opened it belongs there. Asserted in `test:e2e`: a parent who arrives on a group code has the attribution and **no** edge to that group. |
| **The starter list is a rule with its own tests now, because the client has reported it wrong twice** (1 Sep) | `lib/starters.ts`, extracted out of `SearchableChipGroup`'s `useMemo`. Twice is the reason: on 27 Aug the area comparison was against a display name, so nine of seventeen areas silently never matched; on 1 Sep **five of her seventeen approved towns were never offered on the neighborhood question, and the list re-ordered and shrank the moment a parent tapped one**. Same shape both times — typecheck clean, suite green, feature doing the opposite of its purpose, visible only to somebody holding a phone. **The 1 Sep cause, and it is worth understanding before touching the area logic again: the neighborhood question is the one that *establishes* the area, so filtering it by the area is circular.** Two consequences fell out. (a) Seventeen starters against `STARTER_LIMIT = 12`, sliced alphabetically while the question was still unanswered, cut exactly San Gabriel, San Marino, Sierra Madre, South Pasadena and Temple City — her five. Verified against the live table first: all seventeen *are* curated starters, so the data was never wrong. (b) Once she tapped Pasadena, `isHome` matched Pasadena **and its nine sub-neighborhoods** (Old Pasadena, Linda Vista, San Rafael…), which clears `AREA_FLOOR` alone — so the other sixteen towns vanished; tapping Sierra Madre instead matched one record and the list was topped back up to eight. The escape is `wholeList`, per question rather than "no area passed", because a curated set the client requires shown whole must not be capped either. **And selections are no longer pinned to the front.** That pin existed so a chip survived the slice, and it is what produced her *"do not display Pasadena twice as both a list option and a separate selected chip"* — there is only ever one chip, but it had been lifted out of the alphabet and put on top, which on a wrapped grid reads as a second one. The guarantee survives without it: `ownArea` admits anything selected and the belt at the end re-appends what the slice still dropped, so a chip can move to the *end* of a long list and can never disappear. `npm run test:starters` (22 checks) pins both faults by name plus everything the fix had to not break — the multi-word area rule, the top-up order, the refusal furniture. Walked in a browser as well: seventeen towns, unchanged order after selection, `aria-checked=true` on the one chip. |

The chip is gone from all five and the label is `+ Something else`, where the `+` is what says it opens something. **(2) The rules on each page are now enforced against saved state, not only against new taps.** Her report was a page saying "Up to 3" with four chips lit and one saying "Choose up to three" with five — the caps were right in code and the **stored session predated them**, because `normaliseAnswers` only ever checked a value's *shape*. `pruneAnswers` runs on load and drops options a question no longer offers plus anything over the cap. Two rules keep it safe: **static questions only** (a market-sourced answer is checked against a table that arrives *after* the session loads, so pruning those would delete a real school whenever the fetch was slow), and **a retired option keeps its answer** — `RETIRED_OPTIONS` survives the prune, because a chip that went because item 17 *split* it recorded something the parent meant. **(3) `clears` is a new mechanism, and `exclusive` could not express it.** *"'Parenting on my own' must clear 'Parenting with a partner' and 'Co-parenting across households'"* — while blended family and a grandparent in the house combine with it perfectly well, which `exclusive` would have cleared too. One direction only: tapping a partner option does not clear "on my own", or the two chips fight each other as a parent works along the row. **"One per child" is gone from activities, camps and current care**, and the fix was to **invert the default**: `perChildLimit` undefined now means *no* per-child ceiling, and only schools ask for one (`2`). The old default of 1 is what produced a hint reading "One per child" under an instruction reading "Select all that apply" — item 10's own objection — and it also meant any new per-child question silently arrived capped at one with nothing on screen looking wrong. **The listening-ear screen is removed** on her explicit recommendation, folded into the parenting-experiences page as a footnote in her exact words; the `listening_ear` consent scope and vocabulary stay in the schema, because profiles stored under the old screen carry a real consent and `/admin/consents` has to be able to say what each of them agreed to. **Participation is now chosen, never assumed**: `EMPTY_ANSWERS.allowance` is `null` and the question is `required`, so the dock stays locked until a level is picked — and a null reaching the write route lands on **five**, the community minimum, never on the open-ended option, which would grant Pando more access than anybody agreed to. Cost and trust preferences do the opposite and default **on the server** when skipped (across-price-points, no-fixed-preference) rather than by preselecting a chip: they are ranking signals only, so a default asserts nothing, and preselecting would break her third universal comment. `npm run test:feedback` (68 checks) pins every item by her number. **⚠️ One thing deliberately not built**, because it needs a product decision rather than code: item 18's *"Not ready to join"* exit — what it means for access, and item 18's own "participation on hold" is Phase 2 behaviour. |

Fixed by band-filtering the static branch too — `optionsForBands` passes through any option with no `bands` and returns everything before P4 is answered, so no other screen moves. | **A refresh moves a date; a vouch adds a parent — and only the second one validates anything** (1 Sep) | 10.2, and the estimate writes it as one sentence: "lets a parent confirm an existing recommendation, which increases its validation count and refreshes its 'last confirmed' date". That reads as one act. It is **two**, and conflating them inflates the strongest claim Pando makes about a record. A **refresh** is the parent who already contributed it saying it still holds — that is what a freshness ping asks (10.3) — and it updates `last_confirmed_at` and nothing else, because the same parent saying "still good" is not a second parent. A **vouch** is a *different* parent saying they used it too, which is a second firsthand contribution. **The mechanism detail that makes this load-bearing:** `trust-labels.ts` computes "Validated by multiple parents" from `firsthand_count`, and **nothing anywhere reads `shares.validated_count`** when deciding what to say — so a vouch implemented as a counter bump would leave the label exactly where it was, and the feature would silently do nothing. Walked live: after a refresh the record still has one parent behind it; after an approved vouch, two. **Four more rules.** (1) *A vouch enters `pending_review`*, like a blast reply (7.6) and per the client's answer of 27 Aug — an SMS path must not be the one way unread text becomes a validation. (2) *Its `firsthand` is **true**, where a blast reply's is false*, and the difference is what was actually asked: a ping asks somebody the matcher picked as connected to the record, an unprompted reply is an opinion. A vouch stored as secondhand could never produce the validation this exists for. (3) *A withdrawal is marked, never applied* — a "no" makes the record stale and raises `recommendation_withdrawn` for a person, and does **not** reject it: one parent's changed mind is evidence, and three others may still stand behind it. (4) *A "no" still counts as a response* in the ledger — paying only for good news is how a freshness loop stops hearing bad news. **The bug this closed:** 10.3 had been sending pings since 27 Aug and `message_log` records only *that* a ping went out, never which record — so a "yes" could arrive and never be attributed. `freshness_pings` is that missing half, the same hole `answers.share_ids` filled for 9.1 one loop earlier. |
