The API version is pinned in a header, because without it Stripe applies whatever version the *account* is set to — so somebody upgrading it in the dashboard could change these responses with no deploy. |
| **A retry is one message, and every counter had to be told** (3 Sep) | 13.4 is "Delivery status **+ retry**"; the status half shipped 27 Aug and this is the other. It is **mostly refusals**, and that is the substance rather than caution: for most carrier failures a retry is a second failure with a second charge, and for one of them it is a compliance breach. **Never retried: 21610** (they opted out — retrying is texting somebody who asked Pando to stop, a second time, and the first one was already a bug), **30034** (`CARRIER_ERRORS` says it in words: "retrying makes it worse with the carriers"), **30007** (a carrier judged the *wording*, and the same wording is judged the same way — repeated attempts are what gets a sender blocked), plus the ones about the number rather than the moment (30002/3/5/6). **Retried: 30001, 30008, and no code at all** — a timeout or a 5xx, the clearest transient case there is. **An unrecognised code is not retried**, and the asymmetry is the reason: Twilio's 30xxx range is mostly reasons a message can never arrive, so not retrying loses one message and shows up on the delivery page, while retrying a permanent failure spends money forever and teaches the carrier that Pando does not listen. **`RETRY_LIMIT` is 1**, the window is 60 minutes, and past it the message is left failed — a freshness ping arriving two days late is one the parent cannot place. **The part that needed a migration is the promise.** Every contributor-protection counter reads `message_log`: the monthly allowance, the 48-hour gap, the response-rate governor, the one-ping-a-month rule. A retry written as an ordinary row would spend a parent's allowance **twice for one message they received once**, and restart their gap. So `drizzle/0029` adds `retry_of`, **four counter branches in `repo/outreach.ts` now carry `and m.retry_of is null`**, and `test:payments-live` writes two rows and reads the counter back — the naive count says two, the one Pando uses says one. **Three readers deliberately do *not* exclude retries**, and each for its own reason: the delivery *rate* counts attempts, because a route that needs a retry every time is exactly the problem that page exists to surface; `recordInbound` attributes a reply to the most recent outbound, which is the retry, and that is the one that actually reached them; and the weekly thanks window reads `max(sent_at)`, which is likewise the attempt that got through. **And the sweep is narrower than "retry failed messages"**, because `message_log` stores no body (invariant 7 at the schema level) — so it can only re-send what a template can rebuild. It rebuilds a freshness ping, a thank-you and a blast request, and deliberately declines a **verification code** (§19 gives the parent three sends they control, and a background job spending one on a code that may already have expired is worse than the failure) and an **answer** (5.8 stores `answer_text` and `/admin/answers` already has a card for "approved but not sent" — that queue is the retry, worked by somebody who can see whether the answer is still true). Every retry goes back through `sendSms`, which matters most for this job of all: the minutes since the original send are long enough for somebody to have texted STOP. |
| **Stripe first, then the row — and the webhook is the only evidence of payment** (3 Sep) | 13.5–13.7. **The success page cannot activate a blast.** A return URL is a link the browser follows, so anybody who learned its shape could activate their own Ask by typing it; a signed webhook is the only thing this app accepts as proof. A forged one buys free Network Asks indefinitely and puts revenue on the payments page that does not exist in the account — which is why unverified is a 400 and an unset `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` refuses everything, the same fail-closed rule as the Twilio signature and `JOBS_SECRET`. `/admin/payments` paints that state red, because a parent could pay and their Ask would sit unpaid forever with nothing in the logs. **The timestamp tolerance is the rule the Twilio verifier has no equivalent of**: without it one captured webhook stays valid forever, so a real $15 payment could activate a new blast every day. Checked in both directions, so clock skew cannot widen the window. And **every `v1` is checked**, because during a secret rotation Stripe signs with both and a verifier that reads the first and stops rejects half of all traffic for the duration. **Idempotency is a `where` clause and a unique index, never a check.** Stripe retries until it gets a 2xx and delivers the same event more than once by design, so `and payment_status <> 'paid'` plus `blasts_stripe_session_uniq` are what make the fifth delivery a no-op — a handler that read the row, saw `paid` and returned early would be the same logic with a race in the middle. **The one deliberate 5xx** is an unreachable database: the payment happened, the row does not know, and that is the only case a retry helps. **Refunds go to Stripe before the row**, which is the opposite of the order that looks safer: marking it refunded and then failing to reach Stripe leaves a record saying the money went back when it did not, and the parent — who can see their card statement — then knows more about Pando's finances than Pando does. **Two states, not one:** `status` is the question and `payment_status` is the money, because an Ask can be `fulfilled` and `refund_due` at once (the guarantee is about whether an answer was *useful*), and one column would make that unrepresentable. **Payment never clears `human_review`**: Last-Minute Care always carries it and `needsHumanReview` sets it for a short pool, the whole point being that a person looks before five strangers' phones ring — money is not that person. **No partial refunds and no automatic ones.** A partial needs a rule for what three mediocre answers are worth and nobody has written one; the promise is "no useful answer → not charged", which is all or nothing. `expire_blasts` already grants a *credit* automatically, because a credit costs nobody anything and is reversible. **And the hole this closed:** `sendBlast` admitted `draft`, deliberately, so a free tier could send immediately — which meant a paid Ask whose checkout was never completed sat in exactly the state the send button accepts. It now asks `paymentFor` and refuses with `unpaid`. |
| **`paymentFor` lives with the prices, because an import would have cost the test** (3 Sep) | Small, and worth recording because the first factoring was the obvious one and wrong. "What does this Ask owe" was written in `lib/payments.ts`, which then had to `import { TIERS }` — and that import is the one thing that stops a module being loadable by `node --experimental-strip-types`, so the suite could not run at all. CLAUDE.md already records the same trade for `lib/matching.ts` ("deliberately free of *runtime* imports"), where an earlier attempt lost the property by giving the age ladder its own module. **Money is the last place to give up an exhaustive test for a tidier import path**, so `paymentFor` moved to `blast-tiers.ts` — and the factoring that fell out is better than the one it replaced: *what a tier costs is a fact about a tier*, and tiers and prices live there. What stayed in `payments.ts` is everything that is not about a tier — whether a refund is coherent, whether the guarantee is owed, how to write cents as dollars — and that file now imports **nothing**. |
| **The guarantee has a clock, and the page is what proved it did not** (3 Sep) | Found by putting `/admin/blasts` on screen with four seeded Asks, which is the only way it was going to surface. `refundOwed` asked whether anything had been *approved* and nothing else — so a paid Ask sent an hour earlier, still live, its five parents still thinking, rendered **"Pando owes this parent a refund"** in alert red. That is not a cosmetic slip: it tells an admin Pando has failed a promise it is in the middle of keeping, and the obvious response is to refund money nobody owes. The fix is the guarantee's own wording — *no useful answer **in the window*** — so `expires_at` is a required argument and nothing is owed until the window has closed. It reads the date rather than only the status, because `expire_blasts` runs on a schedule and the page is read live, so a window that passed twenty minutes ago is owed before the job has caught up. An Ask with no window at all (`passive` contacts nobody) is never owed anything, which is right: nothing was promised and nothing was charged. The tab count went from 2 to 1 on the demo rows, which is the number being right rather than smaller. |
| **One segment is not 160 characters, and the em dash is the reason** (3 Sep) | 13.3, and the estimate's tilde in "the ~160-character segmentation" is doing all the work. SMS has two encodings and the carrier picks by content: GSM-7 gives 160 characters (153 once concatenated), UCS-2 gives 70 (67). A message is GSM-7 only if **every** character is in the GSM 03.38 alphabet — so one curly apostrophe, one em dash, one ellipsis or one emoji cuts the budget by more than half. **That is not hypothetical for Pando**: the design system says "Em dashes are fine", the copy voice uses curly quotes throughout, and `sms-templates.ts` holds registered copy written in exactly that voice. A template that reads as 150 characters of plain English is a **three-segment** message if one of them is a `—`, and the parent is charged for three while the log says one. Seven more characters (`^ { } [ ] ~ \` and `€`) are in GSM-7 and occupy *two* slots each, so even pure GSM is not `length`; and UCS-2 counts **UTF-16 code units**, so an astral emoji is two and a joined family emoji can be eleven. **It measures and never rewrites.** `toGsm7` exists and is offered, not applied: every substitution in it is typographic (a straight quote for a curly one, three dots for an ellipsis) and it returns null the moment it cannot reach GSM-7 without dropping content — but it is never used on `sms-templates.ts`, because that copy is registered with the carrier and A2P §3.7's rule is that what is registered and what is sent must match. "Correctly handles segmentation" cannot mean quietly replacing the client's em dash. So `sendSms` warns, naming the offending characters (punctuation, so it is safe to log), and the suite asserts the arithmetic — including that **`é` is inside GSM-7** and `ж` is not, which is the distinction the first draft of that test got wrong. |
| **14.9 turns a flag nobody could act on into a decision with two names** (3 Sep) | `/admin/freshness`. 10.2 has raised `recommendation_withdrawn` since 1 Sep on a rule this whole page follows from: *one parent's changed mind is evidence, not a verdict* — three others may still stand behind the same class — so the ping loop marks the record **stale rather than rejected** and stops short of deciding. It had no screen, so the flag landed in the general Flags queue where "resolve" means *I have read this* and leaves the record exactly as it was: still approved, still answering. **That is a defensible outcome, and it was the only reachable one** — reached by default rather than chosen, which is the fault. **Two actions, and neither needed a migration.** `share.retire` sets `status = 'rejected'`, because `shares_answerable` requires `approved` and so "will not reach an answer" already had a name; inventing a `retired` status would have meant a CHECK, a label and a second thing every read had to remember. `share.keep` clears the flag and **leaves the record stale** — deliberately not restored to fresh, which would assert the opposite of what the contributor just said, and `trust-labels.ts` computes the age label from `last_confirmed_at`, so leaving the date alone is what keeps the answer honest. Both carry a **required reason**, on the `claim.decline` rule: the audit row is the only record of why a record stopped answering, or kept going after somebody said it should not. **The two numbers that make it a decision** are on every card — `firsthand_count` and `recommending_count`. A record one parent withdrew and nobody else has used is nearly a retirement; the same withdrawal against four who would still recommend it is nearly a keep, and without both a reader is deciding on the withdrawal alone, which is the one piece of evidence guaranteed to be negative. **The flag is resolved by reason and subject, never by an id the page sent** — a stale screen can hold an id that has since been replaced (a record can be withdrawn, kept, and withdrawn again by a different parent), and what must end up clear is "the open withdrawal flag on this record", whichever row that is now. `flag_id` was on both the row type and the action until it turned out to be exactly that: sent and unused, i.e. the dead-payload fault estimate 2.2 already cost once, so it came back out of all three files. **It carries a nav count**, unlike the conversation record or the matching harness, because every row is a decision nobody has taken — and the count is knowingly a *subset* of `open_flags`, the same overlap the flags and escalation rows already have. **⚠️ "Re-blast" is deliberately not built.** The row says "retire-or-re-blast", and a Network Ask has an asker and a price (M7's tiers) while a freshness question asked by an admin has neither — wiring it would mean inventing who pays. What happens instead is honest: keeping a record stale leaves it eligible for the next `freshness_ping` run, so the loop asks again on its own schedule. Walked live: the queue row reports **2 parents who still recommend it**, and retiring takes the record out of `shares_answerable` — 1 → 0. **Both fixture bugs on the way were the fixture, not the query**: `consent_needs_evidence` refused a consented caregiver with no artefact, and `is_test` rows excluded themselves from a query that filters them, which is each constraint doing its job. |
| **14.1 asks for a transcript, and there is deliberately none to show** (3 Sep) | The row is *"Admin page showing all conversations, inbound and outbound"*, which reads as message text. **`message_log` stores no body** — its columns are direction, category, template, the provider's id, the delivery status and the time. That is invariant 7 holding at the *schema* rather than at a log line: Pando does not warehouse what parents write, and adding a body column to fill this page would be a privacy decision dressed as a feature. So the page says so once, at the top, in an `Explainer` — where a reader meets it before going looking for the text and concluding the page is broken. **What is left is not a consolation prize.** It answers the three questions an admin actually arrives with: did Pando text her and did it arrive (12.5's per-message status); did she reply, and did that reply *answer something Pando asked* — which is the exact signal 8.4's governor counts, carried as a badge rather than as an id nobody can use; and how often has she been asked lately, printed **against the allowance she herself set**, because eight outbound messages is either well inside "ask me anytime" or a bug and the page cannot tell without it. **Four rules.** (1) *No reply box, and no send of any kind* — the matching-harness rule for the matching-harness reason: an answer leaves through `/admin/answers`, where it has been composed, held and read, and a second path around 5.8 is how the one thing this product promises never to send gets sent. (2) *No nav count* — a sidebar number means something is waiting for you, and this is a record you open; the one queue-shaped view ("they spoke last") is not a queue Pando owes an answer to either. (3) *The history is fetched only when a row is opened*, via `useAdminRows`'s `enabled` — a 200ms round trip for a panel nobody looked at. (4) *Unattributed messages are counted, never listed*: a cold inbound before 5.9 made a person, and a caregiver who texted DELETE (`person_id` is `on delete set null`, 11.3), both leave real rows with nobody attached — and the live database has 19 of them, mostly from cleaned-up test runs. A count says the history is not the whole story without pretending there is somebody to open. **And the sample map has no fake rows for it**, unlike every other resource: a conversation history is the one thing where invented rows would answer "did Pando really text her" with a yes. |
| **11.4's hole is a record whose own *name* is a person — and the free-text half was already closed by construction** (3 Sep) | The row reads *"extending caregiver-style consent and care to any named individual provider (e.g. a tutor or coach), **however they're mentioned**"*, and the obvious reading is wrong in a way worth writing down. **The obvious reading:** a parent's free text names a teacher ("Ms. Diane got my son playing in three weeks" — CLAUDE.md's own example of the *most useful* card of four), so filter that. It needs no filter: `AnswerCandidate` in `lib/answer.ts` has **no free-text field at all** — the composer is given `name`, `venue`, `kind`, the trust labels and the counts, and nothing else. A named person in a parent's note structurally cannot reach another parent. That protection is by construction, and a test now pins it so nobody adds a `blurb` field later. **The actual hole:** `shares.name` *is* published. A tutor entered as an activity called "Diane Kovalenko" gets none of the caregiver apparatus — invariant 14's employment gate, invariant 2's 18+ question, invariant 13, invariant 1's four conditions all key on the record being a `caregivers` row — and her name goes to strangers. That is the whole of "regardless of capture path". **The detector is measured, not asserted, and the measurement changed the design.** Run against all 588 curated records in the Pasadena taxonomy, the two-capitalised-words signal alone flagged **16 of them** (2.7%) — "Marshall Fundamental", "Calvary Monrovia", "Altadena Stables", "Masjid Gibrael". That is not a tuning problem: place-plus-descriptor is the dominant shape of an institution as well as of a person, and no lexical rule separates "Diane Kovalenko" from "Marshall Fundamental" without a first-name dictionary. So **two thresholds for two costs.** *Strong* (an honorific, or a bare possessive) is near-impossible to trigger by accident and is what the SMS capture **refuses** on, reusing 10.1's existing caregiver redirect rather than a second wording of it — the cost of being wrong there is a parent sent to a form that can take the nomination properly. *Weak* only raises a flag, where the cost of being wrong is a glance. **Three vetoes, in order, and each earned its place.** A venue word is absolute, because **"Coach Patty's Gymnastics" is a real record in this taxonomy** — the importer's own search example — carrying an honorific *and* a business word, so letting the honorific win would flag a legitimate business every time a market's gyms are named after their founders. Place names are **passed in from `market_options`** rather than hardcoded, which is what took 16 false positives to 6; a list baked into the module would be a second copy of the taxonomy going stale the day a market is added. And the institutional words those last 6 named (masjid, fundamental, immersion, unitarian, stables, symphony…) took it to **0** — added from the measurement, which is why they generalise, unlike the record names would have. **It flags rather than gates, and a second gate in `retrieval.ts` was considered and rejected**: a record only reaches an answer once approved, so the dangerous state is not "unflagged" but "approved without anybody having been asked the caregiver questions" — and holding an *approved* record back on a heuristic would silently withhold something an admin explicitly decided about. Approval is the decision; the flag, whose `labels.ts` meaning carries the instruction rather than a description, is the prompt. Raised from all three share-creating paths through **one** helper, which fetches the place words itself: a parameter every caller had to remember is one a caller eventually forgets, and forgetting it means flagging legitimate businesses. **The bug the tests caught:** `words()` strips an apostrophe to a space, so "Diane's" arrived as two tokens and the `parts.length === 1` possessive branch never fired — it still flagged, via the two-word signal, but as **weak**, i.e. classified as the one signal that is not safe to refuse on. Tested on the raw string now. |
| **11.5 asks Pando to text a nominated caregiver, and the client already reversed that** (3 Sep) | Written down because it is an estimate row that must **not** be implemented as specified, and a future session reading only the workbook would build it. 11.5 is *"sending the caregiver a real explanatory message or link, capturing their 'yes' reply, and storing the consent evidence, timestamp and phone number — which also becomes the key used to de-duplicate that caregiver."* Every clause of that is ruled out by a later client decision. **Invariant 13**: no contact details for a nominated caregiver are ever collected or stored, so there is no number to text. **The 3 Aug decision** reversed the earlier "admin-only number, deleted if they decline" design in so many words: *"Pando never contacts a nominated caregiver… the nominating parent sends an invite we generate, and nothing about the caregiver is stored until they set up their own profile."* And the de-duplication key it proposes cannot exist for the same reason — which is why `/caregiver` has **no token** and an admin matches a claim to a nomination by judgement at `/admin/claims` (11 Aug), rather than by a phone number Pando was never given. **What 11.5's intent is actually served by, already built:** `lib/caregiver-invite.ts` composes the message *the parent* sends, 2C captures the caregiver's own consent with its four scopes and a version string, and `consent_needs_evidence` is a CHECK that refuses a consented caregiver carrying no artefact. So the outcome the row wants — a real explanation, a recorded yes, evidence and a timestamp — exists; only the *sender* is different, and that difference is the whole of invariant 13. **Worth putting to the client** as a line item that is done differently rather than one that is outstanding. |
| **A `npm run build` beside a live dev server makes `test:e2e` fail somewhere new each run** (7 Sep) | Recorded because it cost three suite runs and nearly sent me debugging a mapping that was fine. CLAUDE.md already notes that clearing `.next` under a running dev server breaks it; the subtler form is what happened here. A production build writes into the same `.next` the dev server is serving from, so afterwards **some route handlers answer from a stale module graph while others recompile** — and the symptom is not a 404. It is `test:e2e` failing at a *different* assertion on each run: once "every card was scored by the model — 3 of 4", once "stored in the graph's vocabulary" with `affiliation_visibility` rows carrying neither type, each of them a plausible-looking bug in whatever area happened to be stale. Both vanished on a clean restart: **285 passed, 0 failed**. So the rule is: after a `build`, stop the dev server, clear `.next`, start it again *before* believing a suite that talks to it — and treat "the suite fails in a new place each time" as an environment signal rather than a flaky test. **Two real improvements came out of chasing it and are kept.** The extraction check's denominator counted *every* `share_contributions` row for the parent, including the caregiver nomination — which `extract.ts` never scores **by design**, because a caregiver card's free text *is* the restricted note (invariant 12) — so the check could read "3 of 4" and blame the pass for obeying its own rule; both halves now exclude caregiver shares. And when it does fail it **names the unscored card**, because "3 of 4" sends the next reader to the database to find out which one, and the answer is what decides whether the fault is in the extraction pass or in the count. |
| **Entry is open again, and a link is now measurement rather than a door** (7 Sep) | The client's instruction, and it **reverses the 4 Sep decision two rows below** — *"access is by link, and the code screen is gone"* — so read both before touching either. The marketing page's "Join the founding network" button leads straight to the name-and-number screen, and an arrival with no code, an unknown one or a retired one is a parent rather than an intruder: `/join` no longer redirects, and `proxy.ts` issues the marker to every arrival instead of only to a valid code. **What the reversal deliberately does not touch, which is the part to keep straight.** A code is still resolved server-side and still means exactly what it meant: **attribution**. `people.invite_id` records which link somebody arrived on, `invites.opens` counts the arrivals, PostHog carries the code as a super-property, and **no affinity edge is ever written from a link** (12 and 14 Aug) — a forwarded link is evidence somebody shared it, never that whoever opened it belongs to that group or school. What is gone is only the *gate*. So `/admin/invites` keeps its whole job: it stopped being a door and stayed the measurement estimate 2.2's per-link funnel actually needs. **The marker still has a job too**, which is why it was kept rather than deleted: it is what tells `/profile`, `/share` and `/done` that the flow was entered through the front door rather than typed at — the hole the 4 Sep pass closed ("closing `/join` alone moved the open door one URL to the right") and which deleting it would reopen. ⚠ **The consequence to accept, stated plainly because it is the reason the old decision existed:** the founding tool is reachable by anybody who types the address, so 1.1's *"shared privately inside parent groups, not published"* no longer holds at the door. Everything behind it still does — nothing about a named parent is stored before their phone is verified (invariant 11), and founding is still granted by a person on the second approved contribution. **And one sentence had to go with the gate:** the founding section said places were by invitation and the link was not published, which under a button that admits anybody would have made the page contradict itself in two inches. |
| **The invites page makes school and personal links, and its header row had been mislabelled** (7 Sep) | The admin half of her fourth instruction. One selector — *a parent group · a school · one person (you)* — then the field that kind needs and no other: group shows the `parent_groups` select it always had, school shows a `market_options.schools` select, personal shows nothing, because **the referrer is whoever created the link** and that is already `created_by`. The schools come from the same `/api/market/options` the questionnaire reads, and that is the point rather than a convenience: a value the tap lists do not offer would attribute arrivals to a school no chip can match. A native `<select>` on purpose (13 Aug: typeahead and the OS wheel), and 133 curated starters is exactly the size typeahead is for. **The route refuses a mismatch in words** — *"Pick the school this link is for"*, *"Only a group link can name a group"* — because `invites_target_matches_kind` reaches this surface as a bare 502, and a constraint is the last line rather than the only one; the create path also **clears** the column its kind must not carry, so an `on conflict` re-creation cannot leave a stale group value on a row somebody turned into a school link. Walked live: both kinds created through the endpoint, both refusals in words, an invented kind refused, and the rows render as *"Aldama Elementary School · school"* and *"janet · personal"*. ⚠ **And renaming one header surfaced a fault older than this pass:** the row read `Group \| Link \| Matches` over cells holding `name \| link \| target`, so "Group" sat above the invite's own name and the column that actually showed the group was headed "Matches". Renaming "Group" to "Points at" would have moved the fault rather than fixed it — both are corrected. |
| **An invite can point at a school or a person now, and a referral can be owed to an admin** (7 Sep) | `drizzle/0034`, the schema half of her third and fourth instructions. `invites` gains **`kind`** (`group` \| `school` \| `personal`), `school_option_value` and `referrer_person_id`; `referrals` gains `referrer_admin`. **Four decisions inside it.** (1) *A school link is attribution only*, exactly like the group link has been since 12 Aug — a link forwarded out of a school's WhatsApp group is not evidence that whoever opened it has a child there, so no affinity edge is written from it and the 14 Aug hole stays open and documented rather than quietly closed by a link. (2) *A parent's own referral link is one more `invites` row*, which is the client's own earlier framing (27 Aug: a personal invitation is "one more invite row") and buys the existing mechanism for free — resolution, the 60-second cache, `people.invite_id`, the open counter and `/admin/invites` reporting — instead of a second thing that has to learn all of it. One per parent, by partial unique index. (3) *`referrer_person_id` is `set null` on delete, never cascade*: `people.invite_id` points at that row, so deleting the referrer must not take their arrivals' attribution with it — an orphaned personal link is history. (4) *`referrals.referrer_admin` is a nullable column rather than a `people` row for an operator*, because invariant 10 is that one person is one identity keyed by phone. ⚠ Its CHECK forbids **both** referrers being set and deliberately does not require one: `referrer_id` is set null on delete, so demanding one would make deleting a person fail on a constraint attached to a row about somebody else. Every constraint walked live: a group link carrying a school value refused, an invented kind refused, two referral links for one parent refused, a referral owed to an admin accepted. ⚠ **This also flips the 10 Aug decision** that "a referral is recorded by an admin, never read from a URL" — that row anticipated it ("if the client flips to unique links, this becomes automatic and the shape does not change"), and the shape did not change. |
| **“If you grew up here you have lived here ten years anyway” — and the fix is not the obvious one** (7 Sep) | The client's report on the tenure screen: the options do not hang together. She is describing a real defect and it is **not** the redundancy it looks like. **The question never said what it measured.** For a parent who grew up in Pasadena, left for a decade and came back three years ago, *"How long have you lived in the Pasadena area?"* has two truthful answers — 10+ for a lifetime, 1–3 for the current stretch — and the screen gave no rule, so two identical families answered differently and `time_in_area` became noise in the one dimension it exists to measure. ⚠ **The obvious reading suggests folding "I grew up in this area" back into the band list, and that is the one thing not to do**: item 11 took it out of that list on 24 Aug for exactly this parent, because as an option there it was mutually exclusive with every band and a returner *had* to pick one truth and drop the other. Putting it back restores that bug. So the help line states the rule instead — count from the most recent move, and tick the box too even if you left and came back — which makes every combination coherent: grew up here and 10+ is somebody who never left, grew up here and under a year is a returner. That pair is **more** useful than either answer alone, because it says both "knows this place deeply" and "has been away, so may not know this year's waiting lists". **The graph already kept them apart**, which is what made this a copy fix rather than a data change: `derive.ts` writes `grew_up_here` and `time_in_area` as their own dimensions, so neither stands in for the other and the pair is not one fact counted twice. The sentence it replaces — *"Your local experience helps Pando tailor answers…"* — explained **why** we ask, which is the kind of line the 4 Sep triage removes in favour of one naming a rule the reader would otherwise walk into. ⚠ New user-facing copy, on the list for the client. Walked live: the returner holds `under_year` **and** `grew_up_here` at once, and all four bands stay on offer once local roots is ticked. `test:feedback` is 112. |
| **The expecting gate held everywhere the flag reached, and one screen it could not** (7 Sep) | Asked to check that a child on the way is never asked about, so it was checked rather than assumed — and the way it was checked is the part worth keeping. Instead of reading the gate, the four cohorts were run through `visibleScreens` and every surviving question was matched against a word list (*your child · school · preschool · daycare · sitter · nanny · childcare · class · camp*), so a question that is **about a child and not flagged `perChild`** would surface. That is the `bands` shape of bug — a field declared, commented, reviewed and read by nothing — and it is the only failure mode the gate cannot see. **The gate itself is sound**: expecting-only walks 14 screens with **zero** per-child questions, a mixed family gets all four back with `[4]` as the only answerable child, and a parent who has not reached the ages screen is asked everything (the deliberate inverse). The word list found two unflagged screens, and one of them was real. **`childcare_backup` — "Your backup childcare"** is deliberately *not* per-child (a grandmother who can come over covers every child, so attributing it per child would invent a distinction the parent did not make), so the gate never touched it — and an expecting-only parent was asked *"What can you usually rely on when regular childcare falls through?"* about a child who is not born. ⚠ **The decisive argument is structural, not a matter of taste**, which is why it earns a gate of its own: the thing it is the backup *to* — "Your child's current care" — **is** per-child and is therefore hidden, so the flow asked for a fallback to something it had never asked about. The second reason is the rule this repo keeps applying: every option there would be answered about a hypothetical, and `relevance: "childcare"` derived from a hypothetical is a fact the parent did not state. It returns the moment a child is recorded as born. The other unflagged screen is the ages screen itself, which is where they *say* they are expecting — correct to show. **`hasBornChild` is the predicate now**, used by `isQuestionVisible` and by this screen's `when`, because a third spelling of the same condition is a third place to get it wrong. Walked live at 375px: an expecting-only parent reads **"Step 2 of 15"** against 18 for a family with a born child, the circles screen renders only *Clubs & leagues* and *Faith community* — the words "class" and "camp" appear nowhere in `<main>` — and none of the ten screens walked asks about a child. `test:feedback` is 107. ⚠⚠ **And a measurement note that cost twenty minutes**: the first live read said *"Step 2 of 18"* with `child_ages: [-1]` in storage, which looks exactly like the gate failing. It was the Browser pane's throttled rAF (~5fps, `visibilityState: "hidden"`) serving a stale DOM. Re-read after a real state change and it was 15. In that pane, a number that contradicts a pure test is the pane, not the code — check it twice before believing it. |
| **The progress bar said 18, the announcement said 17, and the code screen said 19 of 18** (7 Sep) | Found by reading the DOM on the first screen of the registration flow, not by looking at it. `Progress` was given `total={screens.length + 1}` (18 segments) while the live region announced `Step ${index + 1} of ${screens.length}` — *"Step 1 of 17"*. A sighted parent counting segments and a screen-reader user hearing the total were handed **different denominators**, and only one of them could be right. Worse on the verification screen, which passed `current={screens.length + 1}` and so rendered `aria-valuenow="19"` against `aria-valuemax="18"`: a bar past its own end. The 3 Sep fix for the caregiver flow is the precedent — *"the bar and the counter read from the same expression, so they cannot disagree"* — and this flow never got it; `totalSteps` is that expression now. ⚠ **The code screen counts as the last step rather than an extra one**, deliberately: whether it appears at all depends on `/verify/status`, fetched lazily at the end (13 Aug), so a denominator that grew by one on the last screen would change under the reader, and one that assumed the screen would appear would leave the bar a segment short wherever verification is off. Review is the last step; entering the code is the act of saving it, and the bar sits full there. Walked at 375px: question 1 says 1 of 18 on the bar, in its label and in the live region; review 18 of 18 with all eighteen segments filled; the code screen 18 of 18 with `valuenow == valuemax`. |
| **“Review my answers” dropped a finished parent into question 17** (7 Sep) | CLAUDE.md's own copy list carried this as an open question — *"`/done/next`'s «Review my answers» leads to `/profile`, which restores the flow on the saved `screen_index` rather than the review screen. Question, not a finding — I did not trace what `screen_index` holds for a completed session."* Walked to the end of the flow and traced it: it holds **16 of 17**. So a parent who had saved their profile, been thanked, asked their question and read what happens next tapped *"Review my answers"* and landed on **"Ask when you need help. Help when you can."** — mid-questionnaire, on the second-to-last question, with a dock button reading "Review". `ProfileFlow` now opens on the **review** when `profile_saved_at` is set. ⚠ **Keyed on the stored fact, not a query parameter**: a URL that changes what a screen does is what this app refuses (4 Aug), and the stored fact is the better test anyway — it is true of *any* return to a saved profile, not only of the one link that points here. An unfinished session is untouched, because the field is null until the profile is written: verified both ways, a saved session opens on "Does this look right?" with all 22 answer rows, and a session at `screen_index: 5` with no save still resumes on "Step 6 of 18". |
| **Two em dashes, two extra segments — and neither string is registered** (7 Sep) | Spotted in the relay's own log during the M5–M8 walk: `non-GSM characters raise the segment count { segments: 3, offenders: '—' }`. Measured with the arithmetic `sms-segments.ts` implements: the caregiver redirect is 185 characters and **three** segments because of one em dash, and **two** with a comma; the last capture question is 86 characters and **two** segments, **one** with a comma. The second is sent to every parent who finishes a capture over SMS, so one character was half the cost of the most-sent message in that script. Safe to change because **neither is registered A2P copy** — that lives in `sms-templates.ts`, where a reword is a compliance event — which is exactly the call `CLARIFYING_COPY` made on 4 Sep for the same reason. `test:capture` is 66 and pins the encoding of every capture prompt plus the redirect's two segments, because the cost is invisible in review: the house voice uses em dashes everywhere and they are free on a screen. |
| **Two things on `/join` that my own 3 Sep change had quietly broken** (7 Sep) | Both found by measuring the screen at 375px rather than by reading the diff. **The consent links were 31px targets.** `InlineAction`'s smaller box is a *documented* exemption — WCAG 2.5.8 lets a target off when it is "in a sentence, or otherwise constrained by the line-height of non-target text" — and that is what Privacy Policy and Terms were on 2 Sep: a clause appended to `SMS_CONSENT_TERMS`. Raising them under the checkbox on the client's instruction removed the thing the exemption rested on, because a standalone row of two links is not a sentence. They are `TextAction` now: 44px, same row, 14px. **And the header pill was the last hand-written font size on the screen**, `text-[12px]` — below the design system's own 12.5px floor and not a step on the scale; it takes `text-dock`. ⚠ **A JSX comment cannot go inside an attribute expression** (`right={ {/* … */} <span…> }` is a parse error, not a style problem) — the note moved above the element. |
| **Phase 1 and M5–M8 walked, and what the Browser pane cannot tell you about the flow** (7 Sep) | The whole registration path driven in a browser at 375px — `/join` → seventeen questions → review → the code → `/share` → `/done` → `/done/ask` → `/done/next` — plus the profile row read back from the database (person, two children with the right birth years, `age_range` ×2 and `neighborhood` derived, allowance 5, the recurring-SMS consent). M5–M8: every pure suite green (intent 31 · answer 48 · routing 36 · onboarding 38 · trust 47 · matching 53 · blast 50 · outreach 57 · inbound 49 · compliance 26 · vouch 32 · capture 66) and **`test:relay-live` 45/45** against a real server and the live database — a signed Slack event in, an unsigned one refused, a cold inbound answered, the answer composed and queued, the thread round trip, the caregiver offer refused with the link instead of an answer, and STOP silencing everything after it. ⚠ **`test:relay` had a stale drift guard** that failed for the right reason and the wrong cause: it asserted `transportFor` does not route on `category` by grepping for the two within 400 characters of each other, and the file grew a legitimate `category === "outreach"` a few lines below the *call site* — the 7 Sep quiet-hours relay exemption. A correct router reported as broken, which is the same shape as the `test:security` regex that once called a signed route unguarded. It now reads **the function's own body**, plus the stronger claim that the category is not a parameter at all. ⚠⚠ **And the measurement caveat that matters most for the next session:** the Browser pane runs `visibilityState: "hidden"` with **rAF at ~5fps**, and `/profile`'s step transition is motion-driven, so an outgoing screen stays mounted for a long moment — a naive heading count sees two, three, even four `<h1>`s and the page measures 3148px instead of 812px. Nothing is wrong with the flow; settle before auditing (poll until the count is stable). Which leaves one thing genuinely **unverifiable here and worth a real device**: four stacked screens is also what a parent tapping Continue faster than the exit animation would get, and the one-cell grid takes the tallest child, so the dock would move under their thumb. |
| **Seven numbers meant to be compared, at seven different x positions** (7 Sep) | The client's report on the matching harness: the coefficients look unstructured, give it a clear structure so somebody who has never seen the platform understands it. Measured before touching it, and the measurement is the whole finding: as a wrapping row of inline label-and-input pairs the seven inputs sat at **378 · 445 · 464 · 553 · 635 · 700 · 769** across three ragged rows at a 940px viewport. Seven values whose only purpose is to be weighed against each other, and not one of them aligned with another — no amount of explanatory copy would have fixed that, and the copy is not available anyway (2 Sep: she had the prose about the weights removed; 7 Sep: every explainer). So the structure has to carry the meaning. **One grid, one row per connection**, label left and number in a single aligned column — all seven inputs now at one x. **And a bar beside each number**, derived from the field next to it, which is what lets a stranger read the card: *school is the strongest connection there is, a neighbouring area is the weakest* is visible at a glance where seven numbers in a ragged row is arithmetic homework. Its denominator is the heaviest weight **as currently typed**, so the longest bar is always full and raising school to 20 shortens the others instead of overflowing. ⚠ **The bar is also what resolves an old tension**: 2 Sep forbids ordering by weight, because a field that jumps position the moment its value changes moves under the cursor of whoever just typed in it — so the ranking had nowhere to live. It lives in the bar, and the fields stay put. **The order was the other half of "unstructured", and the comment above it was wrong.** It claimed a `sortWeights` that **did not exist**: the order came from the query, i.e. alphabetical by `affinity_type` — the *slug*, not the label — which on screen read *class or activity · next-door area · children the same stage · faith · area · school · club*. Alphabetical by a string the reader cannot see is indistinguishable from random. `sortWeights` exists now and sorts by `affinityLabel`. **Three smaller things.** The scale is stated permanently ("A weight is a whole number from 1 to 20") and every field is `aria-describedby` it, rather than the sentence appearing only once somebody has already got it wrong. The read-only context step moved **below a rule**, because a greyed box with an "in code" tag sitting among seven editable ones is a control that looks broken for no stated reason. And each row's `<label>` is tied by `htmlFor` rather than by wrapping, so the association does not depend on `display: contents` behaving well on a form control. **Measured after, at 1440 and at 375.** Desktop: one input column, seven single-line labels, bars proportional (school 5 → 112px, next-door 1 → 22px), and the grid capped at 36rem because unconstrained it put ~500px of empty paper between "Same school" and the 5 that belongs to it. Phone: the bar is `hidden md:block`, because with three columns at 375px the label column was **93px** and every label wrapped to two lines — dropping the bar gives it 221px and one line each. The card is 525px tall against 323px before, and that is the trade being made deliberately: a list you can read costs more height than a wrap you cannot, on a page that is a tool you open rather than a queue you work down. Walked live: typing rescales every bar, 99 rings the field, sets `aria-invalid`, turns the scale line alert-red and disables Save, and Undo puts 5 back. |
| **Every nav group folds now, because “some hide and some don't” was the design** (7 Sep) | The client's report, and it was exactly true: one group of four carried a chevron and three carried a plain label. Which one folded was not arbitrary — it was **the only group whose pages have no count** — but that rule is invisible from the screen, so the affordance looked random, and picking a different group to fold would not have helped. The inconsistency was the thing to remove. Every labelled group has the same chevron now, and whether it starts open is **derived**: it contains the page you are on, or one of its pages has a non-zero count, or you opened it by hand. That keeps the property the static fold existed to guarantee — **a badge is never hidden** — and keeps it by construction rather than by a type, since a count above zero opens its own group. ⚠ **Which is why `QuietItem` is gone.** It was `Omit<NavItem, "count" | "urgent">`, and its job was to stop a counted page being filed behind a fold that could not react to it; a fold that reacts needs no such rule, and keeping it would now *prevent* the design, because the folding groups are all of them. **The state is `boolean | null`, not `boolean`** — and that is the part not to "simplify": counts arrive from a fetch, so a `useState(hasWork)` seeded on the first render would read a null `counts`, come out closed, and stay closed on a group holding twenty-two flags. Null means "nobody has touched this", and the derived answer stays live underneath. Walked at 1440×900: four groups, three open (People · Waiting on you · Network Asks) and Records & tools collapsed, 14 of 20 links visible and the nav 637px inside a 900px window; closing "Waiting on you" by hand takes it to 6 links and leaves its label; navigating into the collapsed group opens it, leaving it closes it again. At 375 all twenty links are in the row and **no toggle renders at all**, which is the fold being desktop-only by construction rather than by a viewport test. |
| **27 buttons called “What this means”, and the fix was to stop hard-coding the name** (7 Sep) | Found by walking every admin page at 1440×900 rather than by reading: `/admin/contributors` had **27** buttons whose accessible name was "What this means" and five more called "What this column means"; `/admin/answers` had 16. `Hint` renders a real `<button>` and already accepted a `label` — nobody passed one, and both callers hard-coded the generic string. That is the parent flow's *"23 buttons all called Edit"* (3 Sep) arriving on the admin side. **The 7 Sep route to fixing it is closed**: that pass took `/admin/activities` from 23 triggers to zero by hoisting the repeated sentences into one `Explainer`, and the client had every explainer deleted later the same day. So the remaining answer is to *name* them — and the name is **derivable**, because a badge and a column heading both already render the thing the hint is about. `plainText` walks the children a component was given, so `Badge` and `Th` name their own hints ("What the Shared column means", "What Waiting on you means") and **not one of the 59 call sites had to be edited**. Six loose `<Hint>`s that are inside neither had to be told, and were. **Two repeats survived naming and were dealt with as repetition rather than naming:** the "Waiting on you" badge carried the same sentence on 21 rows and it is the same fact the *column* heading already states reachably, so it went (the 7 Sep rule: what stays on a row is what differs between rows); and the caregiver sub-count's sentence moved up into the column heading it belongs to, five triggers becoming one. ⚠ **One accepted repeat, named rather than removed:** 6× "What “New number” means" on `/admin/answers`. Its sentence carries a fact the label does not — 5.9's cold inbound, which changes how a reviewer reads the answer — and with explainers gone there is nowhere to hoist it to. |
| **A queue of eight records jumped h1 → h3, and the level is context now** (7 Sep) | `PageHead` renders the `<h1>`, `Card` hard-coded an `<h2>`, `RecordGroup` an `<h3>` and `RecordCard` an `<h3>` — so whether a page had a legible outline depended on which of them it happened to use. On `/admin/activities`, `/admin/demand` and `/admin/payments` the record queues sit in a **titleless** `Card` (the filter tabs name the section instead), so the document went straight from `h1 Contributions` to `h3 Victory Park playground`: eight record titles filed under a section that does not exist. `HeadingLevel` + `Heading` read the level from context, `Card` consumes one **only when it has a title** (a card without one is a box, not a section), and `RecordGroup` consumes another. A level *prop* was the alternative and is worse — it makes every call site responsible for a fact about the tree above it, which goes stale the moment a card gains or loses a title. Every size is set by the caller's `className`, so tag and appearance are independent and nothing moved visually. **`Card` also stopped rendering an empty heading**: it emitted the `<h2>` whenever *either* `title` or `right` was present, so a card carrying only a control put a nameless heading in the outline. Measured across all 21 admin pages afterwards: one `<h1>` each, **zero level jumps, zero empty headings**, and the nesting where it should be (flags: h1 → h2 card → h3 group). ⚠ **And a false positive worth recording, because it was two minutes from being "fixed":** the audit first flagged an `<h2>` ("Find a parent") appearing *before* every page's `<h1>` — QuickFind's dialog, mounted in the shell. A closed `<dialog>` is `display: none`, so that heading is in the DOM and **not** in the accessibility tree. Check `offsetHeight` before believing a heading-order finding from a DOM query. |
| **`Button` takes a `subject`, because thirteen buttons called “Confirm” name nobody** (7 Sep) | Counted at 1440×900 against the live database: **22× "I've read it"** on `/admin/flags`, **19× "Nothing to do"** and 14× "I've dealt with this" on `/admin/demand`, 13× "Confirm" and 13× "Not from the group" on `/admin/founding`, 8× each of "Add to Pando" · "Edit or hold" · "Don't use" on `/admin/activities`, 8× "Approve" on `/admin/answers`, 10× "Stop sharing" on `/admin/invites`, 8× "History" on `/admin/conversations`. Every one of those approves a contribution, retires a link or confirms a person's founding status, and a screen-reader user tabbing the queue hears the same two words with nothing saying who or what. **The visible label is untouched** — a column of buttons reading "Confirm Grace Kim" would be the fix eating the layout it was meant to serve — so `subject` becomes an `aria-label` of "`{label} — {subject}`", built from the label with `plainText` so the accessible name still *starts* with what the button says (WCAG 2.5.3). The subject is whatever identifies that row: the person, the record, the invite's own group name, or the question in the parent's words, trimmed to something that can be read aloud. ⚠ **The caregivers ladder deliberately gets none**, and checking that is what stopped a blanket sweep: its other row actions are `MenuItem`s inside a `Menu` whose own label is already `More for {name}`, so the context is announced on entering the menu. Re-measured across all 21 pages: every repeated action name is gone. |
| **A queue of answers where every row was headed “Answer”** (7 Sep) | `/admin/impact` titles each card with the records an answer used, falling back to the literal word "Answer" — and an answer composed by the inbound pipeline carries an empty `share_ids`, so on the live database **every row was headed "Answer" with a kind of "0 records"** while the one string that tells them apart — *"any good toddler classes near South Pasadena?"* — sat three lines down inside the quote. A heading has to name the row it heads, so the question is the title when no record is named, trimmed at 72 characters. The `Quote` stays: it is the parent's own words against a green rule, which is invariant 8's distinction on screen, and the heading is a plain-text copy rather than a replacement. The kind now reads "no records named" instead of "0 records", which is the same fact said as a fact rather than as an arithmetic result. |
| **One `Loading`, and the text arrives one tick after the region** (3 Sep) | It was written out **24 times** — 23 of them the identical `px-4 py-10 text-center text-[13.5px] text-muted`, one at `py-8` for no reason — and **not one of them announced anything**, so a screen-reader user working the admin got silence between clicking and rows appearing. One primitive in `components/admin/ui.tsx`, with the `inline` variant the file already uses for `ResultNote`. **The mechanism is the part not to "simplify":** a live region has to be in the document *before* its content changes, so mounting a `role="status"` with its message already inside announces nothing — the region and the text arrive in one commit and there is no change to observe. That is the rule `TypingDots` paid for in the chat and `CopyButton` paid for again the same day, so the element mounts **empty** and an effect fills it. Two consequences worth keeping: the box holds its height either way, so nothing shifts when the text lands; and a query that resolves inside one tick now shows **nothing at all** rather than flashing "Loading…", which is the better behaviour on a warm pooler where most of these return in ~200ms. Verified in a browser by watching a client-side navigation with a MutationObserver: the region's content goes `""` → `"Loading…"`, which is exactly the sequence that makes it announceable. **No skeletons**, deliberately — a skeleton blinking for 200ms is more movement than a line of text, and it is a promise about the shape of rows that differs on all 23 pages. |
| **Nine pages offered a "Show sample data" button that could not do anything** (3 Sep) | Nine resources answer with a deliberately **empty** sample — answers, blasts, payments, blast replies, conversation histories, freshness, impact, delivery and matching — and each has its reason written beside it in `app/api/admin/query/route.ts`: a fabricated $15 payment answers *"has anybody actually paid?"* with a yes, and an invented ranking is the one thing the matching harness must never show, since judging the real ranking is its whole purpose. All nine (plus `Standing`) still rendered `NotConfigured`'s button, so an admin on a deployment with no database clicked it and **nothing happened** — a control that visibly does not respond reads as a broken page, so the conclusion is "the tool is broken" rather than "this deployment has no database". `noSample` replaces the button with the reason, and the sentence comes from the same decision as the empty sample rather than being written again at the call site. **What was deliberately *not* done:** adding `SampleBanner` to those pages, which the first audit proposed. There are no rows there to label, so a banner over zero rows would be a new untruth. Verified both branches live, with `DATABASE_URL` unset: `/admin/conversations` states the reason and shows no button, while `/admin/flags` — which has a real sample — still offers it and still fills the page when it is pressed. |
| **Three admin queues could not be typed into, and the cause was where the component was declared** (3 Sep) | Part C of the interface pass opens on six defects that are behaviour rather than styling, and the first is the one worth understanding because nothing about it looks wrong in review. `/admin/answers`, `/admin/responses` and `/admin/flags` each declared their row component **inside** the page component — `AnswerCard`, `ResponseCard`, `FlagCard` and `Grouped`. A function expression created during render is a **new component type on every render**, and React reconciles by type: a new type in the same position is not an update, it is an unmount plus a fresh mount, so the DOM node is discarded and rebuilt. All three rows hold a controlled text field whose value lives in *page* state — so the page re-rendered on each keystroke, and **each keystroke destroyed the field and took the caret with it**. Effectively: an admin could not write a rewrite of an answer, could not name the record a blast reply should merge into, and could not write down what they concluded about a flag. Three of the queues that exist *for* somebody writing something. All four are at module level now, taking the draft, `busy`, the setter and `run` as props — the props change on every keystroke, which is what props are for; the **type** is what must not. Every write became a functional update in the move (`setDrafts((d) => …)`), because the object form was reading a map captured at render. Walked in a browser rather than reasoned about: ten characters into a flag note and twenty-eight into an answer rewrite, asserting the *same DOM node* survives (a marker property set before typing), with focus and the caret still at the end. |
| **A copy button that ate the thing it copied, and a clipboard failure that lied** (3 Sep) | `/admin/invites` rendered the link cell as `copied === row.id ? "copied" : "?i=" + row.code` — the confirmation **replaced** the code — and `setCopied` was never reset. So the moment an admin copied a link, that row stopped showing *which link it was*, permanently, until a reload. A table cell must not be able to lose its own value: the confirmation sits beside the code now, the code never leaves, and the timer clears on unmount because a reload can remove the row while a confirmation is still up. **The second half was hiding a real failure.** `void navigator.clipboard?.writeText(…)` discards both the missing-API case and a rejected promise, so on a browser that refuses clipboard access the admin taps, the row says "copied", and nothing was copied — they then paste whatever was there before into a group chat. That is the `CopyButton` lesson from the parent flow arriving on the admin side, and the fix is the same: say so. **The browser walk proved it rather than the reasoning:** clipboard access is blocked in the preview pane, so the new cell actually rendered "couldn't copy — select it and copy by hand" beside an intact code — which is exactly the state the old cell would have reported as success. ⚠ That sentence is new user-facing copy, on the list for the client. |
| **A restricted note that failed to load said "Loading…" forever, and there were thirty dialogs for one panel** (3 Sep) | `/admin/caregivers`. `readRestrictedNote` had no `.catch`, and `noteBody` is `null` while loading — so a rejected read left the modal on its spinner indefinitely, on the one panel in the admin whose contents are a private note about a named person (invariant 12). An admin waiting there concludes the note is *missing*, which is a different and wrong fact. Failure is its own state now, and it is reported. **And the modal itself was inside the `.map()`**, so a queue of thirty caregivers mounted thirty `<dialog>` elements, thirty `useId`s and thirty effects to keep twenty-nine of them shut — when only one can ever be open, because `noteFor` holds a single id. One dialog at page level, reading `noteFor` directly. |
| **The history panel opened off-screen, and one banner was painted in a colour that does not exist** (3 Sep) | Two on `/admin/conversations`. **(a)** The message history mounts *below* a nine-column table, so an admin clicked a row's "History" and — on a full page — nothing appeared to happen: the panel was a screen and a half further down. It brings itself into view and takes focus now, on the three rules the parent flow settled the same day: `block: "nearest"` so a panel already visible does not jump, **no `behavior: "smooth"`** because this is a tool and the reader wants to be there rather than to watch a journey, and `tabIndex={-1}` so focus can land on it at all — without that the browser scrolls and leaves focus on a button in a row that is now off-screen, so the next Tab goes straight back. Walked live: focus lands on the panel. **(b)** The STOP banner used `text-alert-ink`, and `@theme` has no such token (the family is `alert`, `alert-wash`, `alert-line`) — so Tailwind emitted **no rule at all** and the line drew in inherited ink, on the sentence saying a contributor has asked Pando to stop. It is `text-alert`, which is what `ErrorNote` uses on the same wash. The general lesson is the one the type-scale pass keeps producing: a token that does not exist fails **silently** in Tailwind, so a colour name is only real if `@theme` has it. |
| **The "no database" banner was rendered twice on one page** (3 Sep) | `/admin/responses` printed `{sample && <NotConfigured …>}` above the page *and* the same component inside its first card. The audit first read this as "sample rows under the wrong label", and it is not: `app/api/admin/query/route.ts` gives this resource a deliberately **empty** sample, with the reason written next to it — an invented blast reply answers precisely the question the page exists to ask. So there were never rows to mislabel; there were two copies of one banner. The line is gone, and with it the now-unused `sample` flag. This is also why `SampleBanner` is **not** being added to `freshness`, `impact`, `conversations`, `delivery` or `matching` in the stages that follow: a banner above zero rows is a new untruth. The real defect there is that `NotConfigured` offers a "Show sample data" button that does nothing for those five, and that is C2's job. |
| **The intermediate landing page is gone: the link opens on the phone field** (3 Sep) | Her instruction: *“Проміжну сторінку «Join the founding Network» вирішено видалити. Користувачі повинні потрапляти за загальним посиланням/QR-кодом безпосередньо на екран введення номера телефону.”* `/join` had been a pitch — a headline, a paragraph, and three promise rows (no app · no account · two minutes) with their own icons — above the card that actually asks for a name and a number. **What made it removable rather than a loss:** every parent arriving here was invited by somebody they know, in a group they belong to, through a link that person vouched for. The page was selling something already sold, to a reader who had said yes twice before tapping. So the hero is one line and one sentence, the card *is* the screen, and the three promise rows and their icons are **deleted rather than moved** — a promise repeated on the screen where you act on it is furniture. **The dock now says the same thing for both paths** (“Start — about two minutes”), which is where the removed “two minutes” claim survives: it belongs on the button that starts the two minutes. **What is deliberately kept:** the invite-code gate keeps its own reassurance (“No app. No account. No password.”) — a different screen, she did not point at it, and a stranger who has just hit a gate is the one reader that sentence was written for. |
| **Two instructions named one link, so the consent box says it once** (3 Sep) | Her second item: *“Посилання на політику конфіденційності потрібно підняти вище, ближче до чекбоксу згоди на отримання SMS. Learn more… перенести в цей бокс.”* Privacy Policy and Terms had been a sentence appended **after** `SMS_CONSENT_TERMS` — i.e. below the carrier disclosure, at the foot of the box — and “Learn more about privacy” was a quiet text action further down the page, beside “Share without joining for now”. Both are now a link row directly under the checkbox's own label and above the disclosure. **The part worth telling her rather than absorbing quietly:** both of her instructions pointed at `/privacy`, so following them literally puts two links to one page in a box 375px wide, which is a reader deciding which of them is the real one. The row says it once, and **“Privacy Policy” is the name that survives** — not a style call: it is the document a consent box has to name, and it is the phrase the registered A2P opt-in language pairs with Terms. Her wording is gone; the destination is not. |
| **The explainers are closed now, and hovering peeks while clicking pins** (3 Sep) | This **reverses the 2 Sep decision** recorded above — *“open by default on purpose: a collapsed explainer is a tooltip with a bigger target”* — on her instruction, which is the newer document: *“Тултіпи лише при наведенні курсору, а не бути постійно розгорнутими, щоб не перевантажувати сторінку.”* **She is right about the cost, and it is the cost the earlier reasoning did not price.** Six admin pages carry an `Explainer`, and every one of them opened with a paragraph of ours *above the work* — on `/admin/payments` the reader met four sentences about how money works before the first payment. An explanation permanently on screen stops being read, which is the same failure as a tooltip nobody finds, arriving from the other direction. **What is kept from the earlier decision, because that half was sound: it is still not a `title` attribute.** It stays a real `<details>` — reachable by tap and by keyboard, findable by the browser's own find-in-page — and the hover is added *on top of* that, because hover alone would put the explanation out of reach on the phone an admin sometimes uses, which is the exact defect this component was written to end. **Two mechanism details a future session will otherwise re-break.** (1) *`onToggle` is not where the state is read back.* That event fires on **any** change to the `open` attribute, including one React made for a peek — so reading it would silently pin the moment somebody hovered. The click is intercepted instead (`preventDefault`), which also covers the keyboard, since Enter and Space on a summary both arrive as a click. (2) *The peek checks `pointerType === "mouse"`.* A tap fires `pointerenter` too, and peeking on a tap races the click that pins — the panel would open on the enter and close again on the click. Walked in a browser through all four states: a mouse enter opens, leaving closes, a touch enter is ignored, and a **pinned** explainer survives the pointer leaving. |
| **Month and year, and a child on the way is not asked about** (3 Sep) | Her fourth item, and two changes arrived as one sentence: *“Вік дітей (+Expecting): Замість точної дати народження збиратимуть лише місяць і рік. Для користувачів зі статусом «Expecting» питання для такої дитини не мають відображатися.”* **On the month.** Nothing in Pando has ever asked for a date of birth — the screen says “tap a birth year for each one” — so read as intent her sentence is *month and year is what we should hold*, and the year alone was one field short of it. `drizzle/0031` adds `children.birth_month`, **nullable and offered as optional**: the ages screen is one of only two required questions in the profile, every extra required field there is a measurable drop-off, so the year stays the required tap. Two CHECKs, both walked live — 1–12, and **never on an expecting row** (that child has no birth year, and a month on it would claim a baby was born in a month it has not reached). It is stored as a parallel map keyed by the age id (`{ "3": 11 }`) rather than by widening `child_ages`, and **that is the whole reason it was affordable**: `child_ages` is read by `derive.ts`, `matching.ts`, the `child_of` attribution keys, the review screen, the admin and `test:e2e`, and none of them moved. Nothing reads the column yet — sharpening the age band with it is a separate change, and doing that first would have been a migration whose effect nobody could see. **On Expecting, which was a real defect rather than a wording point.** `child_ages` stores `EXPECTING` as `-1` beside the real ages and every per-child path mapped over the lot — so a parent expecting a baby got a block headed **“Where does your child born in Expecting currently go?”**, asking which preschool that child attends. `answerableChildren` is the one filter all three call sites use now (the attribution chips, the repeated blocks, the “same for all children” shortcut), because three copies of it is three places to forget it and the one that forgets is the one a parent meets. **The age itself stays recorded** — expecting is a strong matching signal, and a parent 30 weeks in beside a parent with a newborn is the pair this network is best at connecting; what it loses is only questions that presuppose a born child. **And the route now derives the `children` rows** from the ages it has already sanitised instead of reading `raw.children` and repairing them: an out-of-range month from an older or crafted client would otherwise abort the whole profile write, and this was the last part of the profile write still trusting the body (11 Aug). `npm run test:feedback` (100) · `test:e2e` (285), which sends a month of 13 and a month for a child nobody tapped and asserts neither survives. |
| **The local-questions screen is gone; `topics` the field is not** (3 Sep) | Her fifth item: *“Сторінку із запитанням про готовність відповідати на чутливі/місцеві питання («Which local questions could you help with?») повністю прибирають з поточного флоу.”* The screen is removed. **The field stays** — in `ProfileAnswers`, in `EMPTY_ANSWERS`, in the route's allowlist and in `derive.ts`, where it is still merged with `topics_lived` into `topic_preferences` — on the same call as the 1 Sep listening-ear removal and for the same reason: parents answered it under the old build, and deleting the field would throw away something they actually said. **What it costs, and this is worth putting back to her rather than filing as done.** `topic_preferences` was the only record of which *local* subjects a parent is the person to ask about — camps, daycare, pediatricians, parks. Phase 2's routing has one fewer signal: the matcher is unaffected (it scores shared connections and life relevance, never topics), but “who should Pando ask about camps in Altadena” now falls back to what somebody has already contributed rather than what they said they know. **`topics_lived` is deliberately kept** — her instruction names one heading, and that screen is also the one carrying the topic-level consent she dictated on 1 Sep. `test:feedback` asserts the screen is absent, the sibling present and the field still there, so a future session cannot restore the page without her asking, nor strip the answers parents already gave it. |
| **The harness had stopped telling the truth about what it showed — search restored, the header fixed** (1 Sep) | The client's report: "there is no search now, and the scoring logic is not entirely clear." Two faults, and the second was more than a wording problem. **No search.** The asker picker was a plain `<select>` over every contributor — workable at the 26-person demo cohort, unworkable well before the pilot's ~350. Fixed the way `/admin/contributors` already does it: a text filter over the same rows, client-side, because the endpoint already returns the whole list in one query and a second round trip would only add latency to typing. The currently chosen asker is kept in the filtered list even when the query would otherwise exclude them, so typing after picking someone cannot blank the select out from under them. **The header claimed a selection this page never makes.** "Pando would ask (8 of 5 wanted)" reads as a decision, and `rankCandidates` makes none — it returns everyone who scored above zero, the entire ranked graph. What actually selects is `selectPool` (M7.3): it asks the scorer for `wanted × 4` candidates and only then removes anyone opted out or inside their 48-hour gap, neither of which this harness does. Saying "8 shown, 5 wanted" in one breath implied the eight were already a short list. The header now reads "Ranked by relevance (8)", and a line above the table states outright that a live Ask narrows this list further. **Two more things worth the same honesty.** A `relevance` of 0 on every row is frequently a property of the *asker* — nobody has answered a context question about them yet, so no candidate can score any relevance points against them — and the page now says so next to the asker's own summary, rather than leaving every "+ 0" looking like a verdict on the candidates. And a flat tail (seven of eight rows tied at score 2, on this cohort) is now named rather than left to look like an order: numbering ties 2 through 8 implies each is a step down from the last, which on a cold network is false, so the page states when three or more trailing rows share one score and calls their order a tiebreaker, not a ranking. The score column itself now reads "5 connections + 0 context" instead of "5 + 0" — a touch device cannot reach the header's tooltip, so the words that explain the split are on the row, not just above it. `npm run test:matching` (44 checks) is unchanged by any of this, because none of it touches `lib/matching.ts` — the scorer was never the problem; what it produced was being explained badly. |
| **The harness scores, and cannot send** (27 Aug) | 6.7, and the client asked for it herself — the estimate's reason is that it is "the cheap way to de-risk matching early without building a consumer web channel". `/admin/matching` answers two questions: who Pando would ask, and why each of them. **Four rules.** (1) *It calls the same `matchesFor` a live Ask will* — a harness that scored differently from the thing it validates is worse than none, so the page's only additions are names and a masked phone. (2) *There is no write action for the resource*, and that is the design rather than an omission: the estimate says "validated **before** any live outreach", and one screen that could both score and message puts the pilot's first blast a mis-click from a page built for experimenting. (3) *The weights are on screen*, because the question it exists for is "did my weight change do anything" — unanswerable if the page will not say what it scored with. That is also why `rankCandidates` breaks ties on person id: an unstable order would show a different ranking each run. (4) *It carries no nav count.* The sidebar's numbers mean "something here is waiting for you" (10 Aug); this is a tool you open, not a queue that fills, and a badge would make the nav cry wolf. **The bug worth remembering is a JSX one.** `the {data.wanted} wanted. In the pilot…` rendered as **"20wanted"** — JSX strips the leading whitespace of a text node when the block wraps across lines. Fixing that side alone moved it to **"wanted.In"**, because *both* boundaries of an embedded expression lose their space. It reads correctly in the source, so it was caught in the DOM and never would have been in review; the sentence is now built in JS, where no whitespace rule applies. |
| **M6 reads the graph, and reads its weights from the table every time** (27 Aug) | Phase 1 writes the matching graph; this is the first thing that scores it. Shape follows `derive.ts`: `lib/matching.ts` is **pure** — no database, no runtime imports at all — and `lib/server/repo/matching.ts` does the one query. The purity is not a style preference: on 11 Aug `derive.ts` had to be moved off the client because whoever computes these numbers decides whose questions reach whom, and a scorer that needs a connection cannot be tested exhaustively. **Seven things worth not undoing.** (1) *Weights come from `affinity_weights` on every call*, never from `weight_at_capture` — §18.1 over §8.1, so a config edit lands on the next question with no backfill. The table was already seeded (school 5 · activity 4 · faith 3 · neighborhood 3 · social_group 3 · age_range 2 · adjacent_neighborhood 1); the developer's check caught me claiming it did not exist. (2) *Life relevance is a boost at 0.5 a dimension, and all five together stay below one shared school* — the estimate says "not a hard filter" and the strategy puts relevant firsthand experience first with context breaking the tie. Counted **per dimension**, not per value, or a chattier questionnaire reads as a better match. (3) *The age band is recomputed from `children.birth_year`, never read from the stored `age_range` edge* — `derive.ts` says the edge is written "as of capture", and a toddler edge from February is a preschool child by the summer. The ladder lives in `matching.ts` and `questions.ts` imports it, so there is **one** copy — an earlier attempt gave it its own module and that made `matching.ts` unloadable in a node test, which is the property worth protecting. (4) *A neighbouring band is worth half*, and a family with four children takes its **best** overlap rather than a sum — otherwise the ranking rewards family size. (5) *The adjacent credit never lands on top of the same-area one*, and `neighborhood_adjacency` is symmetric stored once (`area_a < area_b` CHECK), so every read looks both ways. (6) *The candidate pool is bounded by the asker's own edges*, plus two unions for the matches that share no literal edge value — an adjacent area and a neighbouring band. Loading the whole graph to discard most of it is the shape to avoid. (7) *A contribution enters the graph only after approval* — the client's answer of 27 Aug, and `includeUnapproved` defaults false. Verified rather than assumed: 12 candidates against 21 with the gate off. **Two traps this cost.** Backticks in a SQL comment close the `sql` template — the second time in one day, so any comment inside one is now backtick-free on purpose.
