Own area first, then everything else, never filtered. (2) *"Can't find it? Add it" lives inside the results*, always visible even when there are matches, because the right answer may be the one Pando does not know yet. (3) **A selected non-starter must still have a chip after a reload.** Component state was enough until the page reloaded — after which the id sat in `answers.schools` with nothing on screen representing it, and `labelForOption` printed the raw slug `starkids-preschool` in the "For each one" follow-up. Two symptoms, one cause. The fix is `registerFoundOptions` in the **shared runtime table**, not in the component: every reader — `optionsFor`, `labelForOption`, the review screen, the chat's recap — goes through `marketOptions()`, so a component-level fix would have had to be repeated in each and forgotten in one. Plus a resolve-by-id mode on the search endpoint, for the mount where neither list has the record yet. It obeys `active` exactly as the search does, so it is not a second door into something an admin retired — asserted, along with the alias, misspelling and area-ranks-never-filters rules, in `test:e2e`. |
| **The 24 Aug feedback round is a questionnaire redesign, not a copy pass — 16 screens became 20** (24 Aug) | The client's objection to five screens was never length; it was that the answers were **not comparable**, so the data could not be used. "Two parents, one at home" meant a stay-at-home parent *or* one working from home. "Solo parent" implied no co-parent, when many single parents co-parent across households. "Nanny or regular sitter" ran together a nanny, a nanny share, an au pair and a Saturday sitter. So four screens split into eight: parenting setup / work setup, regular care / backup care, travel time / practical priorities, local knowledge / lived experience. **The cheap part is why it needed no migration:** each half keeps the *same* `life_relevance` dimension as the screen it came from, so the table gains rows and never a dimension, and the CHECK is untouched. Three shape changes matter more than the wording. (1) **Cost is single-select now** (`COST_PREFERENCE`): the old options overlapped so badly — compare carefully, care about value, pay more for quality, seek the best — that five ticks were an instruction to nobody. (2) **`maxSelections` caps trust circles and priorities at three**, because ten ranking hints rank nothing; it composes with `perChildLimit` by taking the tighter of the two. (3) **Regular childcare is `perChild`** and deliberately has no per-child limit — unlike a school, one child genuinely has preschool in the morning and a sitter after. Two corrections were about promises rather than lists: trust circles used to say Pando weighs affiliations *first*, which is wrong (relevant firsthand recent experience always comes first, these only break the tie), and the attribution screen's "Anonymous, but verified" was a contradiction — Pando knows exactly who the parent is — while "First name, only where it can't identify me" promised safety we cannot judge on a parent's behalf. Name and shared connection are now two questions, because a connection is shown **instead of** a name, never combined with it. **What is deliberately not built:** her Privacy Guidance wants a visibility state *per affiliation* (share the school, keep the club private). Today's one answer covers all of them, and the copy on the privacy screen already promises the per-affiliation version — that gap is the open item, not a wording fix. |
| **The four big categories are directories now, not chip lists — starters are curated, the rest is searched** (24 Aug) | The 24 Aug master list turned estimate 1.13 into a different task. It assumed four columns and a chip list; what arrived is **357 schools, 96 activity providers, 84 faith communities and 39 clubs** for one market, each with aliases, a city, an entity type, an operational status and a hand-curated starter flag — and the same instruction on all four sheets: *"tap first, search second."* 357 chips is not a long screen, it is an unusable one. So `GET /api/market/options` serves only `starter = true` for those four (schools 133, activities 35, faith 65, clubs 24), and `GET /api/market/search` reaches the rest with alias and trigram matching — verified: "LCHS" finds La Cañada High School, "CPG" Coach Patty's Gymnastics, "polytecnic" Polytechnic School. **Three fields deliberately overlap and must not be collapsed** (drizzle `0014`): `active` is may-this-be-selected-at-all — a *closed* school stays selectable, because a former pupil's stored answer resolves against that row; `status` (active/paused/closed/unverified) is the operational nuance that gates starter eligibility; `starter` is the curation. A starter is shown only when all three agree, and a CHECK enforces it. **The consequence for the "other" queue, which changed a test:** promoting a typed answer now makes it *findable*, not *featured* — it stays out of the curated set, because a starter list that grows by one every time a parent types something is the opposite of "about 8-12 familiar choices". **Two things the importer will not do:** merge two records with one name (three "Willard Elementary School"s across districts — her rule is not to merge campuses, so the second takes its area into the key), and touch a `user_added` row on a refresh, because the next refresh comes from the CDE directory and would otherwise drop an admin's work. `npm run taxonomy:import` is separate from `options:import` on purpose: the old one is still the format a plain sheet arrives in. |
| **A PostHog session is capped twice: ten minutes idle, thirty minutes total** (24 Aug) | The client saw a **66-hour** session. posthog-js caps a session at 24 hours from its start and rotates on 30 minutes idle, so 66 hours was never one session id — but the default is useless here either way: the seed flow takes about two minutes, so anything measured in hours is a tab somebody left open. Two limits, because one cannot do it. `session_idle_timeout_seconds: 600` is the client's number and the *mechanism is better than it sounds* — posthog-js runs a timer at 1.1× that value and, when it fires on an idle session, resets the id and emits `forcedIdleReset`, which the recorder listens for; so an idle tab splits on its own with no event needed. That still leaves the parent who touches the page every few minutes for three hours and never goes idle, and posthog-js has **no max-session-length option** — hence our own 30-minute ceiling in `useSessionLengthCap`, calling `posthog.sessionManager?.resetSessionId()`. **Two things not to "simplify":** the start time lives in `sessionStorage` rather than a ref, because a reload would reset a ref and the cap would never fire on exactly the long-lived tab it exists for; and it is keyed by session id, because posthog-js rotates the id for its own reasons and a bare timestamp would cut the next session short. Verified against the installed 1.413.2 — clamp `[60, 36000]`, and a backdated marker rotated the id on the next tick. |
| **A phone number is US or Ukrainian, and the formatted value says which — no country is stored beside it** (20 Aug) | The pilot's market is Pasadena; the people testing it are in Ukraine, and until now a `+380` number could not be typed into the app at all. The obvious design is a `country` next to every `phone`, and it is the wrong one: it would have to be threaded through `InviteLanding`, the chat engine, `StepWidget`, `CaregiverFlow`, the autosaved session and back out again on resume, with a stale copy in each — for a fact the number already carries. **So the formatted value identifies its own country**, because each country's own way of writing a number already does: `(626) 555-0143` and `067 123 45 67` are both ten digits, and no US area code can begin with `0`, so the leading digit settles it. `PhoneField` holds the picker in local state and nothing upstream changed — `toE164(phone)` still takes one string. **Four rules that make it safe.** (1) *Length, never prefix:* `380` is a real US area code (Ohio), so a ten-digit `380…` is American and only a twelve-digit one is Ukrainian — a prefix test would misroute a real US number. (2) *What the number is beats what the picker says:* a complete value overrides the selector, so switching to `+1` with a Ukrainian number in the box snaps back rather than leaving the field asserting a country the digits contradict. (3) *The trunk zero is stripped for storage and kept for display* — E.164 is `+380` + 9 digits, but the field has to show the `0` a Ukrainian actually types, and it survives its own keystroke rather than vanishing and reappearing on the second digit. (4) *Nine bare digits need telling:* without the trunk zero the value cannot settle itself, so that one branch takes the field's `hint` — the only reason the argument exists. `npm run test:phone` (35 checks) holds all of it, including the near-collisions, `toE164` idempotence (the routes re-parse what the client already parsed), and that no mask leaks the middle of the number; `test:e2e` walks a `+380` from the field's own national form to the `people` row and asserts one number is one person. The old local `maskPhone` in `VerifyPhone` went with it: it took the last ten digits and wrapped them in US parentheses, so a Ukrainian number read as `(067) •••‑4567` — a trunk zero dressed as an area code. **What this does not do is make a Ukrainian number reachable:** A2P 10DLC is a US registration, so sending to `+380` needs Geo Permissions for Ukraine on the account and a sender the Messaging Service can use outside the US. Both are console configuration; until they are done a `+380` gets `provider_error` while a `+1` sends. Ukrainian numbers work today for the OTP *flow* because dev codes are on screen — which is exactly the state pilot testing needs and exactly the state that must not survive to the first real contributor. |
| **Dev codes do not replace the send, so Twilio must arrive *before* they leave** (20 Aug) | The operational fact that is easy to get backwards, and getting it backwards takes the tool offline. `SEED_VERIFY_DEV_CODES` does **not** short-circuit anything: `/api/seed/verify/start` calls `sendSms` unconditionally and the flag merely *adds* `dev_code` to the response. So the four states are not two. **(a)** Dev codes on, Twilio unset — today: nothing sends, the code is on screen, everything stores. **(b)** Both on: a real text goes out **and** the code is printed on screen, which means anyone who opens the page can confirm any number they type — this is the state to pass through quickly, not to sit in. **(c)** Twilio on, dev codes off — the destination. **(d)** *Both off is the one that must never ship*: `/api/seed/verify/status` computes `sendable: provisioned \|\| devCodes`, so with neither, every founding parent falls to the deferred path and **nothing anyone writes reaches the database** — exactly the state the 14 Aug change existed to escape. Therefore: **add the three `TWILIO_*` values first (or in the same deploy), never remove the flag on its own.** Worth knowing while doing it: the two flags read their env asymmetrically and both fail safe — `SEED_REQUIRE_VERIFICATION` is on unless the value is literally `"0"`, `SEED_VERIFY_DEV_CODES` is off unless it is literally `"1"` — so deleting either line leaves the secure setting. All three Twilio values are server env read at request time, so this is `docker compose up -d` and not a rebuild. Verify with `curl <host>/api/seed/verify/status`; the finished state is `provisioned: true, dev_codes: false`. |
| **Reaching a Ukrainian number is Twilio console work, and its opt-out is not free** (20 Aug) | Distinct from the row above, which is about the *app* accepting `+380`. Sending there needs two things no env var can supply: **Geo Permissions** enabled for Ukraine on the account, and a sender the Messaging Service can use outside the US — because A2P 10DLC is a US registration and governs `+1` only. Until both are done a `+380` fails as `provider_error` while a `+1` sends. **The part with a compliance consequence:** the cheapest sender for OTP is an Alphanumeric Sender ID, and it is **one-way** — the recipient physically cannot reply. Invariant 6's opt-out step is implemented by Twilio refusing a number that texted STOP (error 21610), so on such a sender **STOP cannot work at all**. Fine for a transactional code the parent just asked for; **not fine for outreach**, so the first Phase 2 code that sends a Network Ask to a non-US number needs a two-way sender, or that number must not be on the rota. Also note `isQuietHours` is computed in `America/Los_Angeles` — the wrong clock for a `+380` recipient, and harmless only because quiet hours apply to `outreach` and Phase 1 sends none. |
| **An asynchronous change says so out loud — through one component per surface, never a hand-written line** (26 Aug) | Every result in the app was a paragraph somebody wrote at the point of use, and the duplication had produced the same two faults on both surfaces. **Admin:** the green result banner was hand-copied into **seven** pages, plus two more variants (a neutral box on Claims, a bare grey line on the contributor detail) — nine copies of one concept, and not one of them announced. `ResultNote` (in `components/admin/ui.tsx`, beside the `ErrorNote` that was already shared) is now the only one, with an `inline` variant for the case that sits under its own button rather than at the top of the page. **Parent:** four copies across `ProfileFlow` (×2), `VerifyPhone` and `CaregiverFlow`, and there the duplication had produced a **contradiction**: two of the four were `text-alert` red next to copy reading *"Your answers are safe on this phone — try again"*. Every one of these messages is recoverable by definition, so gold is the honest register and red was the colour telling a parent something was lost. `components/ui/Note.tsx` is the one now. **Three rules.** (1) *`alert` for a failure, `status` for a result* — a failed save interrupts, because the parent is about to move on believing it worked; a completed action waits for the next pause. (2) *The live region is never the list.* The chat transcript, the search results and the card recaps are all deliberately **outside** their region — one wrapping the results would read out forty matches per keystroke, which is worse than silence. So the search's four sibling messages ("Looking…", the failure line, "Nothing matching…") collapsed into **one** `role="status"` line that goes `sr-only` when there are visible results, because then the only missing thing was the count. (3) *A region must exist before its content changes* — which is why the `aria-live` that had been sitting on `TypingDots` never fired: that element mounts together with its one and only message. It is decoration now, and the chat's stable `sr-only` region carries what Pando actually said. |
| **A status a reader hears must not lag what was typed** (26 Aug) | Found by walking the search box in a browser after making it announce, and it is the trap the announcement created rather than revealed. `searching` was a `useState` set *inside* the debounce effect, so on the render immediately after a keystroke the flag was still false and `results` still held the previous query's rows — and the status read **"Nothing matching 'willard'"** before the search had started. As a sub-100ms flicker under a text box that had been survivable for as long as the box existed; announced out loud it is the app stating something untrue about a parent's own school and then correcting itself twice. The fix removes the state rather than adding to it: `resultsFor` records **which query the results answer**, `searching` is `query.trim() !== resultsFor`, and `.finally()` marks it settled in both branches. **The general rule:** a derived flag cannot lag its input, a `useState` set in an effect always can — so any indicator a screen reader will read has to be computed from the thing it describes. Every path that clears the query (`take`, the "add it" button) clears `resultsFor` with it, or the stale answer briefly claims to answer the empty one. |
| **A per-child question is capped per child — one each, except schools, which take two** (14 Aug) | The row below gave each selection an owner but left the list itself uncapped, so a two-child family could still name six schools, eight classes and ten camps — and the "whose is it?" picker underneath then had to attribute a list nobody could have lived. `maxSelectionsFor` (in `lib/questions.ts`) caps every `perChild` question at **unique birth years × `perChildLimit`**, where that multiplier defaults to 1. The children come from the same set the attribution chips are drawn from, so the ceiling and the owner list can never disagree. **Schools are the one question at 2**, and that is not a fudge: the screen invites former ones in so many words ("or has attended — Former counts"), and one child routinely has a preschool behind the school they are in now, so a flat one-per-child made the screen contradict its own copy. Classes and camps stay at 1 — those are things a child is *doing*, not a history. **Four rules that make it a cap and not a wall:** it blocks only *adding*, so a full question is still swapped with one tap off and one tap on; the unpicked chips stay on screen, inert but readable, because a parent has to be able to see what they did *not* choose; a typed "Other" counts toward it, or the one path that produces the least matchable data would be the one path around the rule; and an `exclusive` option (`None / prefer not to say`) is never blocked, because refusing to answer must stay reachable at any count. The sentence explaining the ceiling is generated by `maxSelectionHint` from the same rule that sets the number — a hint reading "one each" beside a cap of two would be worse than no hint — and it appears only once the cap bites, never as an error, because the parent has done nothing wrong. **It is UI-only, deliberately:** an ergonomics rule, not a safety invariant like the derived graph, and truncating server-side would silently discard answers a parent actually gave. |
| **A school, class or camp belongs to a child — and says which one** (13 Aug) | The questionnaire asked a household question and stored a household answer, so a family with a toddler and a teenager produced one undifferentiated list, and the per-school status hung off the school rather than off a child. Two things were wrong with that, and the second is the expensive one: the chip lists were the union of every age band the family covers (a parent's complaint: "безліч"), and **"same school" could match two families whose children are nine years apart** — the opposite of what §7.1's strongest signal is for. Each selection now carries the birth years it belongs to (`answers.child_of` → `child_birth_years` on `social_affinities` and `person_schools`, drizzle `0010`). **Three rules:** it is asked **only when there is more than one child** — with one there is nothing to ask, and the code attributes it silently; it is **skippable**, and an empty answer means "they didn't say", never "nobody's"; and the years are cleaned server-side against the ages actually tapped, so a body cannot invent a child. It stays *one list* per question rather than a repeated question per child (the developer's call): fewer screens, same attribution in the data.


## Phase 2, before any of it

Reconciled 27 Aug against *Pando Strategy — Current Direction* (Aug 2026), the
QC Strategy Reconciliation 8.18, and `Pando_Estimate_v3.2_updated.xlsx`
(M5–M15). Three things a future session must not discover the hard way.

**Two pieces of scope are in the strategy and in no estimate row.** Searched, not
assumed: `digest`, `grove`, `leaves` and `leaf` return **zero** matches in the
workbook. (a) *The Pando Digest* (§10) — a scarce, event-triggered SMS that reads
like a well-connected friend, with layered access (headlines free, depth for
members, and "answer one board question and the week's Digest opens fully"). The
strategy calls it "primarily a retention engine … likely the strongest habit
mechanism we have", so it is not a nice-to-have that fell off a list. (b) *The
grove* (§13) — a private per-contributor ledger where a leaf converts at posted
rates (5 = a free Targeted Ask · 15 = a month of Pando+ · 40 = a year), with
impact receipts and earned statuses, and **no leaderboard ever**. M9 covers the
thanks loop, impact tracking and tiers, which is adjacent but is not a ledger with
a published exchange rate. Both need quoting before they are built.

**The open board is a surface, not a feature of Blast.** §3 and §8 describe a
once-a-day digest of anonymous questions that opted-in parents browse and answer,
priced as the $5 Board Ask, with a rule attached that shapes the whole allowance
model: **answering on the board never spends the monthly allowance, and still
counts as giving** (§7). M7 is written around a targeted blast; nothing in it is
this.

**The tiers are named and priced.** Passive (free) · Board Ask $5 · Targeted Ask
$15 · Last-Minute Care (free during the pilot, strongest neighborhoods only), and
every paid Ask is guaranteed — no useful answer in the window, automatic credit.
The first Targeted Ask is free, deliberately: the pilot's real question is whether
she pays for the *second*.

**Four areas have no neighbour, and that is correct.** `drizzle/0018` seeds 29
pairs; Whittier, San Dimas, Glendora and West Covina are selectable neighborhoods
with curated starters and touch nothing else on the list, because on the ground
they do not. A parent there gets no adjacency credit, which is the honest answer
rather than an omission — and the migration cannot be annotated after the fact,
since drizzle hashes it.

**Three client answers, 27 Aug.** (1) The A2P campaign is **approved** — Twilio
provisioning is unblocked, and the ordering rule in the dev-codes decision applies
now: add the three `TWILIO_*` values first, remove `SEED_VERIFY_DEV_CODES` only
after. (2) **A contribution enters the graph only after approval** — already true
in code rather than a change to make: `shares_answerable` requires
`s.status = 'approved' AND sc.status = 'approved'`, so this closes open question 8
of docs/spec-compliance-review.md by confirming the build. (3) The contest
threshold stays undecided and is **not material for now**, so the app continues to
invent no rule.

| **The admin's interaction primitives are the platform's, not a library's** (13 Aug) | `components/admin/kit.tsx` — `Hint` · `Select` · `SegmentedFilter` · `Menu` · `Dialog`. It began as thin wrappers over **Radix**, which was the right instinct (no styles shipped, so Pando's tokens stay the design language — MUI or Chakra would have brought their own) and the wrong trade **here**: six packages compiled to a **102 KB chunk, 34 KB gzipped, on every admin page**, for five controls used by one or two people at a laptop. Measured before and after, not assumed. The mechanics now come from the platform, which has caught up with what the library was for: `<dialog showModal>` (focus trap, Escape, inert background, focus restored), the **Popover API** (top layer, light dismiss), a native `<select>` (typeahead and the OS wheel on a phone), and `role="radiogroup"` with a roving tabindex. `lucide-react` stays — per-icon tree-shaken, five icons. **The property being bought in both cases is the top layer**, and it is the reason a hand-rolled overlay is not an option: CLAUDE.md's 5 Aug bug is that `position: fixed` inside a *filled* animation is clipped to that element forever, and every admin card is inside animated content. **Two bugs this cost, both found in a browser and neither visible in review.** (1) A `popover` is `display: none` until `showPopover()`, so measuring it in a layout effect reads **0×0** — a menu opened from a card below the fold was positioned as though it had no height and landed at y=816 in an 812px viewport, entirely off-screen. Placement now runs *after* the show, and again on the next frame. (2) **`dialog.close()` fires no `close` event in one of the browsers this admin is walked in** — the element shut, React went on believing it was open, the scroll lock was never released and the note could not be reopened. So React closes it rather than reacting to it having closed: `cancel` and a keydown are both intercepted, and the `close` listener stays as belt and braces. |

## Invariants (breaking one is a product-level bug)

1. A caregiver appears in a user-facing answer **only** if `consent_status = consented`
   **and** `active = true` **and** `discoverable = true` **and** `is_adult`,
   enforced at the query level.

   **The two extra conditions are not padding.** This invariant was written with
   two, and `discoverable` is a separate rung of the ladder (mentioned → invited
   → consented → discoverable → introducible, 11 Aug) because *consent is not
   visibility*: a caregiver can agree to be listed and decline to appear in
   answers, and 2C makes that a real supported outcome. Measured against the demo
   cohort on 27 Aug: of ten caregivers, **three** pass `consented AND active` and
   only **two** also pass `discoverable` — so the invariant as written would have
   surfaced one caregiver who had said no to exactly this. `is_adult` is
   invariant 2 restated at the point of use. `introducible` is deliberately not
   part of it: being in an answer and being introduced are different amounts of
   exposure.
2. **No minors.** A nomination under 18 is discarded, not stored as pending.
3. Never present public information as human trust. Labels are **verbatim** from the
   spec and read the *source*, never who typed it.
4. A "vouched / validated by a parent" label requires `provenance = parent_submitted`
   **and** a real contributor behind it.
5. Contributor-protection numbers are enforced **in code, never by judgement**:
   monthly cap (**5 or 10, default 5, or `as_relevant` = no fixed cap** — the
   18 Aug reciprocity numbers; the spec's older 3/5/10/20 ladder is superseded and
   `allowance_shape` refuses anything else), a **48-hour gap** (see below —
   it has moved twice), response-rate governor at
   25%/30 days, and (v3.2 §10) at most one freshness ping per contributor per
   month, never on the same day as a blast.

   **The gap is 48 hours, and this is its second move.** Track the documents by
   date, because reading any one of them alone gives the wrong answer:

   - spec §14 and estimate row 8.2 (*titled* "48-hour gap") — **48 hours**;
   - *Pando Strategy — Current Direction* (8.18) §6 and §7 — **five days**,
     twice, with a rationale ("so 'five a month' describes a real ceiling rather
     than a burst"). Adopted 27 Aug under this file's rule that the newer client
     document wins;
   - **Seed Feedback, 1 Sep, item 18 — 48 hours, three times.** Once as an
     instruction ("Apply the 48-hour gap across all contribution requests. Pando
     should never send more than one request within 48 hours") and twice inside
     the participation page copy she wrote. Newest document, so it wins in turn,
     and `OUTREACH_GAP_DAYS` is 2.

   There is a second reason beyond recency, and it is what makes this
   unambiguous rather than a coin toss: **the allowance screen has been telling
   parents "with a 48-hour gap" since 18 Aug**, and her page copy repeats it.
   Enforcing five days behind copy promising two was not a broken promise — a
   parent received fewer messages, not more — but the number a parent agreed to
   and the number the code kept were different, and only one of them was written
   where she could read it. One named constant, so it is changed in one place;
   `npm run test:outreach` and `test:compliance` both pin it, so a session
   reading only the 8.18 strategy cannot halve the request rate again.

   **Phase 2**: nothing sends yet, so
   nothing enforces them yet. What exists today is where they go —
   `lib/server/sms.ts` runs opt-out → quiet hours → *frequency* → provider, and
   `monthly_contact_allowance` is captured from the parent (P14) and stored. The
   invariant is that the first code that sends a blast implements the middle step;
   it is listed here so that code cannot be written without meeting it.
6. All outbound SMS goes through one compliant send layer (`lib/server/sms.ts`):
   opt-out → quiet hours 8:00–21:00 PT → frequency → throttle → provider, in that
   order. No raw Twilio calls anywhere, and always via the Messaging
   Service SID.
7. Never log phone numbers, names or free text. Counts and enums only.
8. Free text about a named person is never published verbatim without human review.
9. "Other" answers are not matchable until an admin promotes them into
   `market_options`.
10. One person, one identity, keyed by phone. "Contributor" is a derived status, not
    a second table.
11. **Nothing about a named parent is stored before their phone is verified.** The
    device holds the profile and the cards; the write routes refuse them without a
    confirmed code. `phone_verified` is a server fact read from the verification,
    never a field the client can set.
12. A caregiver's private note — and the reason behind a hesitant "would you hire
    them again" — never leaves the admin surface, in any form.
13. **No contact details for a nominated caregiver are ever collected or stored.**
    The only path in is the invite the nominating parent sends themselves.
14. A caregiver nomination is firsthand-only: the family must have employed them.
    A secondhand one is refused, not stored as a weaker record.

## Where the logic lives

`web/lib/questions.ts` — the profile questionnaire (order, gating, weights, P3–P14).
`web/lib/demand.ts` — D1 routing: what Pando says back to which kind of question.
`web/lib/seed-gate.ts` — link-only access: the marker cookie, and which screens
                       need it. `proxy.ts` issues it on `/join` and enforces it
                       on the rest. Not authentication — see Decisions.
`web/lib/caregiver-invite.ts` — the message the parent sends themselves (C11).
`web/lib/server/repo/referral.ts` — a parent's own link, and who referred whom.
                       A `personal` `invites` row rather than a table of its
                       own, so it inherits attribution, the cache and the funnel.
`web/components/seed/ReferralInvite.tsx` — that link on screen: the popup over
                       `/share`, the panel on `/done`, one icon copy button.
`web/components/seed/SignIn.tsx` · `web/app/api/seed/me/route.ts` — coming back
                       with a number and a code. The phone is read from the
                       verification the server recorded, never from the request.
`web/lib/caregiver-flow.ts` — 2C: the caregiver's own questions (G1–G10), as data.
`web/lib/caregiver-options.ts` — the option lists **both** caregiver surfaces share.
`web/lib/server/repo/caregiver.ts` — 2C: writing a claim, in one transaction,
                       and `deleteCaregiverClaim` — the **one** copy of the
                       cascade both the admin button and DELETE run.
`web/components/admin/Standing.tsx` — M14.4. Counters, response rate, tier and
                       limits. A third view on the contributors page, and the
                       first thing that ever read `lib/tiers.ts`.
`web/app/(admin)/admin/impact/page.tsx` — M14.6. 9.1 joined to 9.2, so "helped
                       but never thanked" is visible at all.
`web/app/(admin)/admin/freshness/page.tsx` — M14.9. Retire it, or keep it
                       marked old. The two write actions the flag never had.
`web/lib/rate-limits.ts` — M15.4. How often one caller may hit each public
                       endpoint, and **which `X-Forwarded-For` entry is
                       actually theirs**. Pure; imports nothing.
`web/lib/server/rate-limit.ts` — the counters, and the one line a route adds.
                       In-process, which is honest about needing to move the day
                       there are two containers.
`web/lib/sms-segments.ts` — M13.3. What a message actually costs to send, and
                       where it breaks. One character outside GSM-7 halves the
                       budget; this measures and never rewrites registered copy.
`web/lib/payments.ts` — M13.5–13.7. Whether a refund is coherent and whether the
                       guarantee is owed. Imports **nothing** — `paymentFor`
                       lives in `blast-tiers.ts` beside the prices, for that
                       reason (see Decisions).
`web/lib/stripe-signature.ts` — M13.6. Verifying a payment webhook, including
                       the replay tolerance the Twilio verifier has no
                       equivalent of.
`web/lib/server/stripe.ts` — the **only** place Pando talks to Stripe: a
                       checkout session and a refund, over `fetch`, inert until
                       `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is set.
`web/lib/server/repo/payments.ts` — the writes behind a paid Ask. Idempotent by
                       `where` clause, because Stripe is not.
`web/lib/server/repo/retry.ts` — M13.4. Which failed messages are worth sending
                       again, and rebuilding the three that can be.
`web/app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts` — the only evidence of payment this app
                       accepts. A success page cannot activate a blast.
`web/app/(admin)/admin/blasts/page.tsx` — M14.3. The Ask, its pool before it
                       goes out, and the two verbs nobody could reach: mark it
                       answered, flag a refund. Cannot send.
`web/app/(admin)/admin/payments/page.tsx` — M14.5. What was paid and what is
                       owed. Leads with whether Stripe is switched on at all.
`web/lib/pending-question.ts` — what Pando says when it cannot read a
                       message, how much of the exchange it remembers, and when
                       to stop asking and fetch a person. Pure.
`web/lib/server/repo/pending-question.ts` — the turns themselves
                       (`drizzle/0035`), the only place SMS conversation text
                       is kept.
`web/lib/server/web-search.ts` — what is generally known about a question,
                       from the open web. Public labels only, never a person,
                       never for a question about care, off by default.
`web/lib/public-info.ts` — reading what came back. Pure, so a node test can
                       load it; every branch drops rather than repairs.
                       `npm run probe:web-search` is the live matrix behind it
                       — ten questions across contexts, through the real pipeline.
`web/lib/named-person.ts` — M11.4. Is this record's *name* a person? Pure, two
                       thresholds, and measured against all 588 real records.
`web/lib/server/repo/caregiver-delete.ts` — M11.3. Finding a caregiver by the
                       number that texted DELETE, and reporting which of three
                       true things to say back.
`web/lib/seed-chat/scripts.ts` — the capture conversations.
`web/lib/trust-labels.ts` — M5.6. The approved wording, verbatim, plus the
                       freshness ladder and the guard that public information
                       never wears a parent label. Pure.
`web/lib/server/repo/retrieval.ts` — M5.5. What could answer a question, with
                       invariant 1's four conditions in the WHERE clause.
`web/lib/server/inbound.ts` — what Pando does with an inbound message, whatever
                       carried it. Extracted from `/api/sms/inbound` when the
                       Slack relay gave it a second door; the transports keep
                       only the signature check and "which number is this".
`web/lib/slack-text.ts` — what Slack's event `text` actually contains, undone:
                       tel links, labels, entities, and the address prefix. Pure,
                       imports nothing, and tested — the regex used to live in the
                       route with a **copy** of itself in the suite.
`web/lib/server/slack.ts` · `web/lib/slack-signature.ts` — the **temporary**
                       test transport standing in for Twilio, and its signature.
                       One channel, threads as addresses, verification excluded.
`web/lib/server/repo/relay.ts` — who a relay message is for and which thread it
                       belongs in. Resolves by phone when there is no person id,
                       because that is how SMS addresses everything.
`web/lib/matching.ts` — M6. The two-layer scorer, and the **only** copy of the age
                       ladder. Pure, and deliberately free of *runtime* imports so
                       `npm run test:matching` can load it in plain node.
`web/lib/server/repo/matching.ts` — the one query that feeds it. Weights from the
                       table every call; the pool bounded by the asker's edges.
`web/app/(admin)/admin/matching/page.tsx` — 6.7. The harness: who Pando would
                       ask, why, and at what weights — the coefficients are
                       editable here since 2 Sep (`matching.weight`, audited).
                       Still cannot send: config, never outreach. Each row is
                       the arithmetic behind its own score.
`web/lib/admin/url-state.ts` — the filter a page is on, in the address bar. What
                       makes a worklist row able to point at a *tab*.
`web/components/admin/QuickFind.tsx` — `/` from anywhere: find a parent and open
                       their page. Ranks with `lib/admin/person-search.ts`.
`web/components/admin/PersonPicker.tsx` — finding one parent among hundreds. A
                       real combobox, because a search box on top of a `<select>`
                       is two controls doing one job.
`web/lib/admin/person-search.ts` — what counts as a match and what ranks above
                       what. Pure, because a search rule fails silently
                       (`npm run test:person-search`).
`web/components/admin/ui.tsx` — the admin's *look*: `ResultNote`/`ErrorNote` (the
                       one way a page reports what an action did — nine
                       hand-written copies before, and none of them announced),
                       `Badge` (with `hint`, the reachable replacement for a
                       `title`), `Toolbar`, `Explainer` (closed since 3 Sep — hover peeks, a click pins), and `controlClass` vs
                       `inputClass` — one look, with and without a width.
`web/components/admin/kit.tsx` — the admin's *mechanics*, on platform primitives
                       rather than a component library: `Hint` · `Select` ·
                       `SegmentedFilter` · `Menu` · `Dialog`. See Decisions for
                       what that swap cost and what it bought.
`web/components/admin/Record.tsx` — the admin's second layout, for the queues a
                       table cannot hold: `RecordCard` · `FactGrid`/`Fact` ·
                       `SpecList`/`Spec` · `Quote` · `RecordGroup` ·
                       `RecordDrawer`. Which one a page uses is a property of
                       its data, never a preference — see Decisions.
`web/components/ui/OptionPicker.tsx` — the searchable dropdown the circles
                       questions use. Which control a question gets is a
                       property of its options: a directory here, a short closed
                       set in `ChipGroup`.
`web/components/ui/TextAction.tsx` — the quiet 44px action beside the loud one,
                       and `InlineAction` for a link inside a sentence. One box
                       for `<button>` and `<a>` alike, which is the whole point.
`web/components/ui/Panel.tsx` — the block on a flow screen. `tone` is what it
                       means, `raised` is the one card the screen is about.
`web/components/ui/Screen.tsx` — the app shell (phone + desktop).
`web/components/site/Shell.tsx` — the public-site shell.

Adding a question should touch one data file and nothing else.

## Keeping this file current

Do this **in the same turn** as the change, not later:

1. **New functionality** → update the status table, and add a row to *Decisions* if a
   choice was made that a future session could unknowingly undo.
2. **A client answer or a new document** → reconcile
   [docs/spec-compliance-review.md](docs/spec-compliance-review.md) (matches /
   deviations / open questions) and note the date.
3. **A new invariant or a new safety rule** → add it to Invariants, and to the
   relevant `.claude/skills/*` if it changes how UI gets built.
4. **A new route, hook or payload** → `web/README.md` (routes table + payload shape)
   and `.env.example`.
5. **Copy changed by the client** in the root `*.html` → port it into `app/(site)/*`
   and record the terminology in Decisions.

Rule of thumb: if the next session would be surprised by it, it belongs here.
